# The scripts catalogue

Entries for the scripts in `$S` (`<base directory>/scripts`), moved out of SKILL.md. `$S` is defined there.

- `$S/supervise.sh <name> <cmd> [args...]` — **the leaf launcher.** Spawns `<cmd>` under a
  heartbeat + hard-timeout watchdog, owns the `.tdd/<name>.log` progress log, and returns
  the captured output on stdout. **Exit 0 = success, 1 = leaf-failure, 2 = killed-stuck**
  (hung past the heartbeat window or over the hard timeout, **after** its stuck-retry
  budget). A one-line status goes to stderr. Tunables: `VERI_HEARTBEAT` (silence window s,
  default 180), `VERI_TIMEOUT` (hard cap s, default 900), `VERI_STUCK_RETRIES` (extra
  attempts after a killed-stuck, default 1 — the **liveness** axis, independent of both the
  conductor's verdict-axis `REVISE_BUDGET` and its cost-axis `VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET`).
  Six more choose the **model** each leaf runs on, resolved here through
  `$S/leaf-model.sh` and passed to `claude --model`: `VERI_MODEL` (every leaf),
  `VERI_MODEL_DEVELOPER` (developer leaves only — `develop-*`, `revise-*`),
  `VERI_MODEL_REVISE` (only the developer leaves that redo rejected work — `revise-*` and
  `develop-exhausted` — beating `VERI_MODEL_DEVELOPER` for them),
  `VERI_MODEL_REVISE_GREEN` (`revise-green` leaves only, beating `VERI_MODEL_REVISE`,
  `VERI_MODEL_DEVELOPER` and `VERI_MODEL` for them and changing no other leaf; unset or blank,
  every leaf resolves as before),
  `VERI_MODEL_VERIFIER` (verifier leaves only — `verify-*`, `verify-pin` included) and
  `VERI_MODEL_ADJUDICATOR` (the `adjudicate-*` leaf only). A role
  variable beats `VERI_MODEL`; all six unset means the shipped defaults: sonnet for first-try
  developer leaves and verifiers, opus for the redo leaves, the adjudicator and the conductor. The value goes through verbatim, so an alias (`sonnet`, `opus[1m]`) or a full id
  (`claude-sonnet-5`) both work.
  Because leaves **stream** (below), the default
  `VERI_HEARTBEAT` is a real silence window — a working leaf keeps it fresh, a genuinely
  hung one trips it — so just use the defaults; no need to pin heartbeat to the timeout.
  **Every leaf is launched through this — never call `run-leaf.sh` or `claude` directly.**
  It records its own final code in the completion file `.tdd/<name>.exit` on **every** path,
  killed-stuck included — that is what makes a detached launch waitable. And because a
  killed-stuck leaf is relaunched (`VERI_STUCK_RETRIES`), one call can take **more than one**
  `VERI_TIMEOUT` of wall time, which is why the wait below loops rather than naming one long
  timeout. When a `develop-*` or `revise-*` leaf exits, any change it made to a committed
  `.tdd/issue-*-mutations.md` record (an edit, a deletion, a new file) is undone and one
  `mutation-record-restored` event (`name=`, `file=`) goes to `events.jsonl` per record —
  only `mutation-probe` writes that record, except a record the leaf's step names with
  `mutation-record: .tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md` in its step text (`VERI_STEP`), which is left as the leaf made it; the marker inside backticks, double quotes or single quotes (an apostrophe within a word is not a quote) is ignored, so a step that only quotes it names no record. When a `develop-red…` or `revise-red-…` leaf exits,
  any tracked production file it changed or deleted from the step's uncommitted state is written back and
  one `production-file-restored` event (`name=`, `file=`) goes to `events.jsonl` per file.
  A refused launch exits 2, writes one `launch-refused` event (`name=`, `reason=`) naming the leaf
  and starts no leaf. Causes: a `run-leaf.sh` launch whose prompt file is missing or empty, a
  `run-leaf.sh` launch with no prompt argument, `VERI_STEP` set but empty, an empty `--step`
  value, and a pending `.tdd/stop` of `at-leaf` or `now`. Run from a `veri-snapshot-*` directory, it also refuses a launch of run
  `VERI_ISSUE` whose snapshot no longer matches the digest the run recorded at extraction in `.tdd/issue-<n>-snapshot-digest`
  (`the skill snapshot <entry> changed since extraction`, the first differing entry, the `.veri-snapshot` marker included), or that has no digest recorded.
  A watched leaf's supervisor records itself in `.tdd/leaf.owner`: `<pid> <name>` on line 1 and
  its start time, as `ps -o lstart=` prints it under `LC_ALL=C` and `TZ=UTC` (the same pinned
  environment the comparison reads it under), on line 2. A later launch in that worktree exits
  2, with no event, refusing `leaf <o> (pid <p>) is still alive in this worktree`, only while that
  pid is alive under that start time, or alive with no line 2 recorded (an older snapshot's file)
  or a start time `ps` cannot read; a pid alive under another start time (a dead supervisor's pid
  reused) refuses nothing, and neither does a dead pid or an owner whose `.tdd/<o>.exit` exists.
- `$S/detach-leaf.sh <cmd> [args...]` — **the detaching half of a detached launch.** Runs
  `<cmd>` as the leader of a **new session**, and so of a new process group, then returns at
  once. Every leaf launch below goes through it, and none uses `nohup` — *Launching a leaf*
  says why. Redirections go on the call as usual — the launched process inherits them.
- `$S/wait-leaf.sh <name> [max-seconds]` — **the foreground half of a detached launch.**
  Blocks polling for that completion file and exits with the code recorded in it as soon as
  it lands (0 / 1 / 2, the launcher's own contract), or **exit 3** — nothing to report yet —
  once `max-seconds` pass with no completion file. The default is 540 s, comfortably inside
  Claude Code's 10-minute cap on a single foreground Bash call. On a **3, call it again**:
  the leaf is still running. A 3 with no leaf running means no result was ever recorded —
  never success. A **2** also means the supervisor died without recording a status: no
  completion file, and the pid in `.tdd/<name>.pid` no longer runs a supervisor for that
  name. It takes the killed-stuck branch like any other 2.
- `$S/run-leaf.sh <prompt-file|-> [claude args...]` — the inner leaf invocation. Runs
  `claude -p --output-format stream-json` so the leaf **streams events as it works** (that
  is the supervisor's heartbeat), then appends the clean final result. You always run it
  *via* `supervise.sh`, not on its own. Tunable: `VERI_LEAF_TOOLS` — unset passes
  `--tools Bash,Read,Edit,Write,Grep,Glob` (`DEFAULT_TOOLS`) to `claude`; set non-empty passes
  `--tools <value>`; set empty passes `--tools ""` (`grade-prose.sh` sets it empty, so its grader
  has no tools). A `--tools` in the caller's own arguments wins over all three.
  Every `--settings` value in the caller's arguments that is a JSON object is merged into the
  one `--settings` flag it passes `claude`; a value that is not a JSON object (a file path, say)
  is refused with exit **2**, naming it, before any launch.
  It always sets `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1` in `claude`'s environment, and sets
  `BASH_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` and `BASH_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS` to the leaf's effective timeout, the one
  `supervise.sh` enforces (`VERI_TIMEOUT`, else the run's `.tdd/issue-<n>-timeout`, else 900 s),
  unless the caller already set them.
  Besides the names it sets itself, the leaf's environment holds only allow-listed names
  (`LEAF_ENV_NAMES` in `run_leaf.py`): `PATH`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `PWD`, `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `LANG`,
  every `LC_*`, every `VERI_*`, `CLAUDE_BIN`, `REVISE_BUDGET`, `VERIFY_LEAF_ARGS`,
  `PYTHONCOERCECLOCALE`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`,
  `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `HTTP_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY`, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`,
  `PYTEST_LEG_LOCK_WAIT_MARKER` (which `supervise.sh` sets for the leaf) and `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` (the
  reconcile leaf pushes, and over an ssh remote its key is held by an agent; it names a socket, not
  a secret); any other name, `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` and cloud keys included,
  is dropped. Tunable: `VERI_LEAF_ENV` (default empty) — space-separated names added to the
  allow-list, for a test command that needs one, or a push that needs one of the dropped names,
  which would show as the reconcile leaf's push failing. An item of `VERI_LEAF_ENV` that is not a
  variable name (`A=B`, a glob) adds nothing, and `run-leaf.sh` names it on one stderr line. A name it adds that
  `VERI_REDACT_VARS` also names passes, and `run-leaf.sh` prints one stderr line naming it, never
  its value.
- `$S/extract-result.sh` — pulls the final result text out of a leaf's stream-json (used
  by `run-leaf.sh`; passes plain text through unchanged).
- `$S/build-develop-prompt.sh <red|green|refactor|cover|transform|diff|structural|predicate|revise|exhausted|reconcile|over-budget|rebase|replaces-report|red-contradicted|red-repair|red-missing|red-supersede|expand|beside|cover-new|dual-stub|migrate|contract-a|contract-b> …` — assemble a **developer** leaf's prompt from a
  versioned template: `red <spec-file> <step-text> <test-cmd>`, `green <spec-file>
  <step-text> <test-cmd> <test-path> <target> <fast-cmd> [<owed-cases>]` (the optional 8th argument
  is the named cases still owed for deletion), `refactor <spec-file> <step-text>
  <test-cmd> <touched-files> [<pairs-file>]` (the optional 6th argument is what `near-duplicates.sh` printed: its
  lines fill the prompt's near-duplicates section, which reads `none` when the argument is absent or the file empty;
  an unreadable pairs file exits 2 with no stdout), `revise <phase> <spec-file> <step-text> <test-cmd>
  <reason-file>` (reads `VERI_ISSUE`, the run's issue number, for the `check-earlier` command in the prompt; **exit 2** with one stderr line when it is not a number), `red-repair <case> <report-file> <superseded>…` (the Red leaf that repairs one case the step's own Red wrote, edited in place; never creates a suite), `$S/build-develop-prompt.sh red-missing <spec-file> <step-text> <test-cmd> <reason-file>` (the Red leaf that adds the one case pinning the `MISSING_TEST:` behaviour the reason file names, editing no other case). `revise red` whose reason file is named `verify-pin.reason` and whose `REASON:` line has a before-state-green clause (a clause naming the before-state and green or pass, with no `fail`, `red`, `not`, `n't` or `after` in it) builds no prompt and exits 2 with a stderr line naming `pin-reject-route.sh`; a surviving-mutant reason (`stays green`, the target red on the before-state) builds the prompt, never refused. Prints the prompt on stdout; **exit 2** with a diagnostic and **no
  stdout** on an unknown phase or a missing template, spec or reason file — never a partial
  prompt. The mirror of `build-verify-prompt.sh` and its opposite in emphasis: the verifier
  builder is defined by what it **cannot** carry (no rationale parameter, so blindness is
  structural), this one by what it **must** carry — the same instructions on every run of a
  phase, with only the variables changing. **Build every developer prompt with it**; a
  phase's instructions live in its template, not in prose you improvise per cycle.
- `$S/build-verify-prompt.sh [--step '<STEP>'] [--fast-cmd '<CMD>'] [--supersede-base <sha>] [--branch-base <sha>] [--step-green <sha>] <red|green|refactor|pin|plan|rebase|transform|diff> <spec-file> <test-path> [target]` —
  assemble the blind verifier prompt. `green`, `refactor`, `break`, `diff`, `transform` and every interface phase refuse to build without `--fast-cmd` (**exit 2**). `refactor` refuses to build without `--step-green <sha>` (hex, 7-40 chars), the step's green tick, whose files bound the refactor, and every other phase refuses one (**exit 2**). `--supersede-base <sha>` (hex, 7-40 chars; `diff` phase) points the verifier at `git diff <sha>` for a supersede edit. The `plan` phase (`plan <spec-file> <ledger>`) adds, after the ledger, a `### Test cases touching the files the plan names` section listing the names, never the bodies, of the committed test cases touching the files the plan's steps name, computed from the ledger and the files git tracks under the current directory (`git ls-files`; a failing one is **exit 2**), none under `.git`, `.tdd`, `.venv` or `node_modules` and no symbolic link: a step names a file when a word of its text is a path or a basename of a tracked file, and a test file touches it when its text holds that file's stem as a whole word, `-` and `_` counted alike. Files touching a path a step gives in full come first, then those with more hits, then alphabetically. A touching test file whose cases cannot be read (unreadable, a call that never closes, a language the reader does not read) gets one `<file>: cases not readable` line, and the section then never says that no case touches a named file. The list is capped at `VERI_PLAN_TOUCHING_CASES` entries, a case name or such a line each (default `100`; past it, one `<file>: <m> more cases` line per test file left over, and one count of the unreadable ones; a value that is not a whole number is **exit 2**). The run lines (`{{AFFECTED_RUN_LINE}}`, `{{RUN_LINE}}`) are read from the `veri: one test file:`, `veri: fast tests:`, `veri: all tests:` and `veri: quiet test files:` lines of `CLAUDE.md` as it stood at the branch base (`--branch-base <sha>`, which `verify.sh` passes), never from the working tree, and whether the repository is veri's own tree (`skills/tdd/scripts/tests/py` a directory, so `make test TESTS="…"`) is decided at that commit too; a branch base that names no commit git can resolve is **exit 2**. Read at that commit the same way: a `⟦lint-repair⟧` `diff` step's `veri: lint:` line, and `{{FULL_CMD}}`, the full test command the `rebase` verifier and a `⟦suite-repair⟧` `green` verifier are given (its `veri: all tests:` line, `the test command` when it declares none). Used by `verify.sh`; you rarely call it directly.
- `$S/verify.sh [--step '<STEP>'] [--fast-cmd '<CMD>'] [--supersede-base <sha>] [--step-green <sha>] --branch-base <sha> <red|green|refactor|pin|plan> <spec-file> <test-path> [target] | --branch-base <sha> diff <spec-file> <step-base-sha> | --branch-base <sha> rebase <spec-file> <onto-sha> <pre-rebase-sha>` — the **verify
  verb**: build the blind prompt → launch the verifier leaf **through the supervisor** →
  parse the verdict. Prints the verifier's **reason** on stdout; **exit 0 = PASS, 1 =
  REJECT, 2 = malformed or killed-stuck**. The `pin` phase is the *already satisfied*
  check: the verifier confirms the test passes now, **fails** under a behaviour-removing
  mutation it applies and then reverts itself, and is behaviour-level — the same trivial
  / over-coupled / too-complex bar as `red`.
  Every phase, `diff` included, takes `--branch-base <sha>`, the run's branch base the conductor recorded
  (`.tdd/issue-<n>-branch-base`), and exits 2 naming the flag when it is missing or not `^[0-9a-f]{7,40}$`: its
  verifier's run lines are read from `CLAUDE.md` as it stood at that sha, from the lines it declares alone, never
  from the working tree, and a `pin` or `surround` verifier takes its before-state there. No phase reads a
  remote-tracking ref for its base: a leaf can rewrite one.
  `refactor` requires `--step-green <sha>`, the step's green tick, and every other phase refuses it (exit 2, from
  `build-verify-prompt.sh`, before any leaf launches) (#4544).
  A `diff` call with a third positional after `<spec-file>` exits 2 naming it.
  The `rebase` phase, which judges a `develop-rebase` or `revise-rebase` leaf's resolution, runs two gates
  before it launches any leaf, in this order. A tracked file with an uncommitted change (`git status
  --porcelain --untracked-files=no`; an untracked file does not count), or a `git status` that fails, is **exit
  2** naming the dirty paths, or the failed `git status`, on stderr: the verifier would judge the working tree while the push ships HEAD. A
  commit of `<onto-sha>..HEAD` that adds a conflict marker line (`<<<<<<< ` or `>>>>>>> `) is **exit 1**,
  naming each such commit and file on stdout. Outside a git repository neither gate runs.
  A verifier leaf is launched with the developer's `--tools` list and no `--disallowedTools`; its
  `--settings` carry a `PreToolUse` hook matching `Write|Edit|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit` that blocks
  such a call (exit 2, saying the verifier may not modify files). A verifier that changes the tree
  another way (through `Bash`) is still restored, relaunched and then refused (exit **2**). The
  hook's limits: it bans only those four write tools, a write through `Bash` is caught by the
  tree-change guard inside the repository only, and a hook that outlives the CLI's hook timeout
  does not block.
  `VERI_EFFORT_VERIFIER` sets the verifier's effort: set to `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` or
  `max`, every verifier leaf is launched with `--effort <value>`; unset or empty, with no
  `--effort`; any other value stops preflight (exit 2), and `verify.sh` itself exits **2** on one,
  naming the knob and the five levels and launching no leaf, since the CLI ignores an unknown
  effort with only a warning. With it set, a `VERIFY_LEAF_ARGS` that carries its own `--effort` makes
  `verify.sh` exit **2** with a stderr line naming both.
- `$S/refactor-owed.sh append|applied|not-done|clear|render|list <file> …` — the structural leftovers a step's refactor leaf reports. `append <file> '<STEP>' [<bounds>]` reads the report on stdin; `applied <file> '<transform>' <sha> '<subject>'` records a candidate as applied, `<subject>` being the applying commit's subject, `"$(git log -1 --format=%s)"`, which `render` prints beside the sha so the row stays readable after a rebase rewrites it; `not-done <file> '<candidate>' <reason>` records a candidate the pass did not apply, which `render` shows under `### Not done`, a `coupling` row replacing any earlier `coupling` row of the file; `clear <file> <reason>` drops that row, `coupling` the one reason it takes (exit **2** for any other); `render <file>` prints the `## Refactor owed` section; `list <file>` prints each structural transform once. Exit **0** done, **1** a write failed, **2** a missing argument or an unknown verb, **3** `render` or `list` found nothing.
- `$S/red-setup.sh record <n> < <red-report> | worst <n> <owed-file>` — the stubs and setup a verified Red's case needed, and the run's worst step. `record` reads the report's `SETUP: <count> | <what>` line and writes `event.sh red-setup count=<count> what=<what>` for the current step of `.tdd/issue-<n>.md`: exit **0** written, **3** no `SETUP:` line (nothing written), **2** a malformed line or no current step (nothing written, one stderr line). `worst` reads the run's `red-setup` events, the latest per step, and when the highest count exceeds `VERI_SETUP_THRESHOLD` (a whole number, default 3) records one `not-done` row `'<step>: <count> stubs and setup (<what>)'` with reason `testability` for that step (the earliest on a tie), local paths, host and account name redacted, replacing its own earlier row rather than adding one: exit **0** recorded, **3** none over the threshold (an earlier row dropped), **2** a threshold that is not a whole number or an owed file it cannot write. No verifier reads the count: a signal, never a gate.
- `$S/refactor-candidates.sh <issue> <owed-file>` — files the one issue listing what a run's structural pass left. It reads `<owed-file>` (`.tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md`) and lists every `out-of-bounds` and `not-done` row under a heading per module; the issue is titled `Refactor candidates left by #<issue>` and labelled exactly `refactor` and `refactor-owed`, never the opt-in label. A rerun finds the open issue by that exact title and replaces its body instead of filing another. Prints the issue's url on stdout. Exit **0** the url printed, **2** a bad argument (a non-numeric `<issue>`, or not two arguments) or a GitHub failure, **3** nothing out-of-bounds or not done (a missing or empty `<owed-file>`), and no issue filed.
- `$S/test-quality.sh files <branch-base> | anchor <ledger> | record <n> < <leaf-report>` — the test-quality check (*4. Draft PR*, sub-step 1). `anchor` prints the last done step of `<ledger>` carrying exactly one `⟦criterion k⟧` tag, the step `add-ledger-step.sh … after` appends the fix steps to: **exit 0** printed, **exit 3** none, or a step of the ledger carries two tags (which `add-ledger-step.sh` refuses), **exit 2** the ledger unreadable. `files` prints, one per line, the test files `git diff --name-only <branch-base>..HEAD` names that `case_reader.is_test_path` accepts and that still exist: **exit 0** printed, **exit 3** none, **exit 2** git could not answer or bad arguments. `record` writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-test-quality` from the `develop-test-quality` leaf's report on stdin, one line `<kind> | <file>::<case> | <why>` per `FINDING:` line, in report order: a `FINDING:` line naming no `<file>::<case>` with a kind and a why, a kind other than `weak-assertion`, `redundant`, `implementation-coupled` and `fixed-wait`, or a case its file does not define (a helper function, say) is refused: skipped, named on stderr, and the other findings still recorded (#4534). A `<file>` that is not a file from the worktree root, a package-relative one such as `spend_test.go`, is read as the one line of `.tdd/test-quality-files` (the `files` verb's list, the files the leaf was shown) that is it or ends in `/<file>`, and recorded under that repo-relative path; matching no line, or more than one, it is refused (#4624). **Exit 0** findings recorded; **exit 3** `FINDING: none`, the file written with no finding; **exit 2** nothing written, an earlier record left as it was: bad arguments, no `FINDING:` line, or every finding refused.
- `$S/coupling.sh <branch-base>` — the Draft PR step's report of mutual references: prints `mutual: <a> <b>` (paths in byte order), one line per pair, for each pair of distinct files git does not ignore, both non-test files (`case_reader.is_test_path`) of the same extension and neither `.md`, `.txt` nor `.rst`, at least one changed in `<branch-base>..HEAD`, each referring to the other: an import statement of its module (`import <stem>`, `from <stem> import`, `from <package> import <stem>`), or its script run by path, its name ending in `.sh`, `.py` or the pair's own extension (a whole string literal that is a path holding a `/`, the bare quoted name as an argument after `(`, `[` or `,`, or an unquoted path after a `/` outside any quote on its line, as a shell command has it), or extensionless after a `/` closing a quoted path; the name inside a longer string, such as a message `"<stem>.sh: …"`, or after `=` or an operator, such as a `who=` label, runs nothing, a stem being the basename without its last extension, `-` and `_` alike; a docstring, comment, identifier or bare string holding the other's stem is no reference (#4556), the walk skipping `.git`, `.tdd`, `.venv` and `node_modules`. Exit **0** one or more pairs printed, **3** no pair, **2** a bad argument or a base that does not resolve, with one stderr line.
- `$S/unwired.sh [--accepted <record>] <branch-base>` — the Draft PR step's gate before readying: prints, one per line, each path `git diff --diff-filter=A --name-only <branch-base>..HEAD` names that is code — not a test file (`case_reader.is_test_path`), not under `.tdd/` or `.github/`, and not of a prose, config or data extension (`.md`, `.txt`, `.rst`, `.yml`, `.yaml`, `.json`, `.toml`, `.ini`, `.cfg`, `.lock`, `.csv`) — whose stem (basename without its last extension) no other non-test file git does not ignore holds as a whole word, `-` and `_` alike, the walk run from the repository root and skipping `.git`, `.tdd`, `.venv` and `node_modules`; the stem inside the file itself is no caller. With `--accepted`, a path `<record>` lists (one per line; a missing record lists none) is not printed. Exit **1** one or more printed, **0** every such file is named or accepted, **3** the range adds none, **2** a bad argument, a base that does not resolve, or a directory that is not a git work tree, with one stderr line.
- `$S/mutation-parity.sh <pairs-table>` — proves each row of a `⟦replaces-case⟧` pairs table by applying its mutation: **exit 0** all proven, **exit 1** a REJECT, **exit 2** a bail. A row whose mutation no kept case catches prints `MISSING_TEST: <old>: no kept case fails when its mutation is applied` on stderr; saved to a reason file, that line routes through `wrong-test-route.sh` as `missing`.
- `$S/run-verdict.sh [--step "<STEP>"] [--fast-cmd '<cmd>'] [--supersede-base <sha>] [--step-green <sha>] --branch-base <sha> <red|green|refactor|pin|plan> <spec-file> <test-path> [target]`
  — the **verdict launcher**: `verify.sh`'s own call, argument for argument, reached as a
  command rather than as a shell string. This is what `supervise.sh verdict-<phase>` runs;
  it passes every argument, `--supersede-base <sha>`, `--branch-base <sha>` and `--step-green <sha>` included, through untouched and `exec`s the verify verb, so the step title
  travels in argv and its exit code is `verify.sh`'s: `refactor` without `--step-green` exits 2.
- `$S/parse-verdict.sh` — extracts `PASS|REJECT` + reason from a leaf's output (used by
  `verify.sh`).
- `$S/tick-issue-criterion.sh <n> "<substring>"` — tick a criterion checkbox in the
  issue body (the human-facing mirror).
- `$S/next-unchecked.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md` — print the **first unchecked ledger step**'s
  description; **exit 3** when none remain (the loop-done / nothing-to-resume signal).
- `VERI_STEP=<step> $S/wrong-test-route.sh --spec <spec-file> <green-report> <red-test-id>` — routes a green report's `WRONG_TEST:`, or a report carrying `MISSING_TEST:` (a verify-green REJECT reason, a mutation-parity stderr saved to a file, a revise-green report answering `IMPOSSIBLE`): **exit 0** prints `fixture <path> <name>` (a helper, fixture or shared setup function defined in the test file `<path>` that is not a test case), `missing <behaviour>` (a behaviour no test pins; the report file is the `red-missing` brief), `later <step>` (the behaviour is an open ledger step after `VERI_STEP`: one a `LATER_STEP:` line names, or, with no such line, one whose criterion text (the Nth box, checked or not, under `## Acceptance criteria` in `--spec`, N from the step's `⟦criterion N⟧` marker; a step with no usable marker never matches) contains the `MISSING_TEST:` behaviour or is contained in it, compared case-insensitively), `red <case>` (an existing case other than the step's own that a `CONTRADICTS:` line names; a case named only in the `WRONG_TEST:` text is evidence, so a report that cites one routes to `repair`; a report is exempt from the `SUITE:` line as a `green-wrong-test` (`WRONG_TEST:` with `SUPERSEDED:`) or a `green-wrong-test-contradicts` (`WRONG_TEST:` with `CONTRADICTS:`) format, and a revise of green as `revise-wrong-test` or `revise-wrong-test-contradicts`), `repair <case> <superseded>…` (a case the step's own Red wrote, per its red record or as `<red-test-id>`, with the older cases listed on `SUPERSEDED:` lines) or `supersede <case> <case>…` (two or more older cases named on `SUPERSEDED:` lines, which one `red-supersede` Red updates together), **exit 3** prints `revise`, **exit 2** bad arguments (a missing `--spec` included), or an unreadable ledger behind a `LATER_STEP:` line, or a bare case name (one with no `::`) that is defined in more than one test file or in none. The `red`, `repair` and `supersede` cases it prints carry the test file that defines them, as `<file>::<case>`. `missing <behaviour>` is answered on exit 0 until the step's `"kind": "missing"` `reroute` events in `.tdd/events.jsonl` (with `VERI_STEP`) reach `VERI_MISSING_REROUTE_BUDGET` (default 3); then `missing-exhausted <behaviour>` is printed on **exit 1**. The other four routes (`red`, `repair`, `supersede`, `fixture`) stay free only for the step's first reroute of any kind: a later reroute that would take one of them (a `WRONG_TEST:` or `SUPERSEDED:` report) answers `revise` (exit 3) and spends one `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt, except that it answers `prep <file::case> …` on exit 0 when a `reroute` event is already recorded for the step and the report's `SUPERSEDED:` or `CONTRADICTS:` lines name one or more resolvable cases other than the step's own Red case (where the script used to answer `revise`). A `fixture` route is taken only when the helper is defined in the step's own test file, the one before `::` in a `<file>::<case>` id; an id with no `::` (the Go and JS names) falls back to searching every test file. A backticked or quoted value in a `WRONG_TEST:` line names a case only when it is spelled as one (`test_…`, `Test…`), is the step's own case, resolves to a defined test case or helper, or is seen in title position (the title argument of an `it(…)`/`test(…)` call, or inside `emit(…)`) in any file outside `.git`, `.tdd`, `.venv` and `node_modules`, live or not, a `.d.ts`, config file, comment or markdown file included; a `SUPERSEDED:` name that expands to the step's own case also counts. Any other value, whatever its characters (`zq7marker_old`, `x.y`, `a b`), is prose, so a line whose only such spans are values names no case. A `WRONG_TEST` naming no case (a `WRONG_TEST:` line whose body is not `none`, or a `WRONG_TEST` claim in prose that is neither negated nor quoted) answers `repair <the step's own case>` on exit 0 even once the step's free reroute is spent, once more after it; when the step's `reroute` events after its first already hold a `kind=repair` one it prints `repair-exhausted <case>` on **exit 1**, never `revise`. A `WRONG_TEST:` line whose body is `none`, and `WRONG_TEST` mentioned only negated or quoted, still answer `revise` (exit 3).
- `$S/pin-reject-route.sh [--missing-reroute] <reason-file> <ledger-file> <matcher> <target-test> | --recheck <reason-file> <ledger-file> <matcher>` — **routes a verify-pin REJECT by script.**
  `$S/pin-reject-route.sh --recheck <reason-file> <ledger-file> <matcher>` routes a verify-refactor REJECT from `pin-recheck.sh` instead, taking no `<target-test>` and running no test: when the reason has a line `pin stayed green under its mutation: <file>` or `pin stayed green on the before-state: <file>`, it inserts a `⟦diff-checked⟧ ⟦added⟧` step after the step the matcher names, `edit the case of the pin on <file> so its recorded mutation turns it red` (through `add-ledger-step.sh`, so it is in `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` too), prints `repin <file>` and exits **0**. For a reason with a line `not checked: <file> (old text in no file of the repo)` or `(old text in more than one file of the repo)` it prints `not-checked <file>` and exits **1**, ledger and `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` untouched. For a reason with neither line it exits **1**, ledger untouched, printing `revise` when a `.tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-half-rejected-wait` record beside the ledger names the matched step (read, never removed) and `revise-impossible` otherwise, since `revise.sh <n> refactor` exits 2 without that record; the conductor bails `not-checked` and `revise-impossible` with cause `needs-user`, a process failure. It prints `repin-failed <file>` and exits **1**, ledger untouched, when the ledger already holds a checked re-pin step for that file (the re-pin ran and the pin is still disarmed): the conductor bails with cause `repin-failed`, a process failure.
  Under `--missing-reroute`, a reason reporting a mutant of the added case that survives (`stays green`), while the target case is red on the step's before-state, prints `revise` and exits **1** with the ledger and events untouched, so the step goes back to Red to strengthen the case; only a reason with a clause (cut at `,`, `;`, `.` or a newline) that names the before-state and says green or pass and holds no `fail`, `red`, `not`, `n't` or `after` ("the case is green on the before-state"; not "fails on the before-state and passes after") ticks nothing, prints `unpinnable-missing` on stdout (neither `retired` nor `revise`), writes one
  `unpinnable-missing` event carrying the matched step, and exits **2** (exit **3**, nothing written, when the matcher
  matches no open step); bad arguments still print nothing on stdout. A reason with no such clause and no `stays green` is one of the other reasons below: `--already-holds` retires the step. It runs `tick-ledger.sh --unpinnable` in-process, passing `<target-test>` as its `--case` (a bare name, one with no `::` and no `/`, is first joined as `<TEST_PATH>::<name>`, `TEST_PATH` the last such line of the `develop-red.out` beside `<ledger-file>`, when that report exists, has the line and its file names the name as a whole word; otherwise the name passes unchanged),
  only for the other reasons or without `--missing-reroute`; when that answers 1 and `--missing-reroute` was given, it runs
  `tick-ledger.sh --already-holds` with the same case. It prints `retired` and exits **0** when either retired the step (` ⟂ retired unpinnable`
  with one `unpinnable-step` event, or ` ⟂ retired already holds` with one `already-holds-step` event, never a `revise`
  event). It prints `revise` and exits **1**, ledger and events untouched, when the reason has no such before-state-green clause and does not say `stays green` (under `--missing-reroute` a before-state-green clause never reaches here: it is exit 2), or under `--missing-reroute` reports a surviving mutant (`stays green`, no such clause) of a case red on the before-state, or the target test fails (and,
  under `--missing-reroute`, `--already-holds` also answered 1). **Exit 2** with a usage line on stderr for fewer than four
  arguments (the `<target-test>` included), or no answer; a `<target-test>` that never ran (see `tick-ledger.sh` below) is that answer, printing neither `retired` nor `revise`, ledger and events untouched. Any other tick-ledger code (3, no unchecked step matched) is passed through without printing `retired`.
- `$S/pin-fast-tier.sh '<fast-cmd>'` — run the fast tier over the tree a pin would commit,
  from the worktree. Takes the fast command as its one argument. On failure it writes the
  command's output to `.tdd/verify-pin.reason`. **Exit 0 = the command passed, 1 = it failed
  (output in the reason file), 2 = no command given.**
- `$S/mutation-probe.sh <file> <old> <new> -- <command…> | --before <sha> --keep <test-path> -- <command…>` — mutate `<file>` (`<old>` replaced by `<new>`), run `<command>` against the mutation, restore the file, and write the run's `.tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md` record, which carries a `context` field: the full source line holding `<old>`. The mutation text is kept out of the tracked record, and the reason is a target repo's guard over its tracked files (an address outside `example.*`, say), which the log of a mutation must not trip: `old`, `new` and `context` are stored encoded, so the record holds neither the old nor the new text verbatim. `VERI_MUTATION_ADDRESS_DOMAIN` (unset or empty: no check): when set, a `<old>` or `<new>` holding an email address whose host is not that domain makes the probe exit **2** before it mutates the file, runs the command or writes the record, stderr naming the address and the allowed domain. In mutate mode, `<old>` must be present in the working-tree `<file>` and `<command>` must exit 0 on the unmutated file (the baseline run): either failing is **exit 2**, no record, the file untouched. The mutated run's exit 0 is **exit 1** (the mutant survived, no record); a non-zero exit writes the record. When the command runs several tests the probe does not prove which one failed. **Exit 2** also for bad arguments, or no answer.
- `$S/pin-target.sh <step-red-report> [<red-missing-report>…]` — print the `TARGET_TEST:` value of
  its **first** argument, the step's own red report, never a later `red-missing` report's.
  **Exit 0** the value printed, **exit 2** no argument (usage line on stderr), an unreadable report, or a report with no `TARGET_TEST:` line, since a caller left with no target got no answer.
- `$S/pin-recheck.sh --branch-base <sha> <base-sha> [--ledger <file>]` — re-run each recorded mutation whose file the range touches (a before record's file is its `keep` file). With `--ledger <file>`, only the records of that mutations ledger the range touches are replayed and other ledgers' records are not; a selector matching no touched record exits **3** and replays nothing, never widening to all records; `--ledger` given with no file, or any unknown argument, exits **2** with the usage. `--branch-base <sha>`, the run's recorded branch base, comes first and is required: missing, or its sha not matching `^[0-9a-f]{7,40}$`, exits **2** with one stderr line naming `--branch-base` and the usage. Without it the replay set is unchanged, every touched record of every ledger:
  **exit 0** every such pin still goes red, printing any that need a look; **exit 1** a pin
  stayed green under its mutation (one that stays green under it on the range's base too is stale, not exit 1), a before-state pin this range disarmed (green at `HEAD`, red on the range's base), a mutation pin not checked (`not checked: <file> (<reason>)` on stdout: its old text is gone from its file and in no other file of that file's directory, or in more than one, and was in its file at `<base-sha>` or added or removed there by a commit of the range), see **Moved**, or a mutated run did not build, which prints
  `needs a look: <file> (mutation did not build)` on stdout; exit 1 also covers a mutated run
  that exits non-zero when the unmutated run printed nothing, printed as `needs a look`; a
  `needs a look` record from a failed run writes that run's stdout and stderr to stderr,
  limited to its last 40 lines. A `needs a look` line names its reason: `old text occurs more than once` (the pin is not mutated), `old text moved off its recorded line` (the old text occurs once, on a line other than the record's `context`; the pin is not mutated), `base run failed`, or `mutation and control green`. A record without a `context` field gets the duplicate check only (`old text occurs more than once`), not the moved-line check; `pin-recheck.sh` reads a record whose fields are plain text as well as an encoded one. Pins sharing one file and one reason print one line with a count, not one line each; these pins leave the exit code and the receipt as they were. **Exit 2** no answer, or a `--branch-base` sha that cannot be resolved or replayed (the base worktree cannot be created at it; stderr names the cause), never read as stale or as a disarm, or a before-state pin with a green commit whose replay range names no file (stderr `empty replay range: before <sha> green <sha>`), or a pin's recorded command that cannot run (not found, or `make` with no makefile), which exits 2 naming the pin file and the command, as does a command `make` rejects as a usage error (make exits 2 printing `invalid option` or its usage), which writes no usage text to stderr; an ordinary red base run (the command runs and fails) is not that: it prints `needs a look`; or a pin's `TESTS=<path>` test file that is missing at `HEAD` yet was not deleted by the range and the `--branch-base` sha holds (stderr `named test missing at HEAD: <path> (pin file <record>)`); a mutation record whose mutated file is absent at `HEAD` is not exit 2 (see **Stale**); **exit 3** no record touches the range, or every selected pin is retired.
  **Red cache:** a mutation record that went red is remembered in `.tdd/pin-recheck-red/<sha256>`, the hash of `HEAD`'s sha, the mutated file's original bytes, the record's old text, new text and command; a later run with the same key skips that record (no base run, no mutation) and counts it still red. A change to anything else the suite reads does not invalidate it. A record that stayed green, needed a look or was stale writes no entry. SIGTERM or SIGINT during a mutated run restores the mutated file before the script exits (SIGTERM exits 143).
  **Retired:** a pin, whatever its record type (a before record included, its mutated file being its `keep` file), whose every `TESTS=<path>` file the range deletes (`git diff --diff-filter=D <base-sha>..HEAD`) is neither replayed nor mutated; it prints `retired: <record file> <mutated file> <path>` on stdout, one line per deleted path, and counts toward exit 3 when no other pin is selected (receipt written); a live pin in the same range decides the exit (0 or 1). A pin naming several `TESTS=` files, only some deleted, drops the deleted arguments and runs on the rest. The `retired:` line is printed before any later exit 2 in the record loop, so a run that retires one pin and then fails on another prints both. A mutation pin whose mutated file the range deletes is retired the same way, neither mutated nor run, printing `retired: <record file> <mutated file> <mutated file>`. A before-state pin is never retired for its replay range: a replay path that neither `HEAD` nor `<base-sha>` holds (deleted before the range, perhaps with its directory) is skipped, neither restored nor removed, at `HEAD` and on the base alike, and the pin still runs.
  **Moved:** a mutation pin whose old text is gone from its recorded file is searched for in the pin's directory first, then, when no file there holds it, in every tracked file outside `.tdd/`; it is replayed in that file when exactly one file holds it. One whose old text is in no file of the repo, or in more than one, is not counted as checked: it prints `not checked: <file> (old text in no file of the repo)` or `not checked: <file> (old text in more than one file of the repo)` on stdout, mutates none of those files, exits 1 and leaves no `.tdd/pin-recheck-ok` receipt. A pin whose old text is also absent from its file at `<base-sha>` (a file absent there counts as absent), and which no commit of `<base-sha>..HEAD` added to or removed from that file (`git log -S`), was broken before the range, not by it: it prints `stale: <record file> <file>` on stdout instead, with no effect on the exit code; `<base-sha>` is read only for such a pin, so one whose old text is at `HEAD` replays as before (#4644). Text the range itself added and then removed (a green commit adding it, a refactor rewriting it away) is the range's breakage: `not checked`, exit 1. A `git show` or `git log` that fails there exits 2, stderr naming the file.
  **Superseded:** a pin whose test file survives the range, and which a ledger step committed in the range declares retired with `retire-pin: <record file> <mutated file> -- <reason>`, is neither replayed nor mutated; it prints `retired: <record file> <mutated file> <reason>` on stdout, appends a `retired:` line holding the reason to the pin's mutation record file, emits one `pin-retired` event (`reason=`), counts toward exit 3 as a deleted-file retire does, and is not replayed on a later run. A declaration with an empty reason, or naming no pin, exits 2. A pin not so declared is replayed as before.
  **Stale:** a before-state pin that replays green at `HEAD` is replayed again on the range's base, the `--branch-base` sha (never `origin/main`, which a leaf can rewrite), in a detached `git worktree` there that is removed afterwards, also on a failed replay. Red on the base: this range disarmed it, `pin stayed green on the before-state: <keep>` on stderr and exit 1. Green on both, or its `TESTS=<path>` file missing at `HEAD` and at the base without the range deleting it: stale, printed `stale: <record file> <test path>` on stdout (the file part of the `TESTS=` value), with no effect on the exit code; a mutation pin that stays green under its mutation at `HEAD` is replayed the same way: its mutation is applied in the base worktree and the recorded command run there, and green on the base too is stale, printed `stale: <record file> <mutated file>` on stdout, with no effect on the exit code, where red on the base is `pin stayed green under its mutation: <file>` on stderr and exit 1, as it is when the mutated file, or the recorded old text in it, is absent on the base; a mutation record whose mutated file is absent at `HEAD` (not deleted by the range) is stale without a run: `stale: <record file> <mutated file>` on stdout, no command run, no effect on the exit code, never exit 2; a range whose only selected pins are stale exits 0 and writes the receipt.
  A before-state pin replays the files changed from its `before` sha to the last commit of its
  issue's ledger (the step's tick), falling back to the record's `green` when the ledger has no
  commit; a before record with no green commit and an empty replay range runs its command instead of exiting 2; replaying a range never fails on a file absent at both of its ends (added after `before`, then deleted by the checked range): the replay skips it rather than exiting 2; `mutation-probe.sh --before` writes no `green` into an `.tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md` record.
  It removes `.tdd/pin-recheck-ok` at start and writes it, holding `HEAD`'s sha, only on exit 0 or 3: that receipt is what
  `tick-ledger.sh` (a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step) and `clear-refactor-pending.sh` require.
- `$S/tick-ledger.sh [--refactor-pending | --phase <phase> | --phase-owed <phase> | --surround-short | --unpinnable <reason-file> | --already-holds <reason-file>] [--case <id>] .tdd/issue-<n>.md "<substring>"` — check a step off
  in the **committed ledger**, in place (staged + committed together with that step's green
  code). Ticking a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step also re-records, via `tests-frozen.sh rerecord-earlier`, the
  earlier frozen cases its diff changed; **exit 2** can then follow the write of the ledger (the re-record failed). **Exit 2** also refuses a step that names a go-red control (`mutation-probe.sh --before <sha>`, or `the go-red control is <file> at <sha>`) when `.tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md` holds no `before <sha>` record for that sha and no `file`/`old`/`new` record for that file, naming the missing record and leaving the ledger unmodified; a step with neither wording, a bare mention of "go-red control" included, ticks as before. **Exit 2** likewise refuses a `⟦go-red⟧` step, ledger unmodified, when its probe (``probe `<file>` `<old>` → `<new>` on `<case>` ``) does not parse, the mutation record cannot be read as one, or it holds no entry whose `file`, `old` and `new` equal the probe's and whose `command` names `<case>` (an entry with no `command` never matches): a record of another probe on that file (a verify-pin probe's) does not tick it. **Exit 2** also refuses, ledger unmodified, a step whose first token is a bare `⟦go-red⟧` that does not trail it, with or without a record, saying the marker must trail the step. **Exit 2** also refuses to tick a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step, ledger byte-identical, naming `pin-recheck.sh`, when `.tdd/pin-recheck-ok` is absent, its sha does not resolve, or a path outside `.tdd/` differs between that sha and `HEAD`. **Exit 3** if no unchecked step matched the substring — the file is left untouched,
  so a green commit that ticked nothing says so instead of reading as done.
  Ticking an `⟦added⟧` step also ticks the first still-open line of equal step text (cut at ` ⟂ `) in `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md`, skipped when that file is missing.
  `--refactor-pending` appends ` ⟂ refactor pending` to that same line in the same write, so
  the green commit records the step as **green but not yet tidied**; that is the flag the
  green PASS ticks with, and the one the pin path must tick without — a pinned step runs no
  refactor loop, so a mark left there would never be cleared. A `⟦replaces-case⟧` step's
  deletion green after a pin PASS is a Green: it ticks with the flag and runs Refactor.
  `--phase <phase>` instead appends ` ⟂ <phase> done` to the first open step listing
  `⟦phases: …⟧` that matches, and leaves its checkbox `[ ]`; once every listed phase is marked
  the step reads as `done` to `ledger-steps.sh` and `next-unchecked.sh` skips it. **Exit 2**,
  ledger byte-identical, for a phase the step does not list or one that is not its next unmarked
  phase; also for a step listing `⟦phases: …⟧` ticked without `--phase`, and for `--phase` given
  with `--refactor-pending`. `--phase-owed <phase>` appends ` ⟂ <phase> owed` instead, under the
  same refusals; an owed phase ends the step, so it and every phase after it are never handed out
  again (see the exhausted-contract paragraph in `<base directory>/PHASED.md`). `--unpinnable <reason-file>` retires the matched step instead of ticking it as green: when the reason file (a verify-pin REJECT, `.tdd/verify-pin.reason`) says the mutation `stays green` or has a clause (cut at `,`, `;`, `.` or a newline) that names the before-state and says green or pass and holds no `fail`, `red`, `not`, `n't` or `after`, it ticks the step `[x]` with ` ⟂ retired unpinnable` appended, in the same write, and appends one `unpinnable-step` event (`step=`, `reason=` from its `REASON:` line) to `.tdd/events.jsonl`, never a `revise` event, and only when the case named by `--case <id>` passes (run through the repo's one-test-file command (the `veri: one test file:` line of `CLAUDE.md`, as `detect-test-command.sh --tier "one test file"` prints it) given the case id's file path, the part before `::`, or as `make test TESTS="<id>"` when the repo declares none). Exit 0 retired; exit 1 the reason is any other rejection, no `--case` is named, or the named case ran and fails, ledger and events untouched and stderr naming the step and the cause; exit 2 no answer (bad arguments, an unreadable ledger, or the event not written) or the named case never ran (its case command exited 2, 126 or 127 (not executable, not found), or 128 and above, or was killed by a signal (a negative code); or its file path, the part of the id before `::`, does not exist, or its output says no tests were collected or ran — under the `make test TESTS="<id>"` fallback the path is checked only when the id holds `::` or a `/`), stderr naming the case, how the command ended (`exit <code>`, or `killed by <SIGNAME>`) and the command that was run, `case <id> never run (<how it ended>): <reason>: <command>`, ledger and events untouched; a case command exiting 1 or 3 to 125 is a case that ran and fails, exit 1, since runners such as mocha exit with their failure count. Make exits 2 for any failed recipe, so a failed make run (the `make test TESTS="<id>"` fallback, or a one-test-file command whose first word is `make`, run with `LC_ALL=C`) is read from its stderr, never its stdout: it is a case that ran and fails (exit 1) only when make's last non-empty stderr line, which make prints after the recipe has ended, is a top-level `make: *** [<target>] Error 1`, no other `make: *** [<target>]` text on any stderr line (glued to recipe output, or a signal's `Killed: 9`) ends in anything but `Error 1`, and the line just before it is not make's own `make: <command>: No such file or directory` or `Permission denied` (a recipe command make could not execute, which it reports as `Error 1`); any other failed make run is a case that never ran (exit 2). So a failure inside a nested make (`make[<n>]: …`), or a `make -k` run with several failures, reads as no answer: a repository that needs either declares its own one-test-file command. A one-test-file command whose first word is not `make` is read by its exit code alone, its output never parsed for make's lines; exit 3 as above when no unchecked step matched. `--already-holds <reason-file>` retires the matched step as ` ⟂ retired already holds` instead, for a step whose behaviour already holds and was rerouted to a pin step: it ticks the step `[x]` with that mark appended and appends one `already-holds-step` event to `.tdd/events.jsonl`, and it requires no mutation wording in the reason file. It exits 1, touching neither the ledger nor `events.jsonl`, when the reason file is empty or has no `REASON:` line with non-blank text, when no `--case` is named, or when the named case ran and fails; it exits 2, likewise untouched, when the named case never ran. `--unpinnable` still retires only a reason that says the mutation `stays green` or has such a before-state-green clause; both retire only when the case named by `--case <id>` passes. When either flag retires a step, it also drops from `.tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md` every mutation record whose `TESTS=` test is absent from the tree. `$S/ledger-steps.sh next-phase < .tdd/issue-<n>.md` prints the first phased step's next
  unmarked phase, **exit 3** when none is left. The cycle loop runs a phased step through
  both — see `<base directory>/PHASED.md`.
  `--surround-short` (with the ledger and matcher only) records a legacy step's one free relaunch of `develop-surround` by appending ` ⟂ surround short` to the open step whose next phase is `surround`, ticking nothing: **exit 0** recorded; **exit 1** the step already carries the mark, its relaunch spent (a resumed run's included), the ledger untouched; **exit 2** `surround` is not the step's next phase, or the ledger is unreadable; **exit 3** no open step matched.
- `$S/reorder-ledger-steps.sh .tdd/issue-<n>.md "<step>" before|after "<anchor>"` — the one sanctioned way to change the order of **unchecked** steps. It rewrites the ledger only and never commits; the caller commits the ledger on its own in a `plan:` commit. Running it is an operator's decision, never the conductor's own.
- `$S/drop-ledger-step.sh <ledger-file> "<step-substring>"` — remove the one **unchecked** step whose line holds the substring, then commit the ledger alone in a `plan: drop a ledger step` commit (other dirty files stay uncommitted), using the repository's own git identity. **Exit 0** the step was removed and committed; **exit 1** no step matches, more than one does, the match is ticked (`- [x]`) or carries `⟂ refactor pending`, or the run's conductor is live (stderr names its pid) — the ledger is unchanged in every case; **exit 2** bad arguments (a usage line) or the ledger is not inside a git work tree (one stderr line), the ledger unchanged. Folding a redundant step into another is dropping it.
- `$S/next-pending-refactor.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md` — print the **first ticked step whose
  refactor is unverified**: the description of the first `- [x]` step carrying the
  ` ⟂ refactor pending` mark, with the mark stripped, so what comes back is the same text
  `next-unchecked.sh` hands the loop and the tick wrappers match on. **Exit 0** when it
  printed one; **exit 3** with empty stdout when no step carries the mark — every green step
  has been tidied, so a resume has no refactor loop to re-enter.
- `$S/ledger-growth.sh <plan-ledger-file> <ledger-file> | plan-sha | record-existing <ledger-file> | record-rewrite <pre-rewrite-ledger-file> <ledger-file> | reopen <ledger-file> <needle> <change>` — called by the conductor at each ledger
  commit: compares the ledger with the plan commit's. The plan commit, `<plan-sha>`, is the newest
  `plan: scope` or `plan: rewrite` commit, so after a verify-plan REJECT the rewrite is the
  baseline: `git log --format='%H %s' | $S/ledger-growth.sh plan-sha` prints its sha (**exit 2**
  when the log holds none). Each step line the plan did not hold must match, by equal step text, a
  record `add-ledger-step.sh` wrote: a line of the tracked `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` beside the
  ledger (committed with it, so it survives a resume in a fresh worktree) or a `step-added` event
  of actor `add-ledger-step` in `events.jsonl`; and it must carry `⟦added⟧` unless it is a
  `⟦suite-repair⟧`, `⟦ci-repair⟧` or `--planned` insertion. The `⟦added⟧` lines never exceed
  ⌈`VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` (default 50) × P ÷ 100⌉, P the steps in `<plan-ledger-file>`. Ticks, ` ⟂ refactor pending` and phase marks on planned lines
  are not growth. **Exit 0** when every line passes; **exit 1**, naming each failing line on
  stderr, when one lacks its record or `⟦added⟧`, or the cap is passed; **exit 2** on a missing
  argument, an unreadable ledger or plan file, a malformed `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`, or a set
  `VERI_STEP_GROWTH`, which is retired (stderr names `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`).
  **`record-existing`** — the one-off migration for a ledger that grew before
  the records existed: writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` beside the ledger, one `- [ ] <step>` line, as
  `add-ledger-step.sh` writes it, for each step line the plan commit (the newest `plan: scope` or
  `plan: rewrite`) did not hold; `⟦suite-repair⟧`, `⟦ci-repair⟧` and `⟦added⟧` lines keep their marks.
  **Exit 0** once the file is written; **exit 2**, writing nothing, when `issue-<n>-added.md` already
  exists (so it runs once per ledger), when the history holds no plan commit, or when the ledger or the
  plan's copy cannot be read.
  **`record-rewrite`** — records the steps a verify-plan rewrite adds, so the rewrite route itself
  writes their records: appends to `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` beside the ledger (creating it when
  absent) one `- [ ] <step>` record, in the line format `add-ledger-step.sh` writes, for each step
  line of `<ledger-file>` that `<pre-rewrite-ledger-file>` did not hold and no existing record already
  covers. It also writes one `step-reworded` event, keys `from` (the step's text before the rewrite) and `to` (its
  text after), for each ledger position whose step text differs, when the two ledgers hold the same number of
  steps; when the counts differ it writes none, since positions pair only when nothing was inserted or removed.
  **Exit 0** once written; **exit 2** on a missing argument or an unreadable file.
  **`reopen`** — `reopen <ledger-file> <needle> <change>` unticks a passed `⟦skill-edit⟧` step for a post-review change: the one step line holding `<needle>` becomes `- [ ] <step> change: <change> ⟦added⟧ ⟦criterion <k>⟧` (one `⟦added⟧` mark, a ` ⟂ refactor pending` tail dropped), and the same line is appended to `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` beside the ledger (created when absent), so the growth check finds its record. Exit 0 once reopened; exit 2, changing nothing, when the ledger cannot be read (stderr names the file), when the needle matches more than one step (stderr lists them), or when the step is not a `⟦skill-edit⟧` step; exit 3, changing nothing and writing no record, when no step matches or the matching step is not ticked, so a repeated reopen never spends the growth cap.
- `$S/ledger-growth.sh split <ledger-file>` — called by the Draft PR step (*4. Draft PR*, sub-step 2) when it composes the PR body, for its `## Plan growth` section. It compares N, the ledger's step lines, with P, those of the ledger at the oldest `plan: scope` commit of the ledger's own history (the plan the run scoped, not the newest rewrite, so a chain of rewrites each under the bound still adds up), neither counting a `⟦suite-repair⟧` or `⟦ci-repair⟧` line, against B = ⌈`VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` (default 50) × P ÷ 100⌉; a rewritten, an `⟦added⟧` and a ticked step each count as one. **Exit 0** when N − P > B, printing two lines: `plan grew from <P> to <N> steps, past the growth bound of <B> (VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT <pct>% of <P>, rounded up)`, then `split suggested after criterion <k>: criteria <first>–<k> (<a> steps) here, criteria <k'>–<last> (<b> steps) in a follow-up issue`, the boundary between two consecutive criterion numbers of the counted steps' `⟦criterion k⟧` tags that leaves the two sides' step counts closest, the earlier on a tie, untagged steps counting on neither side; with one criterion number the second line is `no split point: every counted step serves criterion <k>`, with none `no split point: no criterion tag`. **Exit 3**, printing nothing, when N − P ≤ B. **Exit 2**, nothing on stdout and one stderr line, on a missing or extra argument, an unreadable ledger or history, a history with no `plan: scope` commit, a malformed `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`, or a set `VERI_STEP_GROWTH` (retired; stderr names `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`). It refuses nothing and writes nothing: a plan that grew went ahead, and this only reports it.
- `$S/suite-red-resume.sh check <ledger-file> <branch-red-file> <default-red-file> | insert <ledger-file> <branch-red-file> | rebased-tip <ledger-file> <red-file> <pre-rebase-sha> | flake <at> <red-file> <alone-red-file> | test-only <base-sha> <alone-cmd> <suite>…` — decide whether a resume may go on past a red baseline. Each red file lists one suite per line, optionally followed by ` — ` and its first FAIL line; suites compare by the part before ` — `. **Exit 0**, printing the branch-red file, when no branch-red suite is red on the default branch; **exit 1** naming on stderr each suite red on both; `check` reads no event log; **exit 2** with no arguments (a usage line) or an unreadable file. Called by the conductor's resume preflight. **`insert`** inserts one `⟦suite-repair⟧` step, `fix the full-suite failure: ` plus every line of the branch-red file joined by `; `, before the ledger's first unchecked step (after its last done step when none is unchecked), through `add-ledger-step.sh`, so `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` and a `step-added` event record it; that script's refusals keep their exit codes (1, 2). `insert` inserts no step and exits 0 when the union of the units named by the consecutive pending `⟦skill-edit⟧` steps, counted from the ledger's first unchecked step, covers every unit the red is explained by (every branch-red suite is red only because one of those units' `GRADED` signatures is stale, or because the unit owns the suite through its `## Requirements` rows, e.g. `test_scripts_md_rows.py` for `skills/tdd/SCRIPTS.md`), so the first such step runs first; graded suites match by basename, whether the red lists them by basename or by full path. When every unit the red is explained by has a pending `⟦skill-edit⟧` step but a non-skill-edit unchecked step separates them, `insert` inserts nothing, leaves the ledger unchanged and exits **2** with a stderr line naming the skill-edit steps and `reorder-ledger-steps.sh`, because a `⟦suite-repair⟧` step cannot regrade a signed unit; a red those units do not jointly explain, or a stale-graded unit with no pending `⟦skill-edit⟧` step, is still inserted as a `⟦suite-repair⟧` step, and the other `insert` exit codes are unchanged. When every red names only cases pinning one graded unit's prose, `insert` appends a `⟦skill-edit⟧` step naming that unit; for any other red it appends a `⟦suite-repair⟧` step. **`rebased-tip`** takes the ledger, the red file of a rebased tip's full run and the `<pre-rebase-sha>`: it runs `insert` on them; when `insert` exits 0 it also writes `<pre-rebase-sha>` to `rebase-lease` beside the ledger (in `.tdd/`) and exits 0, the rebased branch kept; when `insert` exits 1 it runs `git reset --hard <pre-rebase-sha>` and exits 1, writing no `rebase-lease`; any other `insert` code is returned unchanged. **`flake`** takes the call site and two files: `<at>` names the call site (`resume` or `suite-repair`); `<red-file>` lists the test files the full-tier red names; `<alone-red-file>` lists those of them still red when run alone with the repo's own runner, or is the single line `none` when nothing is still red alone. **Exit 0** when `<red-file>` names at least one file and `<alone-red-file>` is the single line `none`: it writes one `suite-flake` event naming each file of `<red-file>` and `<at>`. **Exit 1** when any file is still red alone or `<red-file>` names no file: it writes no event. **Exit 2** on bad arguments (a usage line) or an unreadable file. **`test-only`** takes a `<base-sha>`, an `<alone-cmd>` and one or more suites: **exit 0** when the diff since `<base-sha>` touches test files only, deletes no case, removes no assertion, and every named suite passes alone under `<alone-cmd>`; **exit 1** naming the still-red suite, the deleted case, the case that lost an assertion, or the production file (a new untracked one counts); **exit 2** on bad arguments or when git cannot resolve `<base-sha>` (one stderr line).
- `$S/rerun-alone.sh '<alone-cmd>' <red-output-file>` — reruns each failing test the red output names, once and alone, through `sh -c '<alone-cmd>' sh <target>` (the target as `$1`). Targets come from the first of these that finds any: pytest `FAILED`/`ERROR` lines, each as its whole node id (`path::name`, up to the ` - ` reason separator or the end of the line, so a parametrized id holding a space stays whole), in output order; else every `*.test.<ext>` file named anywhere in the output; else lines that are a bare `test_*.py` path. Duplicates are dropped. **Exit 0**: every target passes alone, and one `suite-flake` event naming the targets (`files=<target>,…`) is written. **Exit 1**: a target is still red alone, or the output names none, and no event is written. **Exit 2**: bad arguments or no answer.
- `$S/ci-flake.sh <n> <head-sha> <run-id> '<alone-cmd>' <log-file>` — the flake check of a red CI run (*4. Draft PR*, sub-step 2). It reads test targets from `<log-file>`, the saved `gh run view <run-id> --log-failed` output, the targets `rerun-alone.sh` reads from the same runner output once gh's per-line job, step and timestamp prefix (a byte order mark may precede the timestamp) and ANSI colour codes are dropped; runs each once alone through `<alone-cmd>`, the target as `$1`, each run bounded by `VERI_TIMEOUT`; and when all pass re-runs the failed jobs with `gh run rerun <run-id> --failed`, writes a `ci-rerun` event (`head`, `run`, `files`) and drops the head's CI wait marks as `wait-ci.sh --fresh-window <head-sha>` does. It writes no `suite-flake` event. **Exit 0** the re-run was accepted, the targets printed one per line; **exit 1** the log names no target or one stays red alone, no event and no re-run; **exit 2** bad arguments, an unreadable log or a non-empty line of it that is not one gh line, a head that is not 40 hex, a worktree whose HEAD is not `<head-sha>` or that has uncommitted changes (one stderr line), a log naming more than 5 targets (the check's overall bound), an alone run with no answer (not done within `VERI_TIMEOUT`, or killed by a signal), or `gh` refusing the re-run; no `ci-rerun` event, except after `gh run rerun` itself, whose attempt is written as one even when gh answers non-zero (it may have started the re-run), so a head is never re-run past the budget; **exit 3** the head was already re-run, or `VERI_CI_FLAKE_RERUNS` (default 1) re-runs were made since issue `<n>`'s last `conductor-start`, nothing run.
- `$S/suite-flakes.sh <run-dir> [<run-dir>...]` — reads the `suite-flake` events in each run directory's `.tdd/events.jsonl` and lists every target (a test file or a pytest node id, as the events carry it) cleared by the alone-rerun in two or more of those runs, with the run numbers (the directories' basenames). **Exit 0** when it lists any, **exit 3** when none, **exit 2** on no argument or a run directory with no event log.
- `$S/clear-refactor-pending.sh .tdd/issue-<n>.md "<substring>"` — the counterpart of
  `tick-ledger.sh --refactor-pending`: **strip the ` ⟂ refactor pending` mark** from the first
  ticked step matching the substring, in place, once that step's refactor has been verified.
  Nothing on stdout. **Exit 0** when it cleared the mark; **exit 3** when no ticked step both
  matched and carried it — the file is left byte-identical, because nothing is written unless
  the transform found its line. **Exit 2**, file byte-identical, naming `pin-recheck.sh`, when
  `.tdd/pin-recheck-ok` is absent, its sha does not resolve, or a path outside `.tdd/` differs between that sha and `HEAD`.
- `$S/plan-reverify.sh <ledger-file>` — decide whether a resume must re-verify the plan. It reads the `events.jsonl` beside the ledger, counting only the events of the ledger's own issue (the `<n>` of `issue-<n>.md`), for the issue's last `verify-plan` PASS, and compares its time with the author dates of the `plan:` commits. **Exit 0** when a `plan:` commit is at or after that PASS (a tie re-verifies), or there is no PASS for the issue (or no `events.jsonl`): verify-plan is owed. **Exit 3** when that PASS is newer than every `plan:` commit: no re-verify. **Exit 2** with a message on stderr on a usage error (no argument), a ledger not named `issue-<n>`, or no `plan:` commit in the ledger's history.
- `$S/same-reject.sh <previous-reason-file> <current-reason-file>` — compare the reasons of two rejections after collapsing runs of whitespace: a file's `REASON:` line when it has one, else its whole text, the bare reason `parse-verdict.sh` prints and `.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason` holds (#4550). **Exit 0** when equal; **exit 1** when they differ; **exit 2** with a usage line when called with no arguments, or when either file is missing or holds no reason (empty or whitespace only).
- `$S/add-ledger-step.sh [--planned] [--criterion <k>] <ledger> after|before|instead-of "<anchor substring>" '<text>' […]` — **grow
  the plan mid-run**: insert each text as `- [ ] <text> ⟦added⟧ ⟦criterion k⟧` directly after
  the anchor step, directly before it, or in its place, inheriting the anchor's criterion
  unless `--criterion <k>` names the criterion the added steps serve — any whole number k ≥ 1,
  whether or not a step already carries `⟦criterion k⟧`. `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`
  (default 50) bounds how many `⟦added⟧` steps one ledger may hold, ⌈`VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` × P ÷ 100⌉,
  P the steps in the ledger at the commit `ledger-growth.sh plan-sha` prints (read with `git log`
  and `git show` beside the ledger; only a batch that leaves an `⟦added⟧` step reads it), on a **fifth axis** of its
  own — plan growth, never merged with the other four. **Exit 0** = inserted; **exit 1** = the
  budget cannot hold them, so nothing is inserted and every text is appended as a plain bullet
  under a trailing `## Owed steps` heading instead; **exit 2** = the anchor matches no step or
  more than one, the budget is unreadable, `VERI_STEP_GROWTH` (retired) is set (stderr names
  `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`), no plan commit can be read for P, `--criterion` names a k that is
  not a whole number ≥ 1, or a text already carries a `⟦criterion k⟧` tag (the script
  names the tag, and one bad text inserts none of the batch), or a step text carrying
  `⟦diff-checked⟧` with no `⟦criterion k⟧`, whether given by `--criterion` or inherited from
  the anchor (nothing inserted), with the ledger left
  byte-identical. A step counts as a skill-edit step only when its text starts with `⟦skill-edit⟧` or carries it in its trailing tag run; a step that merely mentions `⟦skill-edit⟧` in prose is not one. A skill-edit step's unit is the first path-shaped backticked token of its text (one containing a `/` or ending in `.md`, earlier non-path tokens skipped); a `<file>#<heading>` unit runs past pairs of backticks in its heading, each opened by a backtick that whitespace precedes and a non-space follows, and ends at the first other backtick, so a unit closed by punctuation ends there; with no such backtick after its last pair it is the plain token. A skill-edit step is refused with **exit 2**, nothing inserted, when its text holds backticks but no path-shaped token (stderr `add-ledger-step.sh: no unit path was found in step: …`), when it holds no backticks at all, when the unit file it names does not exist (stderr `<unit>: the unit file does not exist`), or when the issue's Requirements restate no row that differs from the unit's list (stderr `restate no row that differs`). A `<text>` holding the refactor-pending delimiter (a space, `⟂` U+27C2, a
  space) is also refused with **exit 2**, stderr naming that token, before the ledger is read,
  so the ledger is left untouched and a batch holding one such text inserts none of them; a
  text with U+27C2 but no surrounding spaces is still inserted. Every text
  one growth is made of goes in **one call**: the script takes them all at once and reads the
  budget once per call, so a call per text would refuse a third text that one call either fits
  whole or files whole. Every insertion is marked in the log beside the call it made —
  `VERI_ISSUE=<n> VERI_ACTOR=conductor VERI_STEP="$(cat .tdd/issue-<n>.md | $S/ledger-steps.sh current)" $S/event.sh step-added
  anchor='<anchor>' added=<count>` — because a plan that changes with no record leaves the
  log unable to say which steps the run chose for itself; an exit 1 owes the same mark with
  `added=0`, since a step the budget refused is still a decision the run made.
  When another process's conductor is live in the ledger's worktree, the script also commits the
  grown ledger and its `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` record in a commit of their own, so a later bail's
  revert keeps the step. Stderr then states whether the added step is **durable** (committed,
  naming the commit) or **not durable** (uncommitted).
  `--planned --criterion <k>` inserts steps for a criterion no step serves yet, without `⟦added⟧`, so they do not count toward `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` — the resume's own scoping, not discovered growth. The refusals are all **exit 2**, with nothing inserted and nothing filed as owed:
  `--planned without --criterion`, a criterion a ledger step already serves (the script says "already serves"),
  no readable spec file beside the ledger (the script says "cannot read the spec file", naming it),
  and a k that is not unticked in the issue body the run read (an unknown number or a ticked criterion).
- `$S/tests-frozen.sh record <record-file>|--step "<step>" [--red-case <red-test-id>] <test-file>… | check <record-file>|--step "<step>" | check-earlier <ledger> --step "<step>" [--except <case>]… | rerecord-earlier <ledger> --step "<step>" | rerecord-refactored <ledger> <green-sha> | rerecord-rebased <ledger> <pre-rebase-sha> | rerecord-approved <ledger> --approval <text> <case>... | path "<step>"` — **freeze a step's tests between verify-red and verify-green.** `record --step` digests the given test files and also every test file (`transform-touches-tests.sh`'s rule) differing from `HEAD`: modified, deleted (recorded as deleted, so its deletion is not an unrecorded change) or untracked. With `--red-case <red-test-id>`, `record --step` first checks that the step's own Red case still exists: when it is gone (deleted, or its test file deleted) it exits 1, writes no record and names the deleted case; otherwise it records as without the flag. Every `record` that exits 1 or 2 also logs a `tests-frozen-refused` event (`exit`, `reason`: the message it printed) to `.tdd/events.jsonl`, so the refusal is on the journal and not only on stderr. `check --step` exits 1 naming a recorded file whose digest changed, and also naming a test file outside the record that now differs from `HEAD`; a recorded-deleted file that exists again, and a tracked test file deleted after the record, are named too; a changed non-test file is never named. Exceptions: a `⟦replaces-case⟧` step may change the suite it names (any other unrecorded test file is still flagged), a `⟦guard-subject: <path>⟧` step may change the file it names (a guard suite or sourced test helper; any other unrecorded test file, the case Red wrote included, is still flagged), and `record --step` freezes, in the file a `⟦guard-subject: <path>⟧` marker names, only the step's own Red cases and none of that file's other changed cases, and a `⟦suite-repair⟧` or `⟦ci-repair⟧` step is never frozen, `record` writing nothing and `check` exiting 0. `rerecord-earlier` is run by `tick-ledger.sh` for a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step: it rewrites the earlier steps' frozen records of the cases that step's own diff changed, the diff running from the step's own base (derived from the step's own commits, so a later `plan:` rewrite commit does not move it) to HEAD, at their content at HEAD, so a later `check-earlier` passes; any other step exits 3. `rerecord-refactored <ledger> <green-sha>` is run only after a verify-refactor PASS, or after a `skipped` one (`VERI_SKIP_VERIFY=refactor`): it re-records every ticked step's frozen case that the refactor (the commits past `<green-sha>`) changed when the case kept its title, its assertion lines are identical modulo whitespace, and its body at `<green-sha>` matched the record, or its body at `<green-sha>^` matched the record and `<green-sha>` is one of the own commits of a ticked `⟦suite-repair⟧` step of the ledger (the verified repair green that rewrote the case; a `⟦suite-repair⟧` step ticked elsewhere in the ledger, or one not ticked, does not count); any other case is left as recorded, so `check-earlier` still refuses it. A case a `⟦replaces-case⟧` step anywhere in the ledger names, in the suite it names, is never re-recorded, and the closing `check-earlier` exempts it, so a case its replacement is about to take over does not make the run exit 1. Exits: 0 nothing left stale; 1 `check-earlier` still refuses at least one case after the re-record, whether or not this run skipped it; each case it skipped is named on stderr as `skipped: <file> <case>`; 2 no answer; 3 `.tdd/verdict-refactor.exit` holds neither `0` nor `skipped`, or is missing. When the closing `check-earlier` exits 1 or 2, every frozen record the re-record rewrote is restored byte-identical to what it was before the call, so reverting the refactor lets the next `check-earlier` pass; a record that cannot be written back exits 2. `check-earlier` accepts a case whose recorded body digest matches and whose recorded `helpers:` digest equals the digest the pre-#2888 helper pattern (stopping only at `func`) gives on the same file: it re-records that `helpers:` line under the current pattern and names the case on stderr; a helpers digest matching neither pattern still exits 1. `check-earlier`'s stdout line for a case changed by a `refactor:` commit says the record is stale against a verified refactor, rather than that a frozen case was edited. `rerecord-approved <ledger> --approval <text> <case>...` is the carrying-out of a human's answer to re-record after a `rerecord-refactored` skip: it re-records exactly the named cases, in the earlier record that holds each, at their `HEAD` bodies (a case is named `<file>::<case>`, or by a bare case name that matches exactly one recorded case), each with its `helpers:` line, and leaves every other record line byte-identical. It logs one `tests-frozen-rerecord-approved` event per case, with `case`, `file` and `approval` (the decision as given, required and non-empty). Exit 0 re-recorded; 2 no case named, no or empty approval, a named case no earlier record holds or absent at `HEAD`, or a bare case name matching more than one recorded case (each named on stderr, with every match of the ambiguous name; nothing written, no event), or a record that cannot be written (every record it had rewritten restored byte-identical). `rerecord-rebased <ledger> <pre-rebase-sha>`: for every ticked earlier step's recorded case whose body at `HEAD` differs from its record, it re-records the case at its `HEAD` body when its body at `<pre-rebase-sha>` matches the record and its body at `HEAD` equals its body on `origin/<default>`, and otherwise leaves that record untouched and names the case; a case recorded by several earlier steps is judged by the latest owning step's record only, the way `check-earlier` judges it, so an older owner's superseded record is neither refused, re-recorded nor named; a recorded case absent both at `HEAD` and at `<pre-rebase-sha>` (a case the branch never committed, its work discarded by a bail) is dropped from the record together with its `helpers:` line, and a case present at `<pre-rebase-sha>` but missing at `HEAD` exits 2. Exit 0 every stale record re-recorded; 1 at least one stale case refused, each named on stdout as `<case> (<owning step>)`; 2 no answer (bad arguments, an unreadable ledger or record, a failing `git show`, a record that cannot be written); 3 no earlier record is stale, or the ledger file does not exist (a ledger that exists but cannot be read stays exit 2).
