For the other verbs, exit 2 = no answer (bad arguments, record missing or malformed; for `rerecord-earlier` also a ledger no commit touches, a failing `git show` of a recorded file, or a record that cannot be written); `check-earlier` also exits 2, writing no pass marker and naming both files, when the run's issue is known (`VERI_ISSUE`, else a `tdd/<n>-` branch) and the ledger it is given is not that run's `.tdd/issue-<n>.md`; `rerecord-earlier` exits 0, the record untouched, when every earlier case is unchanged between the base and HEAD. A Go case's helpers are read from sibling `_test.go` files in the same directory (a Python or TypeScript case's never are). A case's helpers are collected transitively within those files (#4642): the helpers it calls, then the helpers those call, each once by name (a self-recursive helper or two helpers calling each other end the walk), so a refactor that moves a direct helper call into a new helper that calls it keeps the helper's lines. A record whose `helpers:` digest was computed by the earlier non-transitive collection still passes `check-earlier` and is neither re-recorded nor named, and `rerecord-refactored` accepts it as green's; a digest matching no collection still exits 1.
- `$S/transform-touches-tests.sh <base-sha> [<bound>] [--touched <list>]` — reject a refactor-kind transform whose commits (`<base-sha>..HEAD`) change a test file, or a file outside `<bound>`. `--touched <list>` takes a comma-separated file list: the range must have changed a listed file. `<bound>` is optional, so `<base-sha> --touched <list>` works with no bound. With `--touched`, **exit 0** when the range changed a listed file; **exit 1**, printing `transform changed no file the issue touched`, when it changed none; **exit 2** when the list is empty or `--touched` has no value, with the usage line on stderr.
- `$S/scope-check.sh <ledger> <repo-root>` — the check the conductor runs before the plan commit. Exit 0 = every named test module is existing or free; exit 1 = a new `test_<x>.py` names a basename already under `tests/py` (stderr names both paths), or a step line carries more than one `⟦criterion k⟧` tag (stderr names the step), or a step not marked `⟦skill-edit⟧` names, ahead of a `⟦skill-edit⟧` step whose unit is U, the ID of a row listed under `## U` in the `REQUIREMENTS.md` beside U's file, as a whole word, while U's `GRADED` file signs U, or U's file path while that file is not yet in the repository (stderr names the step and the unit; no `plan` event is written); exit 2 = bad arguments, or a `⟦skill-edit⟧` step's unit (its first path-shaped backticked token) has no `### <unit>` or `### <unit> (new)` heading under the `## Requirements` of the spec beside the ledger (stderr names the unit; no `plan` event is written), or a `⟦skill-edit⟧` step carries no path-shaped backticked token at all (stderr: `no path-shaped unit for skill-edit step: <step>`; no `plan` event is written).
- `$S/classify-bail.sh <cause>` — deterministically classify a bail cause
  (`killed-stuck`/`no-verdict` → `system`, `revise-exhausted`/`missing-reroutes-exhausted`/`repair-reroutes-exhausted` → `process`; unknown → exit
  non-zero). Used in the bail step in `<base directory>/BAIL.md` so the classification is consistent, not ad-hoc.
- `$S/bail.sh <n> <cause> <phase> --note-file <path> [--attempt <k>]` — `BAIL.md` sub-steps 1–7 in one call, with sub-step 8's report as its last lines. Every call prints one line with its exit code. A call that fails (a snapshot, the reset, the bail event, the metrics render or its commit, the push, the skill-drift read or a comment), or a note path it cannot append to, is named in the output and gone past, never a refusal. **Exit 0** the bail done, a failed call included; **exit 2** bad arguments, before any call, or an unknown cause, after `classify-bail.sh` alone and before any reset or event.
- `$S/stop-requested.sh [--clear]` — **has an operator asked this run to stop, and how?**
  Run from the worktree, it reads `.tdd/stop`. **Exit 0** = a stop was requested: the mode —
  `at-ledger`, `at-leaf` or `now` — is printed on stdout and nothing else, so capture it and
  enter *Stopping* (`<base directory>/STOPPING.md`) with it. **Exit 3** = no request, nothing on stdout; carry on. **Exit 2** =
  the file's first line is not one of the three modes, or an argument the script does not
  take: nothing on stdout, the complaint on stderr, `.tdd/stop` left as it was. `--clear`
  removes `.tdd/stop` and exits 0 whether or not it was there — the last act of *Stopping*
  (`STOPPING.md`).
- `$S/refactor-budget.sh [--issue] <count>` — **bound the refactor loop**, so a step costs the same on
  every run. Given the number of transforms applied so far this step, it decides whether
  another one may run: **exit 0** = still under budget (launch the next transform leaf),
  **exit 3** = budget spent (stop the loop, go to verify-refactor), **exit 2** =
  `VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET` is not a whole number ≥ 0 — a misconfigured run, with a diagnostic
  on stderr and **nothing on stdout**, fail-closed the way `classify-bail.sh` is on an
  unrecognised cause. A one-line status goes to stdout on 0 and 3 alike (`refactor 5/5 —
  budget spent`), so your narration can mirror it. `VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET` (default 5) is a
  **third** tunable on a **third axis**: `VERI_STUCK_RETRIES` is liveness (the leaf never
  answered), `REVISE_BUDGET` is verdict (it answered and was rejected), this one is **cost
  per step** — how much tidying one step is worth, decided once for the run instead of by
  your judgement mid-loop.
- `$S/event.sh <event> [key=value …]` — append one JSON line to the run's event log
  `.tdd/events.jsonl`, relative to the worktree you are in. `supervise.sh` and `verify.sh`
  call it from inside themselves, so their events are complete by construction; **your**
  marks — `step-start`, `step-done`, `pin`, `revise`, `bail`, `done`, `resume` — are prose,
  so each is prescribed below at the point the matching narration is, and the run's context
  (`VERI_ISSUE`, else `VERI_STATE_LABEL_ISSUE`, `VERI_ACTOR=conductor`, `VERI_STEP`, `VERI_PHASE`, `VERI_ATTEMPT`) rides on
  the call in the environment. The log is run state: it is ignored, never committed, and
  goes with the worktree. With `VERI_STATE_LABEL_ISSUE=<n>` set, a `stop` event adds the `stopped`
  label (a `bail`, `bailed`) when the repository has it, never creating it, so every state a run can be in reads off the issue's labels, and `start` and `resume` remove
  `stopped` as they remove `bailed`. `resume` also re-claims a released
  issue: it adds `taken:<lane>` (the text after the last `:` of `VERI_LABEL`, `main` without one) and
  removes the opt-in label, so dispatch does not relaunch it.
  A short write of a line to either the event log or the journal exits **2** with `event: short write to journal <path>` on stderr, `<path>` naming the file written.
  After an `exit`, `step-done`, `bail`, `done` or `stage-end` line reaches an issue's journal it also rewrites that issue's metrics
  file beside it (`PUBLISHED.md`); a failure there is one stderr line and never changes its exit code.
  **Strict mode** (`VERI_EVENT_STRICT=1`): an event name `veri/event_table.py` does not hold, a key it does not declare
  for that event, or a required key that is missing exits **2** and writes nothing to either file, stderr naming the
  event and the key. So does a value that does not match the format the table declares for its key, or an empty value
  where the table does not allow one, stderr naming the event, the key and that declared format. The test suite sets it. A run never checks: `run-conductor.sh` drops it on start, so neither its
  own marks nor the conductor and anything under it see it, and an exit 2 naming the table is never a run's. A script
  a person runs by hand outside a run checks only if they export this test-only variable. Without it the script
  writes the line as before.
- `$S/metrics.sh <n> [--json]` — the event log's **reader**: prints on stdout one markdown
  `### 📊 Metrics` report of what solving issue `<n>` cost — attempts, verdicts, kills,
  retries and budgets spent, per phase and per step. A non-empty pinned `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify` adds the line
  `⚠️ Unverified phases: <list>`, and any `reason=switched-off` skips (apart from `nothing-landed` ones) add
  `Verifiers switched off: <phase> <n>[, <phase> <n>…]`, one pair per phase with at least one such skip
  (`verify-diff-skipped` counts as `diff`, `verify-green-skipped` as `green`, `verify-pin-skipped` as `pin`, `verify-red-skipped` as `red`, `verify-refactor-skipped` as `refactor`),
  comma-separated, the phases in alphabetical order. Everything it knows comes from
  `.tdd/events.jsonl` in the worktree you are in, so it is called there and at the end, once
  the marks it reads have all been written. The conductor both commits its output beside the
  ledger and pastes it into the issue thread (the *Draft PR* step, and `<base directory>/BAIL.md`): a run that
  never calls it leaves the figures computable and uncomputed, which from the issue thread is
  indistinguishable from not having them. When the local `.tdd/events.jsonl` holds no event of issue `<n>` (an event
  naming no issue counts as the local run's own), it reports instead from the events in origin's `refs/veri/runs/<n>`
  (fetched into the clone under that ref, the fetch ended after `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT` seconds and then read as no
  answer in one stderr line), so a run made on another machine is reported here too.
  Right after its `#### By phase` table the report holds a `#### REJECT outcomes` table, one row per phase with a
  verifier REJECT, giving its REJECT count and how many of those REJECTs were caught, flipped, overturned, unresolved,
  pending and untracked, the figures of that phase's `rejects` in `--json`; a run with no REJECT has no such table.
  It holds a `#### Stages` table, one row per stage the run marked, whatever its name, giving its runs, its ends by
  exit code (ok, failed, no answer, nothing), its unended starts and its wall seconds, the figures of that stage's
  entry in `--json`'s `stages`; a run with no stage marked has no such table.
  It holds a `#### Conductor by phase` table whose heading says its figures are estimated (each turn is placed by its
  timestamp), one row per entry of `--json`'s `conductor.by_phase` giving the conductor's turns and cost there,
  `unknown` for a cost that cannot be priced; when no conductor log was read (`by_phase` is `null`), or the log holds
  no turn, there is no such table.
  `$S/metrics.sh <n> --json` prints, in place of the markdown report, one JSON object holding the same figures under
  the top-level keys `run`, `phases`, `steps`, `conductor` and `total`, `leaves`, the phase figures split by
  leaf kind, `runs`, one entry per run of the issue, and `stages`, the figures of each stage the journal marks.
  The markdown report, and only it, reads a standing `metrics-cut` line (one no later `done`, `bail`, `stop`,
  `preflight-refused`, `resume` or `step-added` line follows) as the run's `done` for its Outcome and wall time, so the
  report committed before the PR is readied (`DRAFT-PR.md` sub-step 2) ends at that line; `--json` reads `done` alone. Every figure in it is a number, or `null` when
  it is not known, never a zero standing for unknown; the object holds counts and sums, and no rate. Any other argument
  after the issue number is exit 2, the usage line on stderr and nothing on stdout.
- `$S/over-budget.sh <tier> <seconds> <usual>` — say whether one recorded test-command run
  was slow enough to be worth a word: **exit 0** = over budget, **exit 1** = not, **exit 2** =
  `VERI_UNIT_BUDGET` or `VERI_TIME_WARN_RATIO` cannot be read (the same misconfiguration
  step 5's `time-budget.sh` preflight refuses). On exit 0 it prints **exactly one line**,
  carrying a ⏱; on exit 1 it prints nothing at all. **Paste that ⏱ line verbatim into that
  phase's narration** in the issue thread, and do nothing else with it: **nothing branches on
  this check** — not the phase's verdict, not a retry, not a bail. A slow run is a remark, and
  a run that is not slow leaves you an empty capture and therefore nothing to paste.
- `$S/capture-spec.sh <n>` — the **blind spec**, on stdout: the issue's body, plus a `## Bug`
  section when the issue carries the `bug` label (and nothing extra when it does not). That
  section names the body's `What happened`, `Expected`, `Steps to reproduce` and `Environment`
  headings rather than copying their text, so each heading's text appears once in the spec,
  in the body. Redirect it into
  `.tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md` (step 3). Exit 2 on a missing issue number.
- `$S/refresh-spec.sh <n> <spec-file>` — re-capture the blind spec as `capture-spec.sh <n>`
  would and compare it to `<spec-file>`. **Exit 3** identical, file untouched. **Exit 0**
  rewritten to the live capture. **Exit 1** the live `## Acceptance criteria` checkbox count
  differs from the file's, file untouched, both counts on stderr. **Exit 2** bad arguments or
  no answer from `gh`, file untouched. The conductor calls it.
- `$S/resume-clean.sh` — a resumed run's first act: discard what a killed leaf left uncommitted
  outside `.tdd/`, printing each discarded change as its `git status --porcelain` line. Exit 0.
- `$S/ensure-run-state-ignored.sh [repo-root]` — make the **target repo** ignore veri's run
  scratch (`.tdd/*.log`, `.rc`, `.prompt`, `.reason`, the blind spec) while keeping the ledger
  tracked. Writes the rule to **both** the worktree's `.gitignore` (committed, so the first
  `git add -A` never sweeps leaf logs into history) and the shared
  `$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)/info/exclude` (never committed, read by every worktree,
  so the caller's checkout — still on the default branch — stops reporting `.tdd/` as
  untracked). Idempotent in both files; the word on stdout reports the `.gitignore` only,
  `added` when it wrote the rule there, `already present` when it was there — the exclude
  file is ensured either way. Run once per issue right after entering the worktree (step 1).
- `$S/issue-worktree.sh [--take-over] <n> <slug> | --remove <n>` — the **linked worktree the issue branch lives in**.
  Creates (or hands back) `<repo-root>/.tdd/wt/<n>` on branch `tdd/<n>-<slug>`, cut from
  **`origin/<default>` as just fetched** — never from the caller's `HEAD`, and never from the
  **local** branch of that name: that one **lags the remote by design**, since a run executes the skill pinned from
  `origin/<default>` and nothing obliges anyone to pull the primary clone, so a branch cut
  from it starts **stale** and its PR then owes a rebase over everything that landed meanwhile.
  The fetch moves no local branch and touches no working tree. `<default>`'s **name** is read
  from `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`; when that ref is absent (an origin does not guarantee one) it
  is resolved from the remote itself with `git ls-remote --symref origin HEAD`, and when the
  remote names none either it exits 2 naming `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` — never a guessed
  `main`. A local `main`/`master` is the fallback only when there is **no origin remote** at all:
  then it supplies both the name and the **base** itself, as before. Prints the worktree's **absolute path on stdout** — the only
  thing on stdout; git's chatter goes to stderr, so the path is safe to capture.
  **Idempotent**: a second call for the same issue prints the same path and creates nothing,
  which is what makes a resume land in the worktree the first run made — a **local** issue
  branch is checked out as it is, with no fetch. Only a **local** branch or a **registered
  worktree** makes a run a resume. A branch **only origin** holds is refused (exit 2) unless
  the run is a resume, even when this clone has fetched it (a remote-tracking ref is not a
  resume) — with one exception, the **own lane**: when the issue carries this lane's `taken:<lane>`
  label (the text after the last `:` of `VERI_LABEL`, `main` without one), the run is a resume and the
  worktree is recreated from origin's branch tip, ledger included; another lane's claim is still
  refused. With no exact `tdd/<n>-<slug>` branch, the issue's **one** `tdd/<n>-*` branch is reused
  **whatever slug is passed**: a local one, or an origin one this lane holds (`taken:<lane>`). **Two or
  more** such branches, local or on origin, exit 2 with stderr naming each one and the requested
  branch. A refused one creates no branch and no worktree, and names the takeover — `issue-worktree.sh --take-over <n> <slug>`.
  **`--take-over`** adopts that run in one command, whether or not this clone has fetched it: it asks origin which
  `tdd/<n>-*` branch it holds (`git ls-remote`; the requested one, else origin's only one, and exit 2 naming them when
  origin holds none or several), fetches it into `refs/remotes/origin/<branch>`, and cuts the worktree on a local branch
  from that tip as just fetched, printing its path. A local branch of that name is used only when it is at origin's tip;
  one that differs exits 2 naming both shas and the way out (`git branch -f <branch> origin/<branch>` to take origin's,
  or push the local one first). Each of the two network calls ends after `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT` seconds, and one
  that gets no answer exits 2 naming it, with no branch and no worktree made. Without the flag the refusal stands, exit 2. A **stale**
  remote-tracking ref, whose branch origin no longer holds, is not a claim: the run cuts fresh
  from `origin/<default>`, as above. **Exit 0** = the path. **Exit 2** = no answer
  could be obtained — either the branch is **already checked out in another worktree** (it names
  that path and touches nothing: **never steal a checkout**, another conductor owns that run),
  or the default branch cannot be named, or fetching it failed — never a silent fallback to the
  stale local ref. And exit 2 when the worktree it would have handed back **holds a live
  conductor**: the pid in `.tdd/conductor.pid` there still answers `kill -0` **and** that
  process's start time matches the one recorded on the lock's second line, so it
  names that pid instead of printing the path (#170) — a second conductor `cd`'ing into a live
  run's worktree reads that run's ledger as its own and `RESUME.md` throws the live leaf's
  uncommitted work away. A lock whose pid is **dead**, or answers but with a start time other than the
  recorded one (a **reused** pid), is a bailed or killed run, still a resume, still the path.
  `$S/issue-worktree.sh --remove <n>` removes the worktree once the PR has landed, and
  **refuses** (exit 2) one holding uncommitted changes. Once the worktree is removed it also deletes the run's pinned-scripts snapshot, the path recorded in
  `.tdd/pinned-scripts/.snapshot-dir`, only when that path is a directory (not a symlink) named `veri-snapshot-*` whose
  `.veri-snapshot` marker names this worktree; any other recorded path that exists is left in place with one stderr line
  naming it, and the exit code is the worktree removal's own, except that a snapshot it fails to remove is exit 2 with one stderr line naming that snapshot, the worktree already gone.
  **Where `stack-branch.sh` exits 0** (the repository's `CLAUDE.md` names a stack branch `<stack>`), a new run's branch
  is cut from **`origin/<stack>` as just fetched** in place of `origin/<default>`. When origin has no such branch, it is
  first pushed there at the default branch's tip as just fetched, **never forced**: a refused or unanswered push is
  **exit 2** with no branch and no worktree made, except that a push refused because the branch now exists on origin
  (another machine created it) is followed by a fetch and the cut from `origin/<stack>`. Each of those network calls
  ends after `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT` seconds; one that gets no answer exits 2 naming it. A `stack-branch.sh` exit 2
  (a name that cannot be a stack) is exit 2 here too; where it exits 3 the cut is as above and nothing is pushed. A
  stacked cut records the base it was cut from in the run's own state, `.tdd/issue-<n>-base` in the new worktree,
  written once and never rewritten, holding the stack's name; beside it `.tdd/issue-<n>-base-sha` holds
  `git rev-parse origin/<stack>` as read for the cut (a read that gets no answer is **exit 2** with no branch and no
  worktree made), the sha a join's `git rebase --onto` starts from. Where `stack-branch.sh` exits 3 neither file is
  written.
  There an open issue named by `Closes #<n>` in a pull request merged into the stack branch counts as claimed, as an
  open PR saying it does: its run has joined the stack, so a fresh cut is refused, **exit 2**, though no pull request
  for it is open (a lookup that gets no answer is exit 2 too).
  A fresh cut is refused as claimed (`… claims the issue (Closes #<n>)`, **exit 2**) only for an open PR saying
  `Closes #<n>` whose head is in this repository; a PR from a fork, open or merged into the stack, does not stop the run.
- `$S/rebase-onto-default.sh [--graded-only] | --resolved <onto-sha>` — rebase the run's branch, from the worktree it runs in, onto
  `origin/<default>` as just fetched and checked against what origin reports; `--graded-only` resolves a conflict
  confined to `GRADED` files by keeping each side's re-signed units. **Where the worktree holds `.tdd/issue-<n>-base`**,
  the base `issue-worktree.sh` recorded when it cut the run from a stack, **and it names the stack `stack-branch.sh`
  prints**, it rebases onto `origin/<stack>` in place of `origin/<default>`, with and without `--graded-only`, its exit
  **3** then meaning the branch already contains `origin/<stack>`. Any other branch, a hand-made one in a stacking
  repository included, is rebased onto `origin/<default>` exactly as where no stack is named; a record that is garbled,
  one of several, or names another branch than the named stack is exit **2** naming both. On a run's branch
  (`tdd/<n>-<slug>`) an exit 0 or 3 first re-records `.tdd/issue-<n>-branch-base` in the main working tree as the onto
  sha (on exit 3 the one HEAD already contains), so a resume reads the new base and a write that failed after an earlier
  rebase is redone; a record it cannot write is exit **2** naming it, the rebase kept.
  `$S/rebase-onto-default.sh --resolved <onto-sha>` fetches and rebases nothing: run after a `develop-rebase` leaf
  resolved a conflicted rebase onto `<onto-sha>`, it re-records the run's branch base as `<onto-sha>` the same way,
  **exit 0**, stdout the sha the record held before (nothing when there was none), so the reset path can put it back
  with `--resolved <that-sha>`; it writes no stage line. A missing argument, one that is not a hex commit name, one HEAD
  does not contain, or a record it cannot write is exit **2**, the record untouched.
  **Exit 0** rebased, stdout the pre-rebase sha then the onto sha; **1** a
  conflict, aborted, stdout the conflicting files; **2** git refused the rebase, no base branch resolved (a
  `stack-branch.sh` exit 2 included), origin not fetched, or the remote-tracking ref stale; **3** the branch already
  contains the base, nothing touched. It marks its run as stage `rebase`: a `stage-start` before it and a `stage-end`
  after it whose `status` is its exit code, both carrying the run's issue (`VERI_ISSUE`, else `VERI_STATE_LABEL_ISSUE`,
  which the conductor exports); run where there is no `.tdd/`, it is not marked, and a line it cannot write never
  changes its exit code.
- `$S/stack-branch.sh [repo-root]` — print the stack branch the target repository names (repo-root defaults to the
  current repository): the branch a line `veri: stack branch: <name>` in its `CLAUDE.md` names, **exit 0**. Only a line
  that starts with that marker, outside a code fence, counts; a mention mid-sentence, indented or under a `>` quote
  names nothing. With no such line, or no `CLAUDE.md`, it prints nothing, **exit 3**: the repository stacks nothing,
  and keeps one pull request per run against the default branch, a CI wait and a merge call. **Exit 2**, stderr naming
  the line, when the name is empty, is a `<placeholder>`, starts `refs/` or `origin/`, is the default branch compared
  case-folded (a stack there would be a direct push to it) as origin states it (`git ls-remote --symref origin HEAD`, bounded by `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT`) or as
  `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` records it, or is one `git check-ref-format --branch` refuses; when neither tells the
  default branch; and when two lines name a stack, naming both.
- `$S/stack-pr.sh` — run in the target repository's clone, no arguments: prints the url of the one open pull request
  from the stack branch to the default branch and exits **0**; one whose head is a fork's branch of that name (or a
  deleted fork's) is not it. Read after a fetch of origin; when the stack is ahead
  of the default branch and none is open it opens one, ready and not a draft, and prints that one's url. This pull
  request is what makes CI run on each push to the stack, and it must not read as a claim on any issue or as a
  landing: its body says it is never merged itself and that slices are cut from it, and it carries no `Closes`,
  `Fixes` or `Resolves` line. An open one that is a draft (or does not say) is exit **2**, one stderr line naming it
  and saying it must be marked ready, since CI runs on no push to a draft; the script never changes a pull request's
  state itself. **Exit 3**, nothing opened, when the stack is not ahead of the default branch or origin holds no stack.
  **Exit 2**, nothing opened, when GitHub gives no answer (a read or the open that fails, or an answer that is not a
  list of pull requests naming their urls), when two or more open pull requests track the stack (naming them), or a
  fetch of origin gets no answer. When `stack-branch.sh` does not exit 0, it exits with the code `stack-branch.sh`
  exited with (3 stays 3, 2 stays 2), opening nothing. The join of `queued-merge.sh` calls it.
- `$S/stack-green.sh` — run in the target repository's clone, no arguments: prints the sha of the newest commit in
  `origin/<default>..origin/<stack>`, read after a fetch of origin, that is an issue end and is tested, and exits
  **0**: where a slice of the stack may be cut. A commit is an *issue end* when no issue number named in a commit
  subject at or before it on the stack is named in a subject after it; a commit whose subject names no issue belongs
  to the issue of the nearest earlier commit that names one. A subject names every `#<n>` inside the parenthesised group or
  groups it ends with (`(#1)`, `(#1, #3)`, `(#1) (#2)`, `(#1 and #3)`); a `#<n>` elsewhere in it names nothing, so a revert subject, ending in its closing quote, belongs to the issue
  before it. Read from subjects, the rule survives a landing that rewrites the stack's shas. *Tested* means GitHub's
  check runs for the commit, read by sha (the latest attempt of each, every page), are all completed, each
  concluding success, neutral or skipped, with at least one success; any other conclusion, a check run still in
  progress, or no check run at all is not tested. The entry names no CI job: every check run counts. Issue ends are
  read newest first, one read each, the newest 20 at most. **Exit 3**, one stderr line, when no commit qualifies (the
  line says how many older issue ends were not read, when there are more than 20). **Exit 2** when GitHub gives no
  answer (a failed read, or an answer not of the check-runs shape), when `stack-branch.sh` does not exit 0, 3
  included, or when a fetch of origin gets no answer.
- `$S/stack-red.sh` — run in the target repository's clone, no arguments: says whether CI's latest answer on the
  stack is red, which freezes joins. It reads the runs of every workflow on the branch, not one named workflow: the
  newest 100 workflow runs GitHub lists for the stack branch (a run whose head is a fork's branch of that name is not
  counted). Each workflow (`workflow_id`) is decided alone. A run answers with its conclusion when it completed and
  was not cancelled; a run at an attempt above 1 that is not completed (a re-run) or completed `cancelled` answers from
  its earlier attempts, read from GitHub and walked from attempt n-1 down to 1, the first completed and not cancelled
  answering, so re-running or cancelling a red run does not lift the freeze; an earlier attempt that cannot be read,
  or reads not completed, is no answer. A workflow's answer is its newest run that has one, except that while its
  newest run without one is still going (queued or in progress), the newest answer on that run's own head commit wins
  when there is one. When any workflow's answer is not `success`, `neutral` or `skipped`, it prints one line,
  `<head-sha> <run-url> <conclusion>` of the newest such run, and exits **0**: the stack is frozen. Otherwise **exit
  2** when any workflow gave no answer. **Exit 3**, nothing printed, when every workflow that answered is green, or
  when none has answered yet (nothing completed with a conclusion other than `cancelled`); one stderr line says which
  of the two. **Exit 2** also when GitHub gives no answer or an answer not of the workflow-runs shape, when a fetch of
  origin gets no answer, or when `stack-branch.sh` does not exit 0, 3 included.
  **During an outage**, while `.tdd/github-outage` exists in the main working tree (found from its `.git` files, with
  no process), it makes no GitHub call and no fetch: the freeze is the file `.tdd/stack-local-red`, which a red offline
  gate over a skipped join writes (`queued-merge.sh`). When that file exists it prints one line, `<sha> <path> local`,
  `<sha>` the file's first line and `<path>` the file, and exits **0**; when it does not, **exit 3**, nothing printed and
  one stderr line. **Exit 2** as online when no stack is named (the default branch read from `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`
  as last fetched), and when the file cannot be read or its first line is not a commit sha.
- `$S/stack-culprit.sh <red-sha>` — run in the target repository's clone: names the issue whose end turned the stack
  red, and the cases it broke. It reads the red cases from the failed-job log (`gh run view <run-id> --log-failed`) of
  the red workflow run on `<red-sha>` (each workflow's answer on that head read as `stack-red.sh` reads one, the
  newest red one taken, at the attempt that answered), naming them by `rerun-alone.sh`'s rule from the runner's text as `ci-flake.sh` reads the log, and runs at most
  the first 5, saying on stderr how many it did not run. The commits it tests are the *issue ends* from the newest
  commit at or below `<red-sha>` that `stack-green.sh`'s rule calls tested (a KT5 issue end whose check runs passed,
  the newest 20 such ends read) to `<red-sha>`, `<red-sha>` included, `origin/<default>` standing for the start when
  none is tested; here an issue end is each commit whose next commit toward the tip belongs to another issue by
  subject (a subject names the issues of the parenthesised group it ends with; one naming none belongs to the nearest
  earlier issue). At those commits, oldest first, it runs each case alone through the repository's
  `veri: one test file:` command in a detached worktree of its own under the tree's `.tdd/`, never in a run's worktree
  or the main clone, and removes that worktree on every exit (a removal that fails is said on stderr, naming its
  path, the exit unchanged); a worktree it cannot add is exit **2** with nothing run. It tests at most 20 issue ends:
  with more untested ends than that it exits **2** saying so, running nothing. A case is **red** at a commit when its
  alone run exits non-zero and its output carries a failure line naming that case (`FAILED <case>`, read as
  `ci-flake.sh` reads one); it is **absent**, not red, when its file is missing there or the output says the case was
  not found or not collected; an exit 0 is not red; any other non-zero ending, a kill or no exit within
  `VERI_TIMEOUT` is no answer. The exit code alone never decides: `make test TESTS="$1"` exits 2 for a failing case
  and a missing one alike. A case absent at `<red-sha>` itself is exit **2**: the CI id and the local command
  disagree. A `FAILED` line for an id that only begins with the case's id is not that case's. At the first issue end where a case is red it prints, one per line, `red: <red-sha>` (the full sha the
  search was run for, so a file holding the answer is told apart from one recorded for another red), `issue: <n>`
  (each number, ascending and space-separated, when the subject names several; `none` before any subject names one),
  `commit: <sha>`, `before: <sha>` (the issue end before it, or `origin/<default>`), then one
  `case: <case><TAB><first FAIL line>` per red case, its local paths written `<worktree>` and `~`, and exits **0**.
  **Exit 3**, nothing printed, when no issue end turns a case red, `<red-sha>` included, its stderr naming the cases
  run. **Exit 2** when the log cannot be read or names no case, when `<red-sha>` is not a full sha of a commit of
  `origin/<default>..origin/<stack>` or has no red run, when an alone run gives no answer, when more than 20 issue
  ends are untested, when GitHub or a fetch gives no answer, when the repository declares no
  `veri: one test file:` command, or when `stack-branch.sh` does not exit 0. On exit 0 or 3 it adds one comment to the
  stack's tracking pull request holding what it printed (or, on 3, the cases run) and the red run's url, and adds none
  when a trusted comment there (an owner's, a collaborator's or the `gh` user's) already holds the same text; a
  stranger's copy never stops it. With no one tracking pull request open it opens none, posts
  nothing, says so on stderr and keeps its exit.
- `$S/stack-fix.sh <n> <red-sha> <culprit-file>` — run in a *fix worktree* (a detached worktree under the target
  repository's `.tdd/`, cut from `origin/<stack>`'s tip) with `<culprit-file>` holding `stack-culprit.sh`'s output for
  that red: the one way a reviewed hand fix reaches a frozen stack. It takes the merge lock, runs `stack-red.sh`,
  fetches, and pushes the worktree's `HEAD` to the stack only when every check holds:
  - `<red-sha>` is the commit `stack-red.sh` prints now (another commit is **exit 1**, naming the one red now);
  - `<culprit-file>`'s `red:` line is `<red-sha>` and its `issue:` line names `<n>` (else **exit 1**; a file without
    its `red:`, `issue:` and `case:` lines, or one it cannot read, is **exit 2**);
  - one fix per culprit: no commit of `origin/<default>..origin/<stack>` already carries a `Stack-fix:` trailer and a
    subject naming `<n>` by `stack-green.sh`'s rule (else **exit 1**);
  - `origin/<stack>`, as fetched, is an ancestor of `HEAD`, `HEAD` adds at least one commit, and every commit of
    `origin/<stack>..HEAD` ends its subject `(#<n>)` and carries the trailer `Stack-fix: <red-sha>` (else **exit 1**);
  - no *test file* is added, changed, deleted or renamed in `git diff --name-status origin/<stack>..HEAD` (either side
    of a rename): the file of a case `<culprit-file>` names, or a path with a directory named `test`, `tests`, `spec`
    or `__tests__`, or a file name that starts `test_` or holds `_test.`, `.test.` or `.spec.` (else **exit 1** naming
    it);
  - the worktree has no uncommitted change (else **exit 1**: a run there would not test `HEAD`);
  - at `HEAD`, each named case passes run alone through the repository's `veri: one test file:` command
    (`detect-test-command.sh --tier "one test file"`): a case whose run exits 1, 4 or 5, or exits otherwise with a
    failure line naming it (read as `stack-culprit.sh` reads one), or whose file is absent, or whose output says it
    was not found or not collected whatever the exit, is **exit 1** naming it; any other exit, no exit within
    `VERI_TIMEOUT`, or no such command declared is **exit 2**. The exit code alone never passes a case;
  - at `HEAD`, the fast tier (`detect-test-command.sh --tier fast`) exits 0 (non-zero is **exit 1**; no command found
    or no exit within `VERI_TIMEOUT`, **exit 2**) and `prose-owed.sh` exits 0 (exit 1, a unit owing a grade, is
    **exit 1**; any other, **exit 2**); a run that moved `HEAD` or left the worktree changed is **exit 2**.

  The push is a compare-and-swap leased to the fetched tip (`--force-with-lease=refs/heads/<stack>:<fetched sha>`), a
  fast-forward since `HEAD` descends from it, never a bare force and never the default branch. A push that does not
  answer 0 is read back with a fetch: origin's stack at `HEAD` is the push landed; moved to another commit is **exit
  1**, nothing pushed; still the fetched tip, or a fetch with no answer, **exit 2**. On success it prints the pushed
  sha and exits **0**, then adds one comment to the stack's tracking pull request (`stack-pr.sh`'s) and one to issue
  `<n>`, each naming `<red-sha>`, the pushed sha and the cases; a tracking pull request it cannot tell (none open, or
  several) or a comment that fails is said on stderr and leaves the exit 0, since the push landed. **Exit 3**, nothing
  pushed, when `stack-red.sh` exits 3; **exit 2** on no answer from `stack-red.sh` (any other non-zero exit), the
  merge lock, git or GitHub, when the repository names no stack or origin has none, or on bad arguments (`<n>` not an
  issue number, `<red-sha>` not 40 hex). On every exit 1 or 2 nothing is pushed, the merge lock is freed and the fix
  worktree and its commits are kept. It makes no `wait-ci.sh` call and lifts no freeze: CI on the new tip, read by
  `stack-red.sh`, is the fix's gate.
- `$S/stack-revert.sh <n>` — run in the target repository's clone when the one fix attempt failed: under the merge
  lock, after `stack-red.sh` and a fetch, it reverts every commit of `origin/<default>..origin/<stack>` that belongs to
  issue `<n>` by `stack-green.sh`'s subject rule (a subject naming no issue belongs to the nearest earlier one), the
  hand fix's commits included since they end `(#<n>)` too, newest first, as one commit on the stack's tip whose
  subject ends `(#<n>)`, so the revert stays in the culprit's span. It works in a detached worktree of its own under
  the tree's `.tdd/`, removed on every exit (a removal that fails is said on stderr, naming its path), runs the fast
  tier and `prose-owed.sh` there, and pushes the revert as a fast-forward of the fetched tip, leased to it, never
  forced; when origin's stack moved it starts again from the fetch, at most 5 times. No commit below the tip is
  rewritten. It prints the revert's sha and exits **0**. After the push, in the body of each pull request merged into
  the stack from this repository that carries `Closes #<n>`, that text becomes `Reverted from <stack> by <sha>`, so
  `solvable-issues.sh` and `issue-worktree.sh` no longer read the issue as joined; then it adds one comment to issue
  `<n>` and one to the tracking pull request (`stack-pr.sh`'s), each naming the red commit, the revert commit and the
  hand fix's commit (one carrying a `Stack-fix:` trailer among the issue's) when one was pushed. It adds, removes or
  changes no label: the issue returns to selection only by `release-issue.sh <n>`. **Exit 1**, nothing pushed and the
  lock freed, on a revert conflict (a later commit depends on the culprit: a human's call), a red fast tier or a unit
  owing a grade. An issue reverted before (its revert subject is this script's) has only its commits after its last
  revert reverted, so an issue released, re-run and joined again can be reverted again; when the newest commit
  belonging to `<n>` is that revert itself it reverts nothing and exits **3**, but still rewords the `Closes #<n>`
  lines to name that revert (rewording is safe to repeat, so a re-run after a body edit that failed lifts the claim;
  a body it cannot edit then is **exit 2**). **Exit 3**, nothing pushed, also when `stack-red.sh` exits 3 or no commit
  of the stack belongs to `<n>`. **Exit 2** on no answer
  from git, GitHub, `stack-red.sh` or the lock, when origin moved on every attempt, or on bad arguments; and, the
  revert left pushed, when the merged pull requests cannot be read or a body cannot be edited, naming the pull
  request.
- `$S/stack-land.sh [--now]` — run in the target repository's clone: lands a slice of the stack on the default branch.
  A module, `skills/tdd/scripts/veri/stack_land.py`, behind a `/bin/sh` shim. It refuses (**exit 2**) when
  `stack-branch.sh` does not exit 0, and refuses (**exit 1**) a red stack (`stack-red.sh` exit 0). It cuts only at the
  commit `stack-green.sh` prints, never at another. It lands when that cut is `VERI_SLICE_COMMITS` (default 100) or
  more commits ahead of the default branch, or when the stack is idle; it merges with
  `gh pr merge --rebase --match-head-commit` and no bypass flag. `--now` skips the size-or-idle rule and no other.
  After a landing it made, it frees the merge lock, runs `stack-carry.sh <cut>`, then `stack-tag.sh <slice-pr-url>`,
  then reads the slice's `Closes` issues; each runs whatever the one before answered. The carry-over and the tagging
  take the merge lock themselves and run under no bound of `stack-land.sh`'s, since each bounds its own network calls
  and a bound that killed one would leave the lock held. A carry-over exit other than 0 or 3 (3: nothing to carry) names
  the landed slice and `stack-carry.sh <cut>` as the command owed, the stack left as it was; a tagging exit other than 0
  or 3 names `stack-tag.sh <slice-pr-url>`; a `Closes` issue still open is named, and a `Closes` read that gets no
  answer names the issues whose closing is owed a check, the carry-over and the tagging having run. Any of them is
  **exit 2**.
  While `.tdd/github-outage` exists in the main working tree it exits **3** with one stderr line saying a landing
  waits for the GitHub outage to end, before any read and before taking the lock.
  The slice pull request's body names each commit of its range whose message carries a `Veri-Suite: skipped`
  trailer, one line each, and adds one line naming `.tdd/stack-local-red` when that file exists in the main working
  tree, so a reader can tell the joins no full run proved; a range with neither adds nothing.
  **Exit 0** landed, **1** a clean refusal, **2** no answer, **3** nothing to land.
- `$S/stack-recover.sh` — run in the target repository's clone after local mode ends, no arguments: brings the
  outage's offline joins to GitHub. A module, `skills/tdd/scripts/veri/stack_recover.py`, behind a `/bin/sh` shim.
  While `.tdd/github-outage` exists it exits **2** before any read; when `stack-branch.sh` exits 3 it exits **3**.
  It takes the merge lock and fetches; it exits **3** when the local `<stack>` does not exist or is `origin/<stack>`
  or behind it, and **1** naming both shas when `origin/<stack>` is not an ancestor of the local `<stack>` (or is
  gone), nothing pushed; a `Veri-Joined:` trailer that is not a pull request url and a branch is **exit 1** naming its
  commit, nothing pushed. For each commit of `origin/<stack>..<stack>` with a `Veri-Joined: <pr-url> <branch>` trailer, oldest first,
  it pushes that commit to `<branch>` with `--force-with-lease=<branch>:<origin/<branch> as fetched>`, then pushes
  `<stack>` with `--force-with-lease=<stack>:<origin/<stack> as fetched>`, never `--force`; a refused lease is
  **exit 1** naming the branch, and a refused branch push leaves the stack unpushed. It then re-reads each joined
  pull request over the settle window `queued-merge.sh` uses; one not reading merged is **exit 2** naming it, the
  stack left as pushed. It then deletes each joined run's remote branch and `refs/veri/runs/<n>` and runs
  `stack-pr.sh`; a delete that fails is **exit 2** naming it as owed, and an exit of `stack-pr.sh` other than 0 is
  **exit 2** naming it, since no tracking pull request is known to wait for CI on. Every exit frees the merge lock it took. **Exit 0** pushed, printing `<tip-sha> <tracking-pr-url>`: the
  stack's pushed tip and its tracking pull request, as `stack-pr.sh` prints it; **1** a refused lease or a stack
  origin moved; **2** while `.tdd/github-outage` exists or on no answer; **3** nothing joined offline or no stack
  named.
- `$S/stack-carry.sh <old-cut>` — run in the target repository's clone after a slice landed: carries the stack onto
  the new default branch. `<old-cut>` is the stack commit the landed slice ended at, as it was on `origin/<stack>`
  before the landing. A module, `skills/tdd/scripts/veri/stack_carry.py`, behind a `/bin/sh` shim. It takes the merge
  lock, fetches, and reads `<old-cut>` and the old tip, `origin/<stack>` as fetched, kept as the lease value. It refuses
  (**exit 2**) when `stack-branch.sh` does not exit 0, when `<old-cut>` is not an ancestor of the old tip, when
  `origin/<default>` has not landed it (merging it there would change the tree), or when a git read gets no answer;
  it exits **3** when `<old-cut>` is the old tip. It rebases the stack in a detached worktree of its own under `.tdd/`
  with `git rebase --onto origin/<default> <old-cut> <old-tip>`, never checking out a commit in a run's worktree or the
  main clone, and removes that worktree on every exit; a conflict is **exit 1** naming the file, the rebase aborted.
  It pushes only when `git merge-tree --write-tree --merge-base=<old-cut> origin/<default> <old-tip>` exits 0 printing
  the new tip's tree, else **exit 1** naming both trees; it pushes with `--force-with-lease=<stack>:<old-tip>`, never
  plain `--force`, and an origin that moved since the fetch is **exit 1**, nothing lost. It touches no ref but the
  stack branch: no run's branch is rebased, moved or deleted, since each run carries itself at its join. After the
  push it prints the new tip and calls `stack-pr.sh`; an exit of that other than 0 or 3 is **exit 2** naming it as
  owed. Every exit frees the merge lock. **Exit 0** carried, **1** a clean refusal, **2** no answer, **3** nothing to
  carry.
- `$S/stack-tag.sh <slice-pr-url> [--message <n>=<text>]...` — run in the target repository's clone: cuts the tags of a
  landed slice. A module, `skills/tdd/scripts/veri/stack_tag.py`, behind a `/bin/sh` shim; `stack-land.sh` runs it on
  the slice after a landing. The issues are those the subjects of the pull request's commits name, in the order of
  their last commit. An issue owes a tag when any of its commits in the slice is `feat` or `fix`, and none when every
  one is `docs`, `test`, `refactor`, `chore` or `plan`. Under the merge lock it fetches tags, then cuts one annotated
  tag per owing issue on that issue's last commit of the landed range, the next `vN.M` from
  `git tag --sort=-v:refname`, pushing each before the next. The message is the issue's title, or the `--message`
  text given for that issue. An issue whose commit already carries a tag is skipped.
  **Exit 0** every owed tag cut or already there, **1** reserved: no path returns it yet, **2** no answer (a pull request not merged into
  the default branch, a commit of an unknown type or none, a failed push, its local tag then deleted), **3** no issue of
  the slice owes a tag, or every owed tag is already there.
- `$S/detect-test-command.sh [--tier fast|all|"one test file"|"quiet test files"|lint] [--issue <body-file>] [--at <commit>] [repo-root]` — print the one command that
  runs the **target repo's** suite of that tier on stdout; **exit 3** — the repo-wide *nothing to do*
  code — when no marker it trusts matched, which is your cue to ask the user. First match
  wins: for `--tier all` only, the issue body's own `Test command:` line, unless it equals the
  fast tier's command or is CLAUDE.md's `veri: all tests:` command plus a ` TESTS=` selector (#1225); then the repo's CLAUDE.md line for the tier
  (`veri: <tier> tests:`); then every inference (see *Detecting the test command*).
  `--tier fast` never reads the issue's line. `--tier "one test file"` has one source: the repo's
  CLAUDE.md line `veri: one test file: <command>`, the command taking the target (a test file or a pytest node id) as `"$1"`; no
  inference, and **exit 3** when the line is absent. `--tier lint` has one source too: the repo's
  CLAUDE.md line `veri: lint: <command>`, whose command it prints, **exit 0**; with no such line it
  prints nothing and exits **3**, reading no other source (not the issue's line, not a `Makefile` or
  any other inference), so veri never guesses a lint command. `--tier "quiet test files"` prints the repo's CLAUDE.md
  line `veri: quiet test files: <command>`, else a default for a runner it recognises (`pytest -q --no-header -rf` for
  a pytest section in `pytest.ini`, `pyproject.toml` or `setup.cfg`; `npx jest --silent` for a `package.json` whose
  `scripts.test` runs jest), else **exit 3**; it never reads the issue's line. A bad argument is **exit 2**.
  With `--at <commit>`, every file it reads in the repo (`CLAUDE.md` and each file an inference reads) is read as
  `<commit>` holds it, never from the working tree; an `--at` naming no commit git resolves there is **exit 2**.
  Preflight passes the run's recorded branch base (its entry below), so the fast command every leaf and verifier is
  handed (`{{FAST_CMD}}`, `VERI_TEST_CMD`) is never one a branch's `CLAUDE.md` declares (#4263).
- `$S/affected-suites.sh <range>` — print, one per line, sorted and each once, the suites a change affects, so a leaf
  runs a narrow slice instead of the full suite. `<range>` is `<base>..<head>`, or one revision compared with the
  working tree, untracked files included. In veri's own tree (one where `skills/tdd/scripts/tests/py` is a directory
  in `<base>`, the range's base commit, never decided from the working tree) it prints the pytest files veri's path rules place, section by section for `skills/tdd/SKILL.md`. A changed
  `veri/<m>.py` or `tests/leg/<m>.py`, or a `<m>.sh` shim (`-` read as `_`), places `tests/py/test_<m>.py`, `unit/test_<m>.py`
  and `tests/py/test_<m>_process.py` where they exist, and every `tests/py/test_*_process.py` that imports or runs it by
  name (`veri.<m>`, `<m>.py`, `<m>.sh`, or the name of any `*.sh` shim whose `exec` line names `veri/<m>.py`, as `report.sh`
  for `veri/prd_report.py`, each as a whole word); when no process file names it, the process files naming a
  module whose own line imports it (`import <m>`, `from <m> import`), one hop and no further (#4425). Elsewhere it reads
  no declaration: a changed test file (by `case_reader.is_test_path`) that exists selects itself; any other changed
  file selects every test file git does not ignore whose text holds the file's stem (its basename without its last
  extension) as a whole word, `-` and `_` counted alike, the walk skipping `.git`, `.tdd`, `.venv` and `node_modules`;
  a `.tdd/` path selects nothing. It never widens: a changed file no test names adds nothing, named on one stderr line
  `affected-suites: no test names <paths>; no test file covers them, so the full suite at the PR runs them` (veri's own
  tree: `no rule places <paths>; …`, the same tail). **Exit 0** always prints at least one suite; **exit 3** means
  nothing to run (an empty range, run state alone, no test file at all, or no changed file any rule or test places);
  **exit 2** a malformed or unresolvable range, or a failed listing of the tree's files.
  What it prints is a leaf's `changed` run, the only run beyond the fast command: the leaves run it with the repo's
  declared `veri: quiet test files:` command once over every path (`sh -c '<quiet> "$@"' sh <paths>`), else its
  `veri: one test file:` command once per path, or its fast command once when the repo declares none, its all-tier
  command only when it declares neither (`make test TESTS="<those paths>"` in veri's own tree, whatever it declares),
  each through `timed-test-run.sh changed`. `timed-test-run.sh` saves the run's whole output to
  `.tdd/test-runs/<…>.log` under the worktree's toplevel and prints one of three reports, never a runner's raw stream:
  the declared quiet command's output unchanged (its exit 2 followed by the line `timed-test-run.sh: the quiet test
  command broke the run (exit 2): no test answer`); outside veri's own tree, any other `changed` run as one line with
  the tier, exit status and seconds, at most the output's last 20 lines and `full output: <path>`; else a bounded
  report: the passed, failed and error counts, at most 10 failing cases, each as its `FAILED`/`ERROR` line (the case
  and its first failure line) cut to 300 characters, how many more it cut, and the saved file's path. It exits with
  the command's own status. After a `fast` or `all` run whose seconds exceed twice that tier's idle duration, the
  `idle <tier> <seconds>` line of the ceiling record (`VERI_TEST_CEILING_FILE`), it writes a `test-stretch` event
  (tier, seconds, idle) after the run's `test-run` event (PRD 036.10); another tier, a record without the line, or one
  it cannot read writes none, and the warning never changes its exit code.
- `$S/record-test-commands.sh [--one-file <cmd>] [--quiet <cmd>] <fast-cmd> <all-cmd> [repo-root]` — `/vinstall`'s writer of the
  target repo's test commands (default: the current repository): on the default branch's tip as freshly fetched, it
  writes the `veri: fast tests:` and `veri: all tests:` lines of the root `CLAUDE.md` (with `--one-file` the
  `veri: one test file:` line, with `--quiet` the `veri: quiet test files:` line), each replaced where it stands or appended, in one commit on a new branch
  `veri-install`, pushes that branch and opens a pull request from it against the default branch, printing its URL:
  **exit 0**. It never touches the checkout it runs from (branch, index, working tree), never forces a push, never
  pushes the default branch and never merges. **Exit 3** when the tip already declares every given command exactly.
  **Exit 2**, nothing pushed, for a missing or empty command (`--quiet`'s included); a `--one-file` command that does not take the test file
  as `"$1"` (anywhere in it) or as a `$1` word of its own; a `repo-root` outside a git repository; a root `CLAUDE.md`
  on the tip that is not a regular file (a symlink, say: "CLAUDE.md is not a regular file"); a `CLAUDE.md` that
  would not read back, through `detect-test-command.sh`'s own reader, exactly the command given for each tier written
  and unchanged for every other tier; and a `veri-install` created on origin after its check (the push is create-only,
  under a lease expecting the branch absent, so it never moves one). When origin already has `veri-install`, in this
  order: a pull request from it in this repository (a fork's does not count) is open → **exit 3** naming it, whatever
  commit the branch holds; else the branch holds exactly the commit this run would push (same tree, same parent) →
  it opens the pull request, **exit 0**, so a pull request refused after the push is recovered by running it again;
  else **exit 2**, nothing written, saying whether it holds other commands or these commands cut from an older tip of
  the default branch, and what to do: open a pull request from that branch, or delete it, then re-run.
- `$S/worktree-setup.sh <n>` — run from inside a run worktree: runs the `veri: setup: <cmd>` line of its CLAUDE.md (read like `veri: all tests:`) once in the worktree root; exit 2 with no argument. Called by preflight.
- `$S/solvable-issues.sh [--repo <owner/name>] [--not-running]` — the **selection layer**: prints the
  numbers of the issues veri may take right now — open, labelled `ready-for-agent`, every
  issue under their `## Blocked by` heading closed, and not already claimed by an open PR
  saying `Closes #<n>` nor, where `stack-branch.sh` exits 0, by a PR the answer itself reads as merged into the stack branch saying it
  (either claims only when its head is in this repository: a PR from a fork, or one whose head repository cannot be
  read, claims nothing)
  (the issue's run has joined the stack, and the issue stays open and claimed until its slice lands; read from
  `stack-prs.json` under `VERI_GH_JSON`) (a still-draft PR on the issue's own run branch, `tdd/<n>-…`, claims nothing: a
  released issue's resume continues it, ADR-0036; the same PR once readied, not a draft, claims it again, since the run is Done) — one per line, in four bands: the `bug`s labelled `priority` first,
  then the other issues labelled `priority`, then the `bug`s, then the rest, each band ascending by number. A bug labelled `low-risk` counts
  as the rest, and `priority` outranks `low-risk`. An issue also **inherits** the band of every
  open issue that names it under `## Blocked by`, transitively, and takes the better of its own
  band and the inherited one: blocking a `priority` bug puts it in the first band, blocking
  another `priority` issue in the second; blocking a plain `bug` lifts nothing. Each printed
  issue whose band was inherited gets one stderr line
  `#<blocker> inherits <band> from #<dependent>`. The band only reorders that
  set, it never enlarges it — the four conditions run first, so a `priority` issue, or a
  blocker of one, that is blocked, claimed or not opted in is still not printed. `VERI_PRIORITY_LABEL` names the
  `priority` label (default `priority`; set-but-empty is **exit 2**, like `VERI_LABEL`),
  and `bug` is GitHub's own. An issue is solvable only when its author's `author_association` is `OWNER`, `MEMBER`
  or `COLLABORATOR`, or it carries the label `VERI_AUTHOR_LABEL` names (default `author-read`, applied once the issue
  is read; set-but-empty is **exit 2**). The issue listing is read over REST under every `VERI_GH_DRIVER` value, since
  `gh issue list` carries no association; a record that still lacks one is refused as `unknown`. Each opted-in issue
  this author rule alone removes gets one stderr line `solvable-issues: #<n> skipped: its author is <association>, not
  OWNER, MEMBER or COLLABORATOR; label it <VERI_AUTHOR_LABEL> once read`; a refused issue still blocks its dependents
  and lends its band, and with every otherwise-solvable issue refused the script exits 3. **Exit 0** when it printed at least one,
  **exit 3** with empty stdout when none is takeable (nothing to do, not an error).
  `VERI_GH_JSON=<dir>` reads `issues.json` / `prs.json` fixtures instead of shelling out to
  `gh`, which is what makes it testable with no network. `VERI_GH_DRIVER` picks the route
  every GitHub read and write takes, through `veri/github.py`:
  `rest` (the default when unset) runs only `gh api`, `graphql` runs only the `gh issue` /
  `gh pr` command with no fallback, and `auto` tries GraphQL then REST. Set but empty, or any
  other value, is **exit 2**, with a stderr line naming `VERI_GH_DRIVER` and the value, checked
  at call time. Operations with no GraphQL route — issue edit and create, `gh_rest_list`,
  `gh_json` with no gh args, selection's issue listing — use REST under every value, and `VERI_GH_JSON` takes precedence
  over it. A Claude Code cloud session, where GraphQL is refused, needs nothing set: the
  default is already `rest`. The conductor does not call this —
  it is rung 2's, driven by the loop branch of this skill.
- `$S/wait-ci.sh <pr-url> [slice-seconds] [total-seconds] [head-sha] | --fresh-window <head-sha>` — **wait one bounded slice
  for a PR's checks**, then be called again: *4. Draft PR*'s CI wait. The second form, `wait-ci.sh --fresh-window <head-sha>`, removes `.tdd/ci-deadline-<sha>`, `.tdd/ci-queued-<sha>`, `.tdd/ci-none-<sha>` and `.tdd/ci-head-<sha>` for that sha so the next wait records a fresh deadline and a fresh head wait; it reads no checks and exits 0. Reads the check runs and
  commit statuses of the PR's head; given `<head-sha>`, a PR still reporting another head reads
  as pending; a `<head-sha>` that is not a full 40-hex sha exits 2 with one stderr line. `<slice-seconds>` (default and cap 540) bounds this call; `<total-seconds>` (cap
  600) bounds the whole wait, and when it is not given it is twice the mean of the last 5
  completed CI runs, capped at 600; 540 when fewer than 5 exist or `gh` cannot answer; the
  slice length when reading them fails otherwise. The deadline does not run only while every
  pending check is `queued` (none `in_progress`), or the PR reports another head: it is
  recorded in `.tdd/ci-deadline-<sha>`, which later calls inherit, by the first call where that
  is not so — a check in progress, no check yet, or only a commit status pending. The first
  queued sighting is recorded in `.tdd/ci-queued-<sha>`. A draft PR records no deadline. **Exit 0** every check completed
  `SUCCESS`, `NEUTRAL` or `SKIPPED`, not all `SKIPPED`. **Exit 1** a completed check concluded
  anything else, or a commit status is `failure`/`error`; the failed check names go on stdout,
  comma-separated. After a `ci-rerun` event for the head (`ci-flake.sh`'s re-run of its failed jobs), the
  check runs of the re-run's own CI run (`details_url` holding `/actions/runs/<run>/`, `<run>` the event's) that
  completed before the event are not read, so the failed attempt is never the answer; every other run's checks, of
  another workflow or app, are read as ever. While that filter has dropped a check of `<run>` and left none of
  `<run>`, the re-run's new checks are not listed yet: a red among the other checks is still exit 1, and anything
  else reads as pending, never as green or as no checks. **Exit 2** no answer: bad arguments, a read of the checks that fails and is still failing when the deadline passes (retried with backoff until it; a read refused with a rate-limit message is waited out until GitHub's reset when that falls before the deadline and, for every read (the PR's head, check runs or commit status), before the slice end (this call's start plus `<slice-seconds>`); past the slice end that read sleeps only the time left to it and the call exits 3; the call exits 2 at once, naming the rate limit and its reset time, when the reset falls at or past the deadline; a 4xx other than 403 or 429 exits 2 at once), a
  PR with no checks (or only skipped ones) that conflicts with its base branch (stderr says
  `conflicts with its base branch`), a check still pending past the recorded deadline, an
  unreadable deadline file, or checks that never started — still queued more than
  `VERI_CI_QUEUE_WAIT` seconds after the first call that saw them queued, or a ready,
  mergeable PR whose head has stayed none (no check run, no commit status) for
  `VERI_CI_NO_RUN_WAIT` seconds while a `pull_request` workflow exists (stderr names
  `no CI run for <sha>`, the full 40-hex head). That no-run grace is long enough (default
  600 s) for a slow-to-start CI run to appear, so a finished run is not bailed for a late
  one, and a head still without a run after it exits 2 rather than waiting out the whole CI
  budget. That grace is counted across calls: the first
  call that saw a ready, mergeable PR's head with no check run and no commit status records
  the sighting in `.tdd/ci-none-<sha>`, and later calls for the same sha inherit it, so a
  `VERI_CI_NO_RUN_WAIT` above one slice still exits 2 naming `no CI run for <sha>`; a new head
  starts a new grace, and a sighting of a run clears it. Given `<head-sha>`, the first call that
  sees the PR report a different head records that sighting in `.tdd/ci-head-<head-sha>`, which
  later calls for the same sha inherit; once `VERI_CI_HEAD_WAIT` seconds (default 600) have passed
  since it, the call **exits 2** with one stderr line naming the expected and the reported sha. A
  read finding the PR's head equal to `<head-sha>` removes that mark, so a later mismatch starts a
  new one. **Exit 3** still
  pending, no check yet, every one `SKIPPED`, or a mismatched head, when the slice runs out.
  With `VERI_CI_ON_PR=0`, a PR whose head has no check runs and no commit statuses exits 0, since
  there is nothing to wait for; checks that do exist are still waited on and read as above. A
  `VERI_CI_ON_PR` other than `0`, `1` or empty is an **exit 2**, and so is a `veri: ci on pr:` line
  in the target repository's `CLAUDE.md` whose value is neither `0` nor `1` while `VERI_CI_ON_PR`
  is unset (one stderr line naming the line).
  With `VERI_CI_ON_PR` unset, the target repository's `CLAUDE.md` line `veri: ci on pr: 0` (or `1`)
  acts as `VERI_CI_ON_PR=0` (or `1`); with neither, a PR with no checks in a tree where nothing under
  `.github/workflows/` triggers on `pull_request` or `pull_request_target` (a `.yml` or `.yaml` workflow it could not read in full, such as a quoted `on` key, a JSON or flow-mapping root, or a file over 1 MB, counts as a trigger; other names are ignored) exits 0 with a
  warning, since no check will ever come; an explicit `VERI_CI_ON_PR` (`0` or `1`), or the `CLAUDE.md` line, wins over
  this detection, the variable over the line, so `VERI_CI_ON_PR=1` keeps the wait.
  An unreadable read (HTTP 5xx, 403, 429, or no HTTP status) is retried, not counted out: the
  first one records the deadline like a pending read does (not on a draft), and each consecutive
  one sleeps twice the last, from `VERI_POLL`, at most 60 s per sleep; a good read starts the
  sequence over. `VERI_CI_RETRY_BACKOFF=0` keeps every retry sleep at `VERI_POLL`; a value other
  than `0`, `1` or empty is an **exit 2**. A read of the PR's head, of its check runs or of its
  commit status that GitHub refuses with a rate-limit message is not retried on that backoff: the
  script reads the reset time (`gh api rate_limit`) and, when it falls before the CI deadline
  (recorded first when no call has yet, not on a draft), sleeps until it and reads again, except that
  any refused read (head, check runs or commit status) whose reset falls past the slice end (the call's
  start plus `<slice-seconds>`) sleeps only the time left until the slice end, never a negative time,
  and the call **exits 3**, so a call near its deadline ends inside the 600 s tool cap; when it
  falls at or past the deadline it **exits 2** at once without sleeping, its stderr naming the rate
  limit and the reset time; when the reset cannot be read, the backoff above applies.
  Tunables: `VERI_POLL` poll interval (default 10 s), `VERI_CI_RETRY_BACKOFF` (`1`, the default,
  doubles the retry sleep; `0` keeps it flat), `VERI_CI_QUEUE_WAIT` (default 3600 s),
  `VERI_CI_NO_RUN_WAIT` (default 600 s, a non-negative whole number; anything else is an
  **exit 2** naming it), `VERI_CI_HEAD_WAIT` (default 600 s, a non-negative whole number; anything
  else is an **exit 2** before any read, naming it), `VERI_CI_ON_PR` (unset means the target repository's `CLAUDE.md` `veri: ci on pr: 0` or `1` line decides, else checks are expected, unless the detection above finds no
  `pull_request` workflow).
  Each wait call marks its run as stage `ci-wait`, one stage run per slice, so a slice that runs out ends with status 3: a `stage-start` before it and a `stage-end` after it whose `status` is its exit code, both carrying the run's issue (`VERI_ISSUE`, else `VERI_STATE_LABEL_ISSUE`, which the conductor exports); a call refused for its arguments, or run where there is no `.tdd/`, is not marked, and a line it cannot write never changes its exit code. The `--fresh-window` form waits for nothing and is not marked.
- `$S/stop-reason.sh <issue>` — why a run ended with no ✅ Done or ⛔ Bailed comment: the operator's helper. Exit 2 on no argument, an issue with no run under `.tdd/wt`, a missing events log, or a run whose `conductor-<n>.log` has no result record. Nothing in the procedure calls it.
- `$S/run-finished.sh <issue>` — whether run `<issue>` (its worktree `.tdd/wt/<issue>`) is safe to merge, the check `/vmerge` step 4 runs before its merge call (`make run-finished ISSUE=<n>`). Exit **0**, nothing printed, when the run's state is `done`, its committed ledger has no unticked step and no `⟂ refactor pending`, its PR body has no unticked ⟦human-owed⟧ line, and its mutation record (`.tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md`) pins no old text its file no longer holds. Exit **1** prints `not-done <state>` for a run not `done`, else one line per unmet check: `unchecked <step>`, `refactor-pending <step>`, `human-owed <line>`, `stale-pin <file> <first line of old>`; a stale pin also prints a stderr hint to re-record it with `mutation-probe.sh` (`VERI_MUTATION_RECORD`) or retire its entry (a `retired <reason>` field). Exit **2** on no argument, no run worktree for the issue, an unreadable ledger, no PR recorded, or a PR body that could not be read.
- `$S/merge-lock.sh take --pid <pid> [--owner <token>] | release --pid <pid> [--owner <token>] | release --force | holder | gate [--keep] --pid <pid> [--owner <token>] -- <cmd>` — the merge lock, `.tdd/merge.lock` in the main working tree. `--owner <token>` on `take`, `release` and `gate`: `take` records the token in the holder record. A `release` by a different owner, or by none, of a lock taken with an owner exits **1** and frees nothing; a `gate` by a different owner, or by none, runs nothing and exits **2** (below); `release` by the owner still frees the lock, and `release --force` frees any lock. `release` (by the owner, or with `--force`), and a `gate` that releases on failure, remove `veri-merge-<pr>.token` from the git common dir; a token whose owner the lock's holder record names with a live pid is kept, and a release by a different owner (exit 1, frees nothing) leaves it untouched. A successful `take` leaves the token files of other owners in place, since an owner that holds no lock may still be waiting on `take`; a `take` that finds the holder dead or reused removes only the token file holding that holder's owner, and only when the pid is dead or ps read a start time that differs, never on a file's age or a failed read. `take`: exit **0** the lock taken, after waiting while another holder has it (a lock released before the limit below is taken); the wait is bounded: once `VERI_MERGE_LOCK_WAIT` seconds (default 1800) have passed since it started while another holder's lock still stands, exit **2** with one stderr line naming the holder record and the limit, the lock left as it is and no `.merge.lock.*` staging directory left behind; a `.tdd/merge.lock` that is a regular file is waited on to the same limit, then exit **2** naming it (`0` gives up at the first poll that finds the lock held; a value that is not a non-negative whole number exits **2** naming the variable before anything is created or read); **2** also for no `--pid`, a missing, unreadable or malformed holder record, its own `--pid` no longer alive (checked on every pass of its wait; the dead pid is named on stderr), or the lock directory absent while the staged `.merge.lock.*` directory it would rename into place is gone (a line on stderr); and **2** at once, without waiting, when the holder record's pid is dead or alive under a different start time than the record's: one stderr line `stale lock: <record>` naming `merge-lock.sh release --force` as the remedy, the lock directory and record left in place, no `.merge.lock.*` staging directory left behind, and the lock never taken over (a human decides). Start times are read with `ps` under `LC_ALL=C TZ=UTC`, so a record reads the same in any locale or time zone; a record written in the reader's own local form still reads live. A holder alive under its recorded start time, or alive and not signallable (another user's), is still waited on. `release`: exit **0** removed; **1** the caller is not the recorded holder and `--force` was not given; **2** neither `--pid` nor `--force`; **3** no lock. `holder`: exit **0** a live holder, printed; **1** a stale lock (the recorded pid dead, or its start time differs), printed as such; **2** an unreadable record; **3** the lock is free. `gate`: first checks the caller holds the lock: when `.tdd/merge.lock` is absent, or its holder record is missing, empty, or names another pid or another owner (or an owner the call does not pass), it exits **2** without running `<cmd>`, one stderr line naming the holder record (or that no lock is held) and saying `<cmd>` was not run, the lock left as it is, `--keep` or not. By the holder, it runs `<cmd>` and exits with its code; a code other than 0 or 3 (CI red, a refusing gate) releases the lock `<pid>` holds first, 0 and 3 keep it; with `--keep` (before `--`) every code keeps it, and a code other than 0 or 3 prints a note that the lock is kept (a conflict or a refused push); exit **2** no `--pid`, or no `--` and command, with no lock created or removed. Any verb: exit **2** no verb, or one other than these four. The conductor does not call this; it is an operator's (vmerge's and vhandoff's).
- `$S/queued-merge.sh [--skip-suite <model-id> <reason>] <pr-url> <worktree> <branch> <tag-message|-> | --local-green` — a merge that refuses (exit 2, a usage line naming `<pr-url>`) an argument that is not a GitHub PR URL before it takes the lock, then takes the merge lock with `merge-lock.sh take --pid`, fetches origin, refuses (exit **2**, named on stderr, the lock released, before any rebase or push) a PR GitHub already reports merged, a PR closed and unmerged, a PR whose pull record cannot be read (`could not read the head of <pr> from GitHub`), and a PR whose head is already an ancestor of origin/main, pins a lease to origin/`<branch>`'s sha before rebasing onto origin/main (a worktree strictly behind it, its HEAD an ancestor of origin/`<branch>` with no `git status --porcelain` output, is first fast-forwarded with `--ff-only`) (refusing, exit 2 and nothing pushed, when the worktree lacks a commit origin/`<branch>` has and cannot be so fast-forwarded: it is dirty, has commits of its own, or the fast-forward fails), pushes under `--force-with-lease=<branch>:<sha>` (a rebase that stops on git's "would be overwritten by merge" while `git status --porcelain` prints nothing is a phantom stop: it runs `git update-index --really-refresh` and `git rebase --continue`, at most 10 times, appending one `rebase-refresh-retry` event to the worktree's `.tdd/events.jsonl` per refresh; a dirty status, a conflict or any other output exits with git's code, the lock kept, and a stop that outlasts the 10 does the same, pushing nothing), then waits for CI on the pushed head through `wait-ci.sh` under `merge-lock.sh gate --pid` (called again on exit 3; a `wait-ci.sh` exit 2 for a PR head that never matched the pushed sha ends the merge with exit 2, the lock released and no merge call made), then, when the PR has no checks and the rebase moved its tip, runs the full test command on the rebased tree after that wait and before the merge (a red result exits 1 with the PR unmerged and the lock released; a `detect-test-command.sh` that exits non-zero or prints nothing exits 2 naming which, the PR unmerged and the lock released; a rebase that moved nothing, or a PR with checks, runs no such command), and merges that head. After the merge call, whatever its exit code, the PR's state is re-read up to `VERI_MERGE_SETTLE_READS` times (default 10), `VERI_MERGE_SETTLE_SLEEP` seconds apart (default 3), because a call that errored may have landed and a read after one that succeeded may fail: a read of `merged: true` ends the window and the PR is reported merged (tag, branch delete, worktree removal and the rest proceed, exit 0 or 2 as for any merge); The merge call runs with the lock kept and the window re-reads while still holding it, so a merge that landed reaches cleanup holding the lock its release expects and no other merge can take the lock mid-window; a window that never reads merged releases the lock once, after its last read, nothing tagged or cleaned up, and exits 2 when no read in it gave an answer (after a merge call that exited 0 its message says the merge call succeeded and the PR's state could not be read), 1 when reads answered and none said merged after a merge call that exited 0, and the merge call's own code when reads answered and none said merged after one that exited non-zero. Only a PR GitHub reports `merged: true` is tagged (on the merge commit GitHub reports for it, `merge_commit_sha`, fetched first; never a re-read origin/main, which a concurrent merge may have moved past it), its remote branch deleted under `--force-with-lease=<branch>:<the head it pushed>` (a branch origin no longer lists after a refused delete counts as deleted) and its local branch deleted (and, on a `tdd/<n>-<slug>` branch, origin's `refs/veri/runs/<n>`: a ref origin lacks counts as deleted, any other failure is the cleanup step `run records delete`, exit 2), its worktree removed and the main clone's `main` fast-forwarded to origin/main (`--ff-only`), and the lock is released after those. Exit **0** merged and cleaned up; **1** stopped by red CI, or a merge call that exited 0 whose settle window's reads answered and never said merged, the lock released; a rebase conflict or refused push exits with git's code, the lock kept; a merge call that exits non-zero and whose settle window's reads answered and never said merged exits with that call's code, the lock held through the window and released once after its last read; **2** no answer: a settle window, after either kind of merge call, in which no read of the PR's state gave an answer (the lock released once after its last read), a usage line when called with no argument, origin/`<branch>` or the pushed head unresolvable, origin/`<branch>` not an ancestor of the worktree's HEAD and not fast-forwardable into a clean worktree, or a cleanup step (tag, branch delete, worktree remove, lock release) that failed after the merge, or a `main` fast-forward that cannot be done (non-zero exit, reported on stderr after the tag and the lock release), or a PR already merged, closed unmerged, with an unreadable pull record or with its head already on origin/main (each refused before any rebase or push, the lock released), or a PR whose body cannot be read (`gh pr view --json body` exits non-zero: stderr `could not read the PR body of <pr>; not merged`, nothing merged, the lock released), or a PR whose body lacks `Closes #<n>` for the run's issue (the one its `tdd/<n>-<slug>` branch argument names), or, on a branch not so named, lacks `Closes #<n>` for every `.tdd/issue-<n>.md` ledger the PR adds or changes (read from `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD`; a ledger already on main and unchanged by the PR is ignored, as is any other `.tdd/issue-<n>-*.md` file such as a metrics file), refused before the merge with nothing merged and the lock released, or, on a branch not so named, a `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD` that exits non-zero (stderr names the git diff failure; nothing merged, the lock released), each named on stderr; **1** too for a PR whose issue so checked has a mutation record (`.tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md` in the worktree) with an unretired entry whose `old` text its file in the worktree no longer holds, checked after the `Closes #<n>` line: stderr names each such pin by its file and the first line of its old text and says to re-record or retire it, nothing merged, the lock released; a failing `take` stops with its code. An unexpected error raised after the merge lock is taken and before the merge call (a PR body read that is not JSON, for one) exits 2 with one stderr line naming the error, releasing the lock once unless a rebase is stopped mid-way in the worktree, which keeps it, while a rebase stop or a refused push still exits with git's code, the lock kept. A `VERI_MERGE_SETTLE_READS` that is not a whole number of at least 1, or a `VERI_MERGE_SETTLE_SLEEP` that is not a non-negative number, exits 2 naming it before the lock is taken.
