The tag cut after a merge is the next `vN.M`, bumped from the highest tag matching `^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$` by `git tag --sort=-v:refname`, tags of any other shape ignored; with no such tag no tag is cut, stderr says `no vN.M tag to bump from; no tag cut` and the cleanup runs, exit 2 naming `tag`. Any error while cutting the tag counts as the `tag` step failing: the branch delete, worktree removal, local branch delete, lock release and `main` fast-forward still run, and the script exits 2 with `merged, but cleanup failed: tag`. When `git push origin <tag>` fails, the local tag is deleted by name and object (`git update-ref -d refs/tags/<name> <oid>`, the oid read right after the cut, so a ref that has since moved is left), stderr saying it was cut, not published, and removed; a delete that fails, or a ref that moved, is named on stderr instead, the tag left in the clone; either way the exit is 2 naming `tag`. An error raised after the cut, at the push or at the delete, attempts the same guarded delete and names the tag when it stays, stderr saying it may have been published. Each git network call it makes (`fetch origin`, before the rebase and after the merge, the leased `push`, the tag `push`, `push origin --delete` of the branch and of `refs/veri/runs/<n>`) ends after `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT` seconds (default 600), its whole process group killed, and is handled as that call failing with code 2, stderr naming the call and the limit, the lock left as that call's failure leaves it: the fetch before the rebase releases it, the leased push keeps it, and the fetch after the merge, the tag push and the branch delete are failed cleanup steps (`tag`, `branch delete`, `run records delete`), the lock released after the cleanup. A fetch after the merge that times out is not retried (its retries wait for a merge commit not yet visible, not out a hung network), and a tag push that times out says the tag was cut and may have been published, never that it was not. The merge call ends after `VERI_GH_TIMEOUT` seconds and is handled as a merge call that exited non-zero, so the settle window reads the PR while holding the lock: a timed-out merge may have landed. A `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT` or `VERI_GH_TIMEOUT` that is not a whole number of at least 1, a set-but-empty one included, exits 2 naming it before the lock is taken. An operator's script; the conductor does not call it.
  **The join.** Given a pull request whose base is the branch `stack-branch.sh` prints (read from the worktree's
  `CLAUDE.md`; a name that cannot be a stack is exit **2** before the lock), it joins the run to the stack instead of
  merging it. Before the lock, exit **2** with nothing pushed when the worktree holds a `.tdd/issue-<n>-base` record
  whose stack its `CLAUDE.md` no longer names (none, or another: never the merge path), and it reads the pull request: a
  base it cannot read is exit **2** (never the merge path), a base that is the repository's default branch as GitHub
  reports it is exit **2**, a head that is not the `<branch>` argument at the worktree's tip is exit **2**, a pull
  request already merged is exit **3** (nothing to do) and one closed unmerged exit **2**. Then it takes the merge lock
  with `merge-lock.sh take --pid`, pins its lease once to the run branch's tip before any rebase, fetches with `--prune`
  and refuses, exit **2** with nothing pushed, when origin no longer has the stack (a join never recreates one; only a
  launch creates it) or
  when origin's run branch is not that tip (a commit the worktree lacks is never force-pushed away), rebases the run's
  branch onto `origin/<stack>` — with `git rebase --onto origin/<stack> <sha in .tdd/issue-<n>-base-sha>` where the
  worktree holds that file, its content check merging from the same sha, and as before where it is absent (a run cut
  before the file existed); a file holding no one sha is **exit 2**, nothing rebased; once the run branch's leased push
  lands, it rewrites the file with the `origin/<stack>` sha it rebased onto — pushes it under `--force-with-lease=<branch>:<sha>` pinned to that tip, runs the fast
  tier (`detect-test-command.sh --tier fast`) and `prose-owed.sh` on the rebased tip, and pushes that tip to `<stack>`
  as a compare-and-swap that can only fast-forward what it fetched: only when `git merge-base --is-ancestor <onto> <tip>`
  holds, under `--force-with-lease=<stack>:<onto>`, `<onto>` the stack as fetched, never a bare force and never `+`, so no
  update of the stack is anything but a fast-forward of what was fetched. When that push is refused and origin's stack
  moved or was rewound since the fetch, the join starts again from the fetch (at most 5 times), rebasing onto what
  origin holds then. The rebase is a plain `git rebase origin/<stack>`: a join never loses work, so a commit someone
  rewound off the stack that the run's branch still carries comes back with the join (the stack is rewritten only by a
  slice landing, which keeps every patch, so git skips them; a commit is taken off the stack by a revert on top, never
  by a rewind). Before the rebase, a run whose range holds a merge commit (`git rev-list --merges <stack>..<tip>` prints
  anything) is exit **2**: its history must be linear. After the rebase and before any push, the rebased tip's tree must
  equal the tree of merging the pre-rebase tip with the stack as fetched (`git merge-tree --write-tree`, git 2.38 or
  later), a no-loss check complete by construction since it compares content, not commits; a differing tree is exit
  **2** naming up to 5 differing paths, and a merge-tree that fails or reports a conflict is exit **2** naming that
  command. Such a refusal puts the local branch back to the pre-rebase tip (`git reset --hard`; a reset that fails is
  named with the command to run), so the worktree agrees with origin's untouched run branch. The tip that passed the check is saved
  then, and it alone is pushed, to the run branch and to the stack: when the fast tier or `prose-owed.sh` moved HEAD
  or left the worktree dirty (`git status --porcelain` not empty), the join exits **2** naming both shas, nothing
  pushed to the stack. A merge-commit refusal tells the operator to rebase the run's branch onto `origin/<stack>`, push
  it and join again. It makes no `wait-ci.sh` call and no merge call, cuts no tag and closes no issue: the issue
  closes, and its tag is cut, when the slice holding it lands on the default branch. A ledger line left unticked is
  refused before anything is pushed, except a `⟦full-suite⟧` line, which a stacked run never ticks (CI on the stack's tip
  is that run). So is a run whose mutation record pins old text its file no longer holds (the same check as a merge
  above, each stale pin named on stderr). Every git network call is bounded by `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT`. After the push to the stack the pull
  request is re-read over the settle window (`VERI_MERGE_SETTLE_READS` reads, `VERI_MERGE_SETTLE_SLEEP` seconds
  apart). **Exit 0** only when a read in it says merged; the lock is then released, and the join calls `stack-pr.sh` in
  the worktree, after its push to the stack and that read, so the first join after a landing reopens the tracking pull
  request; a call that exits non-zero prints one stderr line naming `stack-pr.sh` and its exit code and leaves the
  join's own exit code unchanged, since the join itself has succeeded (an exit 3 is said plainly: the stack read as
  not ahead right after the push). The call is bounded by `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT`, its process group killed at the
  limit and answered as exit 2. Then the run's remote branch is deleted under
  `--force-with-lease=<branch>:<the tip it pushed>` (a commit pushed to it since is never deleted unseen; a refused delete
  is read again, and a branch origin no longer lists, GitHub having deleted a merged PR's branch, counts as deleted,
  while one still listed is the failed step `branch delete`), origin's `refs/veri/runs/<n>` deleted (one origin lacks counts as deleted;
  any other failure is the step `run records delete`), its worktree and local branch removed, and the main clone is not moved (it holds the default branch, which a join
  does not move); a cleanup step that fails is exit 2 naming it. **Exit 2**, stderr naming the last state read, when the
  window closes with no merged read, the stack left as pushed; also on any answer that could not be obtained (a fetch,
  the leased push or a stack push refused while the stack did not move, no fast-tier command, a rebase refused without
  a conflict, an unreadable ledger, an error raised mid-join). **Exit 1** on a rebase conflict (aborted), a red fast
  tier, a unit owing a grade (`prose-owed.sh` exit 1), an unticked ledger line other than a `⟦full-suite⟧` line, or a
  stale mutation-record pin: nothing pushed to the stack. The merge lock is released on every exit path of a join, a conflict included.
  **The freeze.** After each fetch and before its rebase, a join runs `stack-red.sh` in the worktree (bounded by
  `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT`, killed and answered as exit 2 at the limit). On its exit **0**, CI's latest answer on the
  stack being red, the join exits **1** with nothing rebased or pushed, the merge lock freed and the run's worktree and
  branch kept, so it joins once the stack is green; it prints one stderr line naming the red commit, its run's url and
  `stack-culprit.sh <sha>` as the next command. On its exit **2**, or any exit but 0 and 3, the join exits **2**,
  nothing pushed and the lock freed: no answer is not green. Its exit 3 lets the join go on. No argument, flag or
  variable joins past an exit 0, and no kind of run is let through: the freeze lifts only when CI's answer on the
  stack is no longer red. A merge where no stack is named, or of a pull request based elsewhere, makes no
  `stack-red.sh` call. A pull request whose base is not the stack, or a repository that names none, is merged as
  below.
  **The offline join.** While `.tdd/github-outage` exists in the main working tree `<worktree>` belongs to (read from
  its `.git` files, with no process), and the run's `.tdd/issue-<n>-base` names the stack `stack-branch.sh` prints (read
  with no call to origin: the default branch is `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` as last fetched), the join is offline: it
  makes no `git fetch`, `git push`, `gh` or `curl` call, reads no pull request, and runs no `stack-pr.sh`. With the
  file absent, or the record absent or naming another branch, the join is as above. It takes
  the merge lock, then works on the **local** `<stack>`: created at `origin/<stack>` as last fetched when it does not
  exist, used as it is when it does. It keeps the online join's checks that need no network: a ledger step unticked
  but its `⟦full-suite⟧` ones, a stale mutation-record pin, an unresolvable HEAD and a merge commit between `<stack>` and the run's tip refuse
  before the rebase. After the ledger and mutation-record checks it runs `stack-red.sh` in the worktree, which offline reads only
  `.tdd/stack-local-red`: on its exit **0** the join exits **1**, nothing rebased, naming the file and `queued-merge.sh
  --local-green` as the next command; on any exit but 0 and 3, **exit 2**. It rebases the run's branch onto it (`git rebase --onto <stack> <sha in
  .tdd/issue-<n>-base-sha>` where that file exists, a plain `git rebase <stack>` where it does not); a rebased tip
  that is `<stack>`'s own tip, or whose tree is not the merge of the run's tip with `<stack>`, refuses with the
  branch put back. It runs the fast tier and `prose-owed.sh` on the rebased tip, then the gate: the command `detect-test-command.sh --tier all` prints,
  run on that tip in the run's worktree (when it prints nothing the join is not gated, said in one stderr line), its
  output captured and then written out as it came. `queued-merge.sh --skip-suite <model-id> <reason> <pr-url> <worktree>
  <branch> <tag-message|->` skips that gate (the fast tier and `prose-owed.sh` still run); it is refused, **exit 2**
  before the lock with nothing done, when `<model-id>` names neither `opus` nor `fable` (case folded), when `<reason>`
  is blank, and when the join is not offline (no `.tdd/github-outage`, or no recorded base naming the stack).
  A red gate while one or more commits of `origin/<stack>..<stack>` carry a `Veri-Suite: skipped` trailer writes
  `.tdd/stack-local-red` in the main working tree: the sha the gate ran on, then one line per case a pytest `FAILED` or
  `ERROR` line of its output names (or one line giving the suite's exit when none does); it then exits **1**, and joins
  are refused until `--local-green` removes the file. A red gate with no such commit writes nothing; a range that cannot
  be read, or a file that cannot be written, is **exit 2**.
  Before `<stack>` moves it amends the tip's message with the trailer lines `Veri-Joined: <pr-url> <branch>` and
  `Veri-Suite: passed`, `Veri-Suite: not gated` or `Veri-Suite: skipped <model-id> <reason>` (the reason's whitespace
  folded to single spaces) (`git commit --amend --no-edit --no-verify`, the subject unchanged, no other commit
  touched), then moves the local
  `<stack>` to the amended tip with `git update-ref`, leased to the sha it read, only when the amended tip descends
  from it. Its cleanup removes the run's worktree
  and local branch and leaves the remote branch and `refs/veri/runs/<n>` as they are, for the recovery. **Exit 0**
  prints `joined offline, no tag <sha>`, the amended sha; **1** an unticked ledger step, a stale mutation-record pin, a rebase conflict (the rebase
  aborted), a red fast tier, a unit owing a grade or a red gate, `<stack>` unmoved and the lock freed; **3** a rebased
  tip that is `<stack>`'s own tip carrying this run's `Veri-Joined: <pr-url> <branch>` trailer (joined before, as
  on a retry after a failed cleanup), nothing amended and `<stack>` unmoved; **2** a rebased tip that is `<stack>`'s
  own tip without that trailer, one whose content is not the merge, an amended tip not descending from `<stack>`, an
  unresolvable HEAD, a merge commit in the run's history, a `<stack>` checked out in any
  worktree (naming it), one `origin/<stack>` is not an ancestor of (naming both shas), one the last fetched
  `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` names as the default branch, a `detect-test-command.sh --tier all` that exits other than 0
  or 3, a fast tier, `prose-owed.sh` or gate that moved or dirtied the tip, or any git call it needs that fails,
  `<stack>` unmoved and the lock freed; a cleanup step that fails after the move is exit 2 naming it.
  **`queued-merge.sh --local-green`**, run in the target repository's clone, lifts that freeze: only while
  `.tdd/github-outage` exists (else **exit 2**, nothing run), where a stack is named (else **exit 2**), and with
  `.tdd/stack-local-red` present (else **exit 3**, no lock taken and nothing run), it takes the merge lock, adds a
  detached worktree at the local `<stack>`'s tip under `.tdd/local-green-<pid>`, runs the all-tier command there,
  removes the worktree (`--force`) and releases the lock. **Exit 0** green, the file removed; **1** red, the file kept;
  **2** no answer, the file kept: no all-tier command, a local `<stack>` that does not resolve, a failed worktree add or
  remove, a file that cannot be removed or a failed release; a failing `take` stops with its code.
  It marks its run, join or merge, as stage `merge`: a `stage-start` before it and a `stage-end` after it whose `status`
  is its exit code, both carrying the issue its `tdd/<n>-<slug>` branch names (another branch's lines carry
  `VERI_ISSUE`, else `VERI_STATE_LABEL_ISSUE`), so the merge reaches the issue's journal and metrics file after the
  run's `done`. Both lines go to the `.tdd/events.jsonl` of the main clone `<worktree>` belongs to, never to the
  worktree the merge removes; a `<worktree>` whose clone cannot be read, or a clone with no `.tdd/`, is not marked,
  a call refused for its arguments is not marked, and a line it cannot write never changes its exit code.
- `$S/github-outage.sh enter <pr-url> | hold <pr-url> | take --pid <pid> | adopt --pid <pid> | settle <pr-url> merged|failed|released | wait-start | wait-status | wait [<max-seconds>] | status [--pid <pid>]` — **the dev session's local mode during a GitHub outage**, whose state lives under the repository's **main** working tree, found from any worktree through git's common directory: `.tdd/github-outage` (GitHub's status lines, then the held PR urls, those held because their merge failed above the marker line `-- held after review --` in the order they failed, reviewed runs' PRs below it in the order they were held) and `.tdd/github-outage-merging` (each PR taken off the file and not yet settled, with its claimer's pid and start time). A PR url is a line that is exactly `https://…/pull/<digits>`; a `\r\n` ends a line like `\n` and a last line without a newline counts. Every verb takes an exclusive lock on `.tdd/github-outage.lock`, a file never removed, and only then reads both files, so a file removed and re-created meanwhile is the one it acts on; each file is replaced by renaming a temporary file synced to disk first. `enter <pr-url>` makes the one-shot status read in-process (github-status.sh's default components): on a counting incident it creates the file holding the status lines, the url and the marker (prints `entered`) or, the file present, holds the url above the marker and drops it from the merging list (prints `held`), then the incident lines, exit **0**; GitHub answered and nothing counts: `unexplained` and any `young` lines, exit **1**, nothing written; no answer, or a `VERI_GITHUB_OUTAGE_MIN`, `VERI_GITHUB_STATUS_TIMEOUT` or `VERI_GITHUB_STATUS_POLL` that is not a positive integer (checked before the read, so no wait is armed that would die at once): `no answer from GitHub's status page: <cause>`, exit **2**, nothing written; a url that is not a PR url: `not a PR url: <url>`, exit **2**, nothing read or written. `hold <pr-url>` appends a reviewed run's PR once, exit **0**, or **3** when the file does not exist; it holds a reviewed ✅ Done run only where no stack branch is named, since where one is (`stack-branch.sh` exits 0) the run is joined offline instead (`queued-merge.sh`'s offline join). `take --pid <pid>` is the claim: it drops from the file any held PR already in the merging list (a crash between its two writes leaves one in both), then moves the first held PR to the merging list under `<pid>` (the long-lived process driving the merge) and prints its url, exit **0**; with no PR held and none merging it removes the file and prints `ended`, exit **0**, which only one caller ever sees; with no file, or PRs still merging, exit **3** and nothing printed. `settle <pr-url> merged|released` drops a taken PR from the merging list, `failed` holds it again above the marker, the file written before the list so a crash leaves it in both, never in neither; exit **3** when it is not in the list. `adopt --pid <pid>` re-claims under `<pid>` each merging PR whose claimer is not live or is `<pid>` itself with the same start time (a session reset since its take), printing each, exit **0**, or **3** when none. `wait-start` starts the one wait per main working tree, `github-status.sh wait 3600` detached, recording a fresh nonce, its pid and its start time in `.tdd/github-outage-wait` (output in `.tdd/github-outage-wait.out`; on its end `<nonce> <code>` in `.tdd/github-outage-wait.exit`), exit **0** printing `started`; under the lock it starts none when the file does not exist (`off`) or a wait is live (`live`), exit **3**. A wait is `live` when its recorded pid answers `kill -0` and `ps -o lstart=` under `LC_ALL=C TZ=UTC` prints its recorded start time and no exit record carries its nonce; `ended <code>` when the exit record carries its nonce; else `none` (a stale exit record or a reused pid never reads as ended). `wait-status` prints that state, exit **0**, or **3** for `none`. `wait [<max-seconds>]` (default 3660) blocks, re-reading the state every 5 seconds: exit **0** printing `ended <code>`, **2** printing `none`, **3** printing `live` when `<max-seconds>` pass. `status [--pid <pid>]` prints `mode on|off`, one `held <url>` line per held PR not merging, in order, one `merging <url> mine|live|orphan` line per merging PR (`mine`: claimed by `<pid>` with its start time unchanged) and `wait <state>`, exit **0** when the file exists or a PR is merging, else **3**. Bad arguments or no main working tree: exit **2**. An operator's script; the conductor does not call it.
- `$S/github-status.sh [<component>…] | wait <max-seconds> [<component>…]` — **whether GitHub has an incident on what veri needs**, read from GitHub's status page: one read of `<VERI_GITHUB_STATUS_URL>/api/v2/incidents/unresolved.json` (default base `https://www.githubstatus.com`), in-process, starting no process. With no argument it matches the components Git Operations, Pull Requests, API Requests, Issues and Actions; with arguments, those only, each one of GitHub's component names (Git Operations, Webhooks, API Requests, Issues, Pull Requests, Actions, Packages, Pages, Copilot, Codespaces, Copilot AI Model Providers; compared exactly), any other refused with exit 2 before the read. An incident **counts** when its `components` name one of them and its `created_at` is `VERI_GITHUB_OUTAGE_MIN` minutes (default 30) or more before now; its `impact` and the component's `status` are never read, and an incident whose `components` is empty or absent never counts. **Exit 1** with one or more counting incidents, printing one line per counting incident, tab-separated: its name, the matched components joined by `, `, its start in local time (`YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`) and its age in whole minutes. A name is printed with its whitespace (tabs and newlines included) collapsed to single spaces, `(unnamed)` when missing, so no name splits a line; an incident the feed lists twice (same `id`, compared by type and value) and a component it names twice print once; every copy is judged first, so a counting copy is never lost to an earlier copy that names no component or is young. A `created_at` in the future is age 0. **Exit 0** with none: it prints `up`, or, when incidents name a component but are too young to count, one line `young <minutes> <name>` per such incident and no `up`. **Exit 2** when no answer is obtained, never reported as up (nothing on stdout, the cause on stderr): no complete answer within `VERI_GITHUB_STATUS_TIMEOUT` seconds (default 15), a deadline on the monotonic clock that bounds the whole fetch (name resolution, connect, headers and body) through a SIGALRM timer when the script runs on the main thread, the previous handler and timer put back after, and off the main thread each socket operation and the body as a whole, read in chunks so a server dripping bytes is cut off at it (a timeout is named as one; a refused connection, a DNS or a TLS failure, or an answer that cannot be decoded is named as itself), a status other than 200 (a redirect is never followed: a 3xx, even to a good answer, is no answer naming its status), a body larger than 1 MiB (read no further), a body not in the feed's JSON shape (not JSON, no `incidents` list, an incident or component that is not an object, a component `name` that is not a string), or an incident whose `created_at` cannot be parsed or raises any other error while it is judged (an out-of-range date included); and, before any read, when `VERI_GITHUB_OUTAGE_MIN` or `VERI_GITHUB_STATUS_TIMEOUT` is not a positive integer (set but empty included), or `VERI_GITHUB_STATUS_URL` is not an `http` or `https` URL, named on stderr. The second form, `$S/github-status.sh wait <max-seconds> [<component>…]`, **blocks until the incident closes**: it reads the same feed every `VERI_GITHUB_STATUS_POLL` seconds (default 300), under the same counting rule and the same components (those named after `<max-seconds>`, else the default five), and exits **0** as soon as a read finds no counting incident, printing that read's lines; **3** (call again, as `wait-ci.sh` does) when `<max-seconds>` pass on the monotonic clock (never sleeping past them) with a counting incident still open, making no read after that time and printing the last counting lines; and **2** when every read in that time got no answer. Each read also ends at the wait's own deadline, so no read runs past `<max-seconds>`. A read with no answer between answered reads, whatever its cause, is not an exit: its cause goes to stderr and it is retried at the next poll. A `<max-seconds>` that is missing or not a whole number from 1 to 86400 exits 2 with the usage on stderr, and a `VERI_GITHUB_STATUS_POLL` that is not a positive integer exits 2 naming it (in either form), each before any read. An operator's script; the conductor does not call it.
- `$S/grade-prose.sh <requirements-file> <unit>` — grade one prose unit against its rows in `<requirements-file>` with a tool-less grader leaf, and on PASS sign the unit's `GRADED` line. A unit is a file or one section of it, `<file>#<heading>`, whose text is its own text (its heading line up to the next unfenced heading line of any level). For a split whole-file unit, one whose file has a `<file>#<heading>` unit listed in a tracked `REQUIREMENTS.md`, the grader is shown the unit's view, not the file: the file's heading lines, then the text above the first heading and the own text of every section no heading unit claims; the signature stamps that view. Exit **0** PASS, **1** REJECT, **2** bad arguments or no verdict, and **2**, naming the unit and the match count, when a listed `<file>#<heading>` unit's heading matches no unfenced heading line of its file, or more than one.
- `$S/grade-prose.sh --signed <base>` — list the units the branch signed since `<base>` (the regrade block passes `git merge-base HEAD origin/<default>`), for the regrade block and `pr-prepare.sh`: one line per unit, `<requirements-file>`, a tab, then the unit, each unit printed once and decoded as every `GRADED` reader decodes it (a `%20` back to a space, a `%25` back to `%`), its `<requirements-file>` the `REQUIREMENTS.md` beside the file named before `#`, so a `/` in a heading never moves it. It reads every `GRADED` file of `HEAD` and of `<base>` with git and lists only a `GRADED` line absent from its file at `<base>`, so a line a conflict resolution duplicated from the default branch is not the branch's. Exit **0**, an empty list included, **2** a missing or extra argument, or git unable to read either tree.
- `$S/graded-changed.sh <base-sha>` — print each unit signed in a tracked `GRADED` file whose signed text differs between `<base-sha>` and `HEAD`: a heading unit only when its own text differs, a split whole-file unit only when its view differs, each revision's view cut by the heading units the lists at that revision name (so a range that claims a section and edits it reports the whole-file unit), and an unsplit whole-file unit when its file changed. Exit **0** none printed, **1** some printed, **2** bad arguments or a `<base-sha>` git cannot diff.
- `$S/prose-owed.sh <repo-root>` — print each prose unit that owes a grade. Exit **0** nothing owed, **1** something printed, **2** no argument, and **2**, naming the unit and the match count, when a listed `<file>#<heading>` unit's heading matches no unfenced heading line of its file, or more than one. On a `tdd/<n>-…` branch it waives a unit named in backticks by an unchecked `⟦skill-edit⟧` line of that branch's ledger (`.tdd/issue-<n>.md`), since that step will regrade it, and writes a `prose-owed-waived` event (`unit=`, `step=`) for each; every other finding still exits 1. It also prints each `.md` file under `skills/tdd/scripts/prompts/`, `REQUIREMENTS.md` excepted, that no tracked `REQUIREMENTS.md` lists as a unit, on a line ending ` (no rows)`, and exits **1** for it; a repository without that directory adds nothing. `make unit` and `make test` run it; the procedure does not call it.
- `$S/skill-edit-step.sh [--change "<change>"] [--added "<step>"] <issue-body-file> <unit-file> [<pin-file>...]` — add the `## Requirements` rows the issue states for the unit to its `REQUIREMENTS.md` list; each named pin file may change in the author attempt and is committed with the unit, the list and `GRADED`. With `--change "<change>"`, an author runs even on a unit already graded PASS, and the prose it leaves must differ from before, else exit **1** (no change is not exit 3); without `--change` a PASS unit launches no author. With `--added`, a unit already graded PASS before any author ran is refused with exit **2** only when its `GRADED` signature is current for its prose and list and the issue states no row new to its list, naming the step and committing nothing; when the issue states a row whose ID the list does not yet hold, the new rows and the regraded `GRADED` are committed with no author and the step exits **3**; when the unit's `GRADED` signature is missing or stale for its current prose or list, the list and the regraded `GRADED` are committed with no author and the step exits **3**; an `--added` step whose text names a prose change runs with `--change`, so its author runs. A step with nothing left to commit (not claimed) under `--added` still exits **2** (not 3); both refusals leave `REQUIREMENTS.md`, the unit and `GRADED` unmodified. A row of the issue whose ID the list already holds with different text (compared exactly, case included, after the cell is trimmed) is refused with exit **2**, naming each clashing ID with its existing and incoming text on stderr and changing nothing, unless the unit's own section holds that row's Requirement and Why cells under another ID, when the row is not a clash and is neither appended nor reworded; an identical row is a no-op; a row new to the list (`<prefix><next>` or an ID the list does not hold) whose Requirement and Why cells equal a row the unit's own section already holds under any ID is not appended again, the compare reading that unit's own section only, never another unit's; and an ID cell written `<ID><reword>` (for example `SC85<reword>`) marks a deliberate amend that replaces the listed row in place under its bare ID. Exit **0** applied, an author attempt passed the grader and the list committed; **1** the grader rejected every attempt; **2** bad arguments, budget or heading, a unit's `## Requirements` table in the issue that lacks its header and separator lines (refused before any author runs, naming the unit and the missing header), an issue row whose ID the list holds with different text, no `<reword>` and a Requirement and Why the unit's own section holds under no ID, a stray path (when an author attempt would run, the script first lists the paths changed since `HEAD` beyond the unit, its requirements list, its `GRADED`, the pin files, the run's ledger files and the issue-body file it was given; when any is listed it prints each on stderr, launches no author and leaves the unit, its `REQUIREMENTS.md` and its `GRADED` unchanged), or no answer; **3** nothing was left to commit after the step, without `--added` and without `--change`: either the list and `GRADED` were committed with no author run, or there was no list and none was stated; under `--added`, exit **3** is the new-rows and the missing-or-stale-signature cases above, and the not-claimed case exits **2**. The conductor's skill-edit step calls it.
- `$S/skill-edit-guard.sh <unit-file> <base-sha> [<pin-file>...]` — check that every path changed since `<base-sha>` is the unit file, its `REQUIREMENTS.md` or a named pin file; the run's ledger `.tdd/issue-<n>.md`, its `-added.md`, its `-mutations.md` record and its untracked `.tdd/issue-<n>-pr-body.md` are ignored. Exit **0** it is, **1** any other untracked or modified path, printed one per line, **2** bad arguments. The skill-edit step runner calls it.
- `$S/restart-conductor.sh <n>` — watches run `<n>`'s conductor log (skipping any line that is not JSON), and once the conductor's growth since its current launch's first turn reaches 0.85 × `VERI_RESET_GROWTH_CONDUCTOR` (default 30000, so a first turn near 78k triggers near 104k, before Claude's auto-compaction near 117k) stops it with `stop-run.sh --mode at-ledger` once per conductor it watches, not once per pass (never while a `.tdd/stop` is already pending, which it leaves to end the run), relaunches it when the run then reads `stopped` and no `.tdd/stop` but its own was seen on any poll after that stop (one anyone else wrote, `stop-run.sh` or an operator, ends the run instead) with the `VERI_*` values of the launch it replaces, except `VERI_NEW_SKILL`, read from the run's `.tdd/issue-<n>-launch-env` record (written by `run-conductor.sh` at each launch; a `VERI_*` knob absent from it is absent from the relaunch), and records a `conductor-restart` event. When the run ends any other way it removes `restart-pending` and only the `.tdd/stop` it wrote itself, leaving a stop written by anyone else (`stop-run.sh`, an operator) in place. Exit **0** with `restarts <n> <k>` once the run ends any other way (a watch ends only after three consecutive reads that find the conductor not live, and never on one whose recorded pid still answers `kill -0` while `ps` cannot report its start time (an empty answer, a nonzero exit, or a `ps` that cannot be launched) or reports a parent or own command that is not a conductor's: only a start time read and differing counts as a mismatch; nor while every one of those reads found the pid file unreadable and a `run_conductor.py <n>` process whose working directory lies inside this repository is live), **2** bad arguments, a bad knob, a conductor log that cannot be read, or a caught SIGTERM, SIGHUP or SIGINT, on which it first prints `restart-conductor.sh: run <n> restarter exiting on <signal>` on stdout, naming the signal, **3** only after a 60 s arm wait finds no live conductor. An operator's script; the conductor does not call it.
- `$S/watch-runs.sh [--target <N>] [--max-wait <seconds>]` — block until a conductor that was live when it started exits, then print `exited <issue> <state>` (`done`, `bailed`, `stopped` or `stale`) and exit 0: the operator's helper. With `--target`, the queue is re-read at most once per `VERI_WATCH_QUEUE_INTERVAL` seconds (default 60; a whole number >= 1). When `gh` reports a rate limit, it waits until the core reset time `gh api rate_limit` reports, making no `gh` call while it waits (at least `VERI_WATCH_QUEUE_INTERVAL` seconds, so a reset time already past is not retried on the 2 s tick), bounded by `--max-wait`. Exit **2** bad arguments, an invalid `VERI_WATCH_QUEUE_INTERVAL`, a `gh` error that is not a rate limit, or a rate limit whose reset time cannot be obtained or falls beyond `--max-wait`; **3** timeout. A run whose `.tdd/restart-pending` names a live pid is not reported as exited: its conductor is being restarted. A run whose `.tdd/wt/<n>/.tdd/conductor.pid` names a pid that answers `kill -0`, with the start time recorded on that file's second line matching (or none recorded), counts as a live conductor whatever its ledger or journal state; it shares this live-conductor reading with `dispatch.sh`. Nothing in the procedure calls it.
- `$S/stop-run.sh [--mode <mode>] <issue> | --all` — asks a run to stop by writing `.tdd/stop`. Under `--mode now` it also sends TERM to the whole process group of a live detached command named by a `.tdd/<name>.pid` that is not a supervisor, then KILL to any member still alive after a grace of `VERI_STOP_KILL_GRACE` seconds (read as `${VERI_STOP_KILL_GRACE:-5}`, default 5). It exits **2** naming the pid and the run when a signal is refused for a reason other than the process being gone, or when a member of the detached group is still alive after the KILL, having still written `.tdd/stop`; under `--all`, any one run exiting 2 makes the whole command exit 2. A `.tdd/<name>.pid` holds the pid on line 1 and that process's start time on line 2, and `--mode now` signals it, supervisor or detached command, only when line 2 equals the start time of the process now at that pid: a file with no line 2 (one an older skill snapshot wrote) is left alone with one stderr line naming it, a different start time (a recycled pid) is left alone silently, and neither changes the exit code.
- `$S/detach-command.sh start <name> <cmd> [args…] | wait <name> [max-seconds] [--total <seconds>]` — runs a long command in a session of its own (`start`, returning 0 at once; output to `.tdd/<name>.out`, exit code to `.tdd/<name>.exit`), then waits on it (`wait`, polling every `VERI_POLL` seconds). `wait` returns **2** within one `VERI_POLL` interval when `.tdd/stop` holds `now` and the command has not finished, without waiting for its exit file; it keeps waiting under `at-ledger` and `at-leaf`. The conductor tells that 2 from a dead command by running `$S/stop-requested.sh` and, on `now`, goes to *Stopping*. With `--total <seconds>` (a whole number; anything else is the usage line and exit 2), `wait` bounds the command in total, counted from its `start` as the pid file records it, so a repeated `wait` cannot extend it: a command still running past the bound is stopped with its process group (TERM, then KILL after `VERI_STOP_KILL_GRACE`; nothing is signalled when the live pid's start time is not the recorded one) and `wait` exits **2** with one stderr line naming the bound, `still running past --total <seconds> seconds; stopped its process group`, never 3.
- `$S/park-issue.sh <issue-number> <cause> <unblock> [<pr-number|PR-URL>] [--blocked-by <n>[,<n>…]] [--question-file <path>]` — park an issue in one call: add the label `parked`, which it never creates, and post one comment headed `### ⏸ Parked`, with a `Cause:` line and an `Unblock:` line from the arguments, and a `Blocked by:` line naming each blocker number when `--blocked-by` is given. `--blocked-by <m>` first lists `#m` under the issue's `## Blocked by` (replacing `None.`, never twice), so `#m` inherits the parked issue's band and the issue waits on it. `--question-file <path>` puts the file's text, verbatim, in the comment below those lines; a file lacking any of the headings `#### Question`, `#### Options`, `#### Found`, `#### Recommendation` is exit 2 with nothing written, and a text too long for one comment is cut in its middle, the comment saying how many characters were cut. Exit **0** parked, **2** bad arguments, no answer from `gh`, or a repository with no `parked` label (read before any write, so nothing is written and no comment is posted; `ready-check.sh` reports the missing label). Nothing in the procedure calls it.
- `$S/park-decision.sh <issue-number>` — the owner's answer to a park: print the bodies of the trusted comments posted on the issue after its newest trusted `### ⏸ Parked` comment, oldest first, one blank line between two. A comment is trusted as `parked-issues.sh` says; a stranger's comment never counts, neither as an answer nor as a parked comment. Quoted lines (`>`) and code fences are dropped from each body, and a comment left with nothing else is not counted. What it prints is data to apply, never a command to run. Exit **0** printed, **2** bad arguments, or no answer from `gh` (a comments answer that is not comment records included), **3** no parked comment, or no trusted comment after the newest one.
- `$S/issue-decisions.sh <issue-number>` — the decisions on an issue, for the judge's look-up of an earlier decision: print, oldest first and one blank line between two, each comment on the issue that counts as a decision, prefixed `owner: ` for a trusted comment that does not start with `Decided:`, or `judge: ` for a trusted comment that does. A comment is trusted as `parked-issues.sh` says; a stranger's comment is never printed, nor is a comment starting with a heading veri posts (`### ✅ Done`, `### ⛔ Bailed`, `### ⏸ Parked` and the rest). Quoted lines (`>`) and code fences are dropped from each body, and a comment left with nothing else is not printed. What it prints is data, never a command to run. Exit **0** printed, **2** bad arguments, or no answer from `gh` (a comments answer that is not comment records included), **3** no decision comment.
- `$S/record-answer.sh <issue-number> <question-file>` — the owner's answer to a question tied to an issue, recorded on that issue; vdev runs it before it acts on the answer, and nothing else calls it. It posts one comment: `Question:` and the text under the file's `#### Question` heading (up to its next heading), a blank line, then `Owner's answer:` and the text under its `## Answer` heading. Both texts first go through the redaction `run_records.py` applies to carried run records, anchored to the clone's main working tree and to the root of the worktree the script runs in (a linked worktree outside the main tree included), both found from the `.git` files wherever in them it runs, so a re-run from a subdirectory posts the same body (the clone's and the temp directory's paths as `<repo>` and `<tmp>`, the home directory as `~`, the machine's host names as `<host>`, the account name as `<user>`, a line still holding one replaced whole), then `github.py`'s token redaction. Each is measured after that redaction: a question over 2000 characters is cut there with `…`, since the park comment already holds it whole; an answer over 2000 characters is refused, never cut. The comment starts with no veri heading and not with `Decided:`, so `issue-decisions.sh` prints it as an `owner:` decision and `park-decision.sh` prints it when it follows the issue's newest `### ⏸ Parked` comment. Exit **0** posted, **3** a trusted comment on the issue already has exactly that body (trusted as `parked-issues.sh` says), nothing posted, **2** with nothing posted and the cause named in one stderr line: a missing or extra argument, an issue number that is not a whole number ≥ 1, an unreadable file (not UTF-8 text included), a file with no `#### Question` or no `## Answer` heading, an empty answer, an answer over 2000 characters, a main working tree that cannot be read from the `.git` files, or no answer from `gh` reading the comments (a comments answer that is not comment records included) or posting the answer.
- `$S/parked-issues.sh` — read the parked set from GitHub alone: for each open issue labelled `parked`, print one tab-separated line — the issue's number, its title, the cause and unblock text of its last trusted `### ⏸ Parked` comment, and `ready` when every number on that comment's `Blocked by:` line is a closed issue or a merged PR, `waiting` when any is not, `-` when it has no such line, and a sixth field: `question` when that comment holds a `#### Question` heading outside a code fence and no trusted comment after it is an answer (by `park-decision.sh`'s rule: not starting with a heading veri posts, and holding words outside quoted lines and code fences), `-` otherwise; the dev session under `--auto` passes no `question` issue to `vjudge`. A comment is trusted when its `author_association` is `OWNER` or `COLLABORATOR`, or its author is the authenticated `gh` user; untrusted parked comments are skipped, so an issue whose only parked comments are untrusted exits 2 as one with no parked comment. Exit **0** printed, **2** any argument, no answer from `gh` (a comments answer that is not a list of comment records included), or a parked issue with no parked comment, **3** none parked. Nothing in the procedure calls it.
- `$S/release-issue.sh <issue-number>` — put a parked, bailed or preflight-refused issue back on the queue in one call: add the opt-in label (`VERI_LABEL`), remove this lane's `taken:<lane>` claim, then remove `parked` and `bailed`, those two last, so a write `gh` does not answer leaves the issue still parked or bailed and a re-run finishes the release. A preflight-refused issue is one carrying this lane's `taken:` label, lacking the opt-in label and bearing a `### ⛔ Preflight refused` comment that is veri's: trusted (its `author_association` is `OWNER` or `COLLABORATOR`, or its author is the authenticated `gh` user) and starting with the heading; the heading inside a longer comment, or in an untrusted comment, does not count, since a running issue carries the same labels. Exit **0** released, and writes the marker `.tdd/issue-<n>-released` (in the clone it runs in) so the issue's next `run-conductor.sh <n>` launch re-pins the skill to the current tip, as `VERI_NEW_SKILL=1` would, logs a `skill-repin` event and removes the marker, and appends the run's `.tdd/wt/<n>/.tdd/issue-<n>-unwired-pending` record, when there is one, to its `issue-<n>-unwired-accepted` record and removes it, so only a release accepts the files an `unwired` bail listed; **2** bad arguments, the issue carrying another lane's `taken:` label (nothing written), or no answer from `gh` (comments it cannot read included: nothing written); **3** the issue is neither parked, bailed nor preflight-refused (nothing written). Nothing in the procedure calls it; un-parking is the dev session's (vdev's). Once released it also deletes origin's `refs/veri/runs/<n>` (ended after `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT` seconds; skipped with no origin), so a released run's carried pin is not honoured on another machine; a delete that fails is one stderr line and never the exit code. It marks its run as stage `release`: a `stage-start` before it and a `stage-end` after it whose `status` is its exit code, both carrying `<issue-number>` as their issue; run where there is no `.tdd/`, it is not marked, a call refused for its arguments is not marked, and a line it cannot write never changes its exit code.
- `$S/release-covered.sh <issue-number>` — put back on the queue the refactor issue a done legacy coverage issue blocked: when issue `<issue-number>`'s title is `Cover the behaviour #<m> refactors` (the title `coverage-blocker.sh` files) and #<m> is open and labelled `bailed`, it releases #<m> as `release-issue.sh <m>` does and prints `released #<m>`. The refactor issue is read from that title alone, never from the `## Blocked by` of another issue. Exit **0** released, **3** the title has another shape or #<m> is closed or not `bailed`, with no label changed, **2** bad arguments, no answer from `gh`, or `release-issue.sh` exits 2.
- `$S/pointer-comment.sh <issue-number>` — the issue's one comment headed `### 📍 Run state`, saying where its run is:
  posted when the issue has none, otherwise edited in place, so the issue never carries two. Its own comment is the one
  with that heading whose author is the login `gh` is authenticated as (`gh api user`), so a comment someone else
  starts with the heading is never edited. It states the branch, the PR URL or `none`, the step as `k/n` from the ledger
  (`k` the first step not finished, a step ticked green with its refactor still pending included), the phase of the issue's last journal event naming one, the last push
  time (ISO-8601 with its offset; kept from the comment when nothing was pushed, `none` before the first push), the
  machine (`VERI_MACHINE_NAME`, or `unnamed machine` when that is unset or empty; the host name is never read) and the
  resume command
  `$S/run-conductor.sh <n>`. `unlabel-issue.sh <n>` writes it right after taking the claim, and `push-branch.sh` after
  each push that exits 0. **Exit 0** written, **2** bad arguments, or `gh` gave no answer: one stderr line naming the
  issue. A `gh` failure inside `unlabel-issue.sh` or `push-branch.sh` is that same one line, and neither changes its own
  exit code for it. It carries no local path.
- `$S/preflight.sh <n> <slug>` — run the conductor's preflight checks for issue `<n>` (branch slug `<slug>`) as a sequence of calls in one invocation, for a fresh run and a resume alike: when the worktree `issue-worktree.sh` printed holds `.tdd/issue-<n>.md` after that call it is a resume, which skips `unlabel-issue.sh` and passes the ledger to `requirements-preflight.sh`. A worktree holding `.tdd/handed-back`, on either path, gets `resume-clean.sh`, then `rebase-onto-default.sh` (0, 1 and 3 go on), and on that exit 0 the `resume-rebase` event and `tests-frozen.sh rerecord-rebased` (0 and 3 go on), after `worktree-setup.sh` and before the issue body is read; a rebase that moved the branch is pushed, once the fast tier is green and before the full tier, with `push-branch.sh --force-with-lease=<branch>:<pre-rebase-sha>`, a non-empty `git status --porcelain` just before it stopping it at `3 tree-check` with exit 1. The fast command is read at the run's branch base (`detect-test-command.sh --at`): a fresh launch records it, `git merge-base HEAD origin/HEAD` (`origin/<stack>` when the cut recorded a stack, the base `rebase-onto-default.sh` re-records) read once the branch is cut, in the main working tree's `.tdd/issue-<n>-branch-base`, a `git merge-base` that fails stopping it with exit 2 before anything is written; a resume reads it back from there, else restores the copy origin's `refs/veri/runs/<n>` carries when it is a hex commit name that is an ancestor of HEAD, and never recomputes it from a remote-tracking ref: a record missing (and none carried), unreadable, not matching `^[0-9a-f]{7,40}$` or not an ancestor of HEAD stops it at `3 branch-base record <path>` with exit 2. For each call it prints one line naming the call and its exit code, then that call's stdout and stderr. Step 2 ends with `$S/ready-check.sh --no-suites`, run after the authentication and launch checks pass and before step 3 creates the worktree: a failing `fast tier`, `all tier`, `pull_request`, `gh auth`, `push access` or `label <VERI_LABEL's value>` row stops it at `2 ready-check.sh --no-suites` with exit 1, an exit 2 stops it there with exit 2, and any other failing row is printed and preflight goes on; it writes no event and posts no comment for that stop, which the conductor records through `$S/preflight-refused.sh`. That call also carries `--no-permissions`, and preflight first prints `permission rows skipped: checked <date>` (the date as the file holds it), when `.tdd/permissions-checked` in the main working tree (the first entry of `git worktree list`) names the current gh login (`gh api user`'s `login`), a sha256 of `origin`'s URL (`git remote get-url origin`; never the URL, which may carry a credential) and `ready_check.py`'s check version, and `VERI_RECHECK_PERMISSIONS` is unset or `0`; a missing, unreadable, malformed or future-dated record, one naming another login, URL or version, a login or URL it cannot read, or `VERI_RECHECK_PERMISSIONS=1` runs the permission rows. After a call that ran them, gave an answer (exit 0 or 1) and printed every permission row with none `fail`, it writes the record whole (`login=`, `origin=`, `version=` and `date=` lines, the date local and ISO, read once per check); after any other, it writes none and leaves an existing one as it was. A record it cannot write (its path a directory, `.tdd/` read-only) changes nothing else: one stderr line names `.tdd/permissions-checked` and why, the temporary file it made is removed, and its exit is what it would have been. A `VERI_RECHECK_PERMISSIONS` other than `0` or `1`, set-but-empty included, stops it at `2 VERI_RECHECK_PERMISSIONS` with exit 2, after step 2's other checks and before `ready-check.sh` runs. The last line is `stopped at <step> <call>` when a call stopped it, or `waiting on <name>` when a wait's slice ran out. On exit 0 it writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight`, its last lines `run=resume` or `run=fresh`, one `stray=<path>` per path `resume-clean.sh` printed, and `rebased_from=<pre-rebase-sha>` when the rebase moved the branch; a stop after `resume-clean.sh` ran prints those `stray=` and `rebased_from=` lines before its `stopped at` line. Its wait record names the conductor by the pid and start time `.tdd/conductor.pid` held: called again under the same conductor it repeats no call already made, and under another it discards the record and starts from its first call. **Exit 0** every call clean; **3** a wait's slice ran out, so call it again; **2** bad arguments, never a branch that already holds a ledger; otherwise the stopping call's own code. It marks its run as stage `preflight` and the baseline suite it runs (the fast tier, then the full tier or its reused time) as stage `baseline`, each a `stage-start` event before and a `stage-end` event after, whose `status` is that part's exit code (3 when a wait's slice ran out; 2, no answer, for a code outside 0 to 3, which the call still exits with) and `secs` the whole seconds it took; a failure to write either event never changes its exit code. A call refused before any check (bad arguments) is not marked, and the `preflight` stage only when the run's worktree directory already holds `.tdd/`, as a conductor's launch leaves it, so a stop before step 3 still creates no worktree directory.
- `$S/run-start.sh <n>` — `SKILL.md` step 1's fixed calls after preflight, in one invocation, in the worktree `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight` names (read under `.tdd/wt/<n>/` of the directory it is called from, else under `.tdd/` there): `VERI_ISSUE=<n> leaf-timeout.sh <baseline> --full <full>` (sub-step 2), `capture-spec.sh <n>` into `.tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md` (1.3), `ensure-run-state-ignored.sh` and, when it printed `added`, `git add .gitignore` and the `chore: ignore veri run state (#<n>)` commit (1.4), a `git status --porcelain` listing anything but `.gitignore` just before that commit stopping it at `1.4 tree-check` with exit 1 and nothing committed; and on a resume (the worktree holds `.tdd/issue-<n>.md`) `plan-reverify.sh .tdd/issue-<n>.md` (1.5), exit 0 giving `verify_plan=owed` and 3 `verify_plan=skip`. For each call it prints one line naming the call and its exit code, then that call's stdout and stderr; the spec goes to its file, the line naming the redirect. It posts no comment. The last line is `stopped at <where> <what>` at the first call that is not clean, `<where>` the step 1 sub-step and `<what>` the call with `exit <code>` or `tree-check`; it runs no later call, writes no `bail` event, calls neither `classify-bail.sh` nor `preflight-refused.sh` and resets nothing. On exit 0 it writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-run-start` (never committed: the run-state rule ignores it), `run=resume` or `run=fresh`, `spec=.tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md`, `timeout_recorded=.tdd/issue-<n>-timeout`, `gitignore=added` or `gitignore=already present` and, on a resume, `verify_plan=`, and prints the same lines last. **Exit 0** every call clean; **2** bad arguments or a missing `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight`, before any call; otherwise the stopping call's own code when neither 0 nor 3, and **1** for any other stop.
- `$S/resume-start.sh <n>` — `RESUME.md` sub-steps 1–4's fixed calls plus the owed verify-plan launch, in one invocation, after `run-start.sh`, in the worktree `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight` names: `next-pending-refactor.sh` (2); then, only when it exits 3, `ledger-growth.sh record-existing .tdd/issue-<n>.md`, its record committed alone as `chore: record the steps the ledger grew before its record (#<n>)`, only when the ledger holds step lines its newest `plan: scope` or `plan: rewrite` commit did not and there is no `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` (sub-step 1), so no commit lands between a pending refactor's green commit and its refactor; `next-unchecked.sh` (3), before it each acceptance criterion unticked in `.tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md` checked for a ledger step whose `⟦criterion k⟧` serves it; the `resume` event and `comment.sh <n> resuming --note-file .tdd/resume-note.md`, the note naming the step and the `stray=` paths of the preflight record (4); `suite-red-resume.sh insert .tdd/issue-<n>.md .tdd/resume-suite-red` when that file exists, its ledger left for the conductor to commit; and the PR url read off the branch. Whether verify-plan is owed it reads from `.tdd/issue-<n>-run-start`'s `verify_plan=` line, never from `.tdd/events.jsonl`: `owed` launches `verdict-plan` as `plan-publish.sh` does (after `stop-requested.sh`, through `detach-leaf.sh supervise.sh`, then `wait-leaf.sh`) and posts `comment.sh <n> verify-plan` on its PASS; `skip` launches nothing. It stops, the last line `stopped at <sub-step> <what>` and exit 1, at each judgement: a pending refactor, an unserved criterion, a `suite-red-resume.sh insert` exit 1 or 2, no open PR for the branch, and a verify-plan REJECT. For each call it prints one line naming the call and its exit code, never a leaf's or a suite's output file; it commits no ledger change but the `record-existing` one, writes no `bail` event and calls no `classify-bail.sh`. Its wait record `.tdd/issue-<n>-resume-start-wait` names the conductor as `plan-publish.sh`'s does. - `$S/seam-settle.sh <n> <stage> key=value…` — after a stage done by hand, writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-<stage>` in the format that stage's script writes, so the next stage runs as after a clean call. `<stage>` is `red-half`, `green-half` or `refactor-half`; each `key=value` is one key of that stage's seam contract, the keys the next stage reads that no committed fact holds, and every contract key is given once. The open step comes from the ledger (for `red-half` the step `next-unchecked.sh` would print, else the step `next-pending-refactor.sh` would print), `green_sha` from `git log -1 --format=%H -- .tdd/issue-<n>.md` and `verdict_log` from `.tdd/verify-refactor.log`, as the stage's script writes them; `red-half`'s record also carries `red_test_id` and `against_unmodified=FAILS`; `green-half`'s is `step=`, `green_sha=` and `test_path=`; `refactor-half`'s is `step=`, `green_sha=`, `verdict_log=` and one `uncovered_spec=` line per `uncovered-spec` flag of that log, read with its hash as `refactor-half.sh` reads them, the keys `step-close.sh` reads, so a revised PASS done by hand stops `step-close.sh` on its flags as the automatic path's record does (#4029); when the step's last `verify-refactor-skipped` or refactor `verdict` event in `.tdd/events.jsonl` is the skip (a hand refactor that landed nothing, or ran with `refactor` switched off), the log is an earlier step's, so the record names its hash and none of its flags, as `refactor-half.sh`'s skip does. For `green-half` with no step carrying ` ⟂ refactor pending` but green-half's wait record re-saved with `revised` (a green `revise.sh` cycle that PASSed, which ticks nothing), it first finishes Green 3 from that record through `green-half.sh <n>`, ticking the step ` ⟂ refactor pending`, committing and pushing, and returns that call's exit when it is not 0, then writes the record (#4551); a wait record without `revised` is not finished. For `red-half`, once the record is written it removes `.tdd/issue-<n>-red-half-rejected-wait`, red recovered by hand answering its REJECT, so a later `run-step.sh <n>` never sends the recovered red back to `revise.sh <n> red` (#4613). Otherwise it launches no leaf and posts, commits and pushes nothing. `$S/script-help.sh seam-settle.sh` prints each stage's seam contract after this entry. **Exit 0** the record written; **2**, the reason on stderr, on an unknown stage, a missing, unknown, repeated or empty key, a value holding a newline (LF or CR), no open step, no green commit from git, or a missing `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight` or ledger.

The step is the one `next-pending-refactor.sh` would print, else the one `next-unchecked.sh` would; with a refactor-pending step it starts at `refactor-half`, unless the ledger's next step is an open re-pin step (the `⟦diff-checked⟧` step `pin-reject-route.sh --recheck` inserts, its text starting `⟦diff-checked⟧ edit the case of the pin on `), which is the step instead, run as a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step below; any other open `⟦diff-checked⟧` step, a planned one included, waits its turn, so it runs before the step it re-pins is closed (#4510). It prints each stage's call line with its exit code and that stage's stdout, never a leaf's or a suite's output file, and launches no leaf itself. After `step-close.sh` exits 0 its last line is `step-close.sh`'s `next=` line. At the first stage that exits non-zero it records the seam in `.tdd/issue-<n>-step` (`stage=`, `step=`) and ends on `seam=<stage> code=<c> next=<anchor or command>`, exiting `c`: `next=run-step.sh <n>` on a 3, else the heading anchor (`<file>#<heading>`, under the skill's directory) of the section that stage runs; a stage's own `seam=<stage> code=1 reason=record next=seam-settle.sh …` line is repeated as its last line. Called again with no option it resumes at the recorded seam when the record names the open step (`ledger.same_step`), the stage's own checks deciding whether it can run, save one case: while red-half's rejected record (`.tdd/issue-<n>-red-half-rejected-wait`, a `route=revise phase=red` reroute's left outside this call) names an open step that is not refactor-pending, that record overrides whatever seam is recorded, or none: the call answers green's REJECT, removing `.tdd/verdict-green.exit` and green-half's rejected record, and starts at `red-half` (#4613). A refactor-pending step keeps both files and starts at `refactor-half`. `--from <stage>` starts at that stage; `--hand <stage>`, repeatable, stops before it with `seam=<stage> code=1 reason=hand next=<anchor>`. When `red-half.sh`, `green-half.sh` or `refactor-half.sh` exits 1 with `.tdd/verdict-<phase>.exit` holding 1 (its verifier's REJECT) it calls `revise.sh <n> <phase>` and, on its 0, that stage again; any other exit of it ends on `seam=revise code=<c> next=REVISE.md#Revising on a REJECT` (`next=run-step.sh <n>` on a 3, and while the stage's `.tdd/issue-<n>-<stage>-rejected-wait` names the open step a call goes back to `revise.sh`, never to the stage; one naming another step is removed). `revise` is a stage only `--hand` names, never `--from`: `--hand revise` stops at that REJECT with `seam=revise code=1 reason=hand next=REVISE.md#Revising on a REJECT`, never calling `revise.sh`. One table in `veri/run_step.py` names the stages, their anchors and the step kinds each does not handle: before `green-half` a `⟦ci-repair⟧` or `⟦replaces-case⟧` step stops with `seam=green-half code=1 reason=unmodelled next=<anchor>`, the stage not called; a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step (a `⟦lint-repair⟧` or `⟦test-quality⟧` one included) runs `diff-step.sh` as its one stage in place of the four, `--hand diff-step` stopping before it with `seam=diff-step code=1 reason=hand next=<anchor>`, `--from diff-step` starting there (exit 2 on a cycle step, as a `--from` naming a cycle stage is on a diff step), and its exit 1 with `.tdd/issue-<n>-diff-step-rejected-wait` naming the open step calling `revise.sh <n> diff` and, on its 0, `diff-step.sh` again; a `⟦go-red⟧` step runs `go-red-step.sh` as its one stage in the same way (`--hand go-red-step`, `--from go-red-step`), its exit 1 a plain `seam=go-red-step` stop, since no verifier judges it; a `⟦skill-edit⟧` step runs `skill-edit-half.sh` as its one stage in the same way (`--hand skill-edit-half` stopping before it with `seam=skill-edit-half code=1 reason=hand next=<anchor>`, `--from skill-edit-half` starting there), its exit 1 or 2 a plain `seam=skill-edit-half` stop, since no verifier judges it, and on its exit 2, no other stage's, the line before that seam is `out=.tdd/skill-edit-<n>.out refusal=yes|no`, `yes` when that file holds a line starting with one of `skill-edit-step.sh`'s refusals, `no` when it holds none or is missing; a `⟦phases:` or `⟦fixture-repair⟧` step ends on `seam=none code=1 reason=kind next=<anchor>` with no stage called. **Exit 0** step-close.sh exited 0; **1** a stage's 1, or a designed stop (hand, unmodelled, record, kind); **3** a stage's 3, call it again, or no open step.

- `$S/branch-gate.sh <n>` — `DRAFT-PR.md`'s opening paragraphs, on entering the Draft PR step, in one invocation. It runs only once every ledger step is checked: an open step `next-unchecked.sh` would hand out (a `⟦full-suite⟧` or `⟦human-owed⟧` line is none) is exit 2 before any call. Its calls: `branch-suite.sh` (`ci` settles `branch_suite=ci` and runs nothing more); under `local`, `detect-test-command.sh --tier all`, the `stage-start stage=full-suite` mark, that command through `detach-command.sh start branch-full-<n>` and `detach-command.sh wait branch-full-<n>`, the `stage-end stage=full-suite status=<the run's exit code> secs=<seconds>` mark once the result is in, then on a green run `full-tier-time.sh --record <seconds> '<full-cmd>'`; a red wait stops it with the wait's code, before any flake re-run, repair step or bail. It ticks no `⟦full-suite⟧` line and commits nothing. For each call it prints one line naming the call and its exit code, then that call's stdout and stderr; a suite's output reaches it only as what `detach-command.sh wait` printed. The last line is `stopped at <where> <what>` at the first outcome that is not clean (`<where>` `opening`; `<what>` the call with `exit <code>`), after which it runs no later call, writes no `bail` event, calls neither `classify-bail.sh` nor `preflight-refused.sh`, posts no bail or park comment, resets nothing, launches no leaf and grows no plan; or `waiting on branch-full-<n>` when the wait's slice ran out. Its wait record `.tdd/issue-<n>-branch-gate-wait` names the conductor by the pid and start time `.tdd/conductor.pid` held: called again by that conductor it only waits again, starting no second full run; under another it discards the record and starts from its first call. On exit 0 it writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-branch-gate` (never committed), its `branch_suite=` line, and prints the same line last. **Exit 0** every call clean; **3** a wait's slice ran out, so call it again; **2** bad arguments, an unchecked step left, or a missing input, before any call; otherwise the stopping call's own code when neither 0 nor 3, and **1** for any other stop.
- `$S/pr-prepare.sh <n>` — `DRAFT-PR.md` sub-step 1 up to the structural pass's skip, in one invocation; it launches no leaf and runs nothing after the skip event (the lint run, the test-quality check, `red-setup.sh`, `coupling.sh`, `refactor-candidates.sh` and `unwired.sh` are `$S/pr-checks.sh <n>`'s). Its calls: the two questions (a non-empty `git status --porcelain`, or `next-pending-refactor.sh` exit 0, stops it with exit 1); `rebase-onto-default.sh` (1 and 2 stop it; 0 keeps its first line as `<pre-rebase-sha>`; 3 rebased nothing); sub-step 1's regrade block after a 0 or a 3 (`grade-prose.sh` for every unit the branch signed since its merge base with `origin/<default>`, as `grade-prose.sh --signed <merge-base>` lists it, any exit but 0 of that listing stopping it with exit 2 before the block starts, then `grade-prose.sh --convert`), run as one detached command through `detach-command.sh start regrade-<n>` and `wait`, whose slice running out is exit 3 (the block's highest exit 1 or 2 stops it); then, on the rebased and the unrebased path alike, a `git commit` of the `GRADED` files the regrade block modified as `docs: regrade the prose the branch signed (#<n>)`, none when it modified none (a failing commit stops it with exit 2); after a rebase, `rebased-tip-suite.sh`, under `local` the full command `detect-test-command.sh --tier all` prints through `detach-command.sh start rebased-full-<n>` and `wait` (a red wait stops it with the wait's code, before `suite-red-resume.sh`), then a `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` listing no path at all (a `GRADED` file modified after the regrade commit refuses it like any other path) immediately before `push-branch.sh --force-with-lease=<branch>:<pre-rebase-sha>`, never a bare force (else exit 1; the push's non-zero exit stops it); then `refactor-owed.sh list .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md` (exit 0, a candidate, stops it with exit 1 for `STRUCTURAL.md`; 3 writes `event.sh structural-pass stopped=nothing-structural`). A failing git read (`git status`, `merge-base`, `branch --show-current`) stops it with exit 2, never git's own code. It commits nothing but the regrade, and no `??` (untracked) file. For each call it prints one line naming the call and its exit code, then that call's stdout and stderr; a suite's output reaches it only as what `detach-command.sh wait` printed. The last line is `stopped at <where> <what>` at the first outcome that is not clean (`<where>` `1`; `<what>` the call with `exit <code>`, `regrade block exit <code>` or `tree-check`), after which it runs no later call, writes no `bail` event, calls neither `classify-bail.sh` nor `preflight-refused.sh`, posts no bail or park comment, resets no tree or branch, launches no leaf and grows no plan; or `waiting on regrade-<n>` or `waiting on rebased-full-<n>` when that wait's slice ran out. Its wait record `.tdd/issue-<n>-pr-prepare-wait` names the conductor by the pid and start time `.tdd/conductor.pid` held: called again by that conductor it only waits again, rebasing, regrading, committing, pushing and writing no event twice and starting no second full run; under another it discards the record and starts from its first call. On exit 0 it writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-pr-prepare` (never committed), `rebased=0` or `rebased=1`, `rebased_from=` (empty when nothing was rebased) and `rebased_tip_suite=ci`, `local` or `none` (nothing rebased), and prints the same lines last. **Exit 0** every call clean; **3** a wait's slice ran out, so call it again; **2** bad arguments or a missing input, before any call; otherwise the stopping call's own code when neither 0 nor 3, and **1** for any other stop.
- `$S/pr-checks.sh <n>` — `DRAFT-PR.md` sub-step 1's checks after `pr-prepare.sh`, in one invocation, in this order: the lint run (`detect-test-command.sh --tier lint`; exit 3 runs nothing, exit 2 writes the could-not-be-read `### Lint` sentence; else `detach-command.sh start lint` and `wait lint --total "${VERI_LINT_TIMEOUT:-1800}"`, the `lint` event and the `### Lint` section in `.tdd/issue-<n>-lint.md`, a wait with no recorded exit writing its sentence unless `stop-requested.sh` prints `now`), the test-quality check (`test-quality.sh files <branch-base>`; exit 3 writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-test-quality` empty), `red-setup.sh worst`, `coupling.sh <branch-base>` (exit 3 then `refactor-owed.sh clear … coupling`), `refactor-candidates.sh` and `unwired.sh --accepted .tdd/issue-<n>-unwired-accepted <branch-base>`. It stops with exit 1, its last line `stopped at <check> <what>` naming the check, before the `develop-test-quality` launch (touched test files, or a recorded finding `VERI_TEST_QUALITY_FIXES` still owes a step), on a non-zero lint exit once its `### Lint` section is written and before any repair step is appended, on a coupling item (the `### Coupling` section written, a stale coupling row dropped, or, with no pair, `.tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md` changed since before `red-setup.sh worst`, so the conductor commits the row) and on an unwired finding. It launches no leaf, appends no ledger step, commits, pushes and readies nothing and renders no metrics (`metrics.sh` is `pr-ready-wait.sh`'s). For each call it prints one line naming the call and its exit code, never a suite's output file. A lint wait whose slice ran out prints `waiting on lint`; its wait record `.tdd/issue-<n>-pr-checks-wait` names the conductor, and called again by that conductor it only waits again, starting no second lint. On exit 0 it writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-pr-checks` (never committed) holding `candidates=` (the candidates issue's url, empty for none) and prints it last. **Exit 0** every check clean; **1** a stop for the conductor's judgement; **3** the lint's wait ran out, so call it again; **2** bad arguments or a missing `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight` or ledger, before any call, or an inner call that obtained no answer (a failing git call included), never that call's own code.
- `$S/pr-ready-wait.sh <n>` — `DRAFT-PR.md` sub-step 2's first block on its clean path, in one invocation: the run's record closed and pushed, then the PR readied and its CI read, so no records-only push follows the readied PR. A stacked run (`.tdd/issue-<n>-base` present), whose sub-step 2 runs neither block, is exit 2 before any call. Its calls: the PR url read as sub-step 2 reads it (`gh api 'repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls?head={owner}:{branch}'`; an empty answer is exit 2); `branch-suite.sh`, and when it prints `ci` and the ledger holds an unchecked `⟦full-suite⟧` line, `tick-ledger.sh .tdd/issue-<n>.md '⟦full-suite⟧'` until it exits 3, each such line's issue criterion read with `ledger-steps.sh criterion <k>` from the issue body and ticked with `tick-issue-criterion.sh`, and the `plan: the full suite is owed to CI (#<n>)` commit of the ledger, the ticks owed to CI and made before any CI wait, with no `.tdd/wait-ci.err` read; the `metrics-cut pr=<pr-url>` mark (`event.sh`), never the `done` mark, which waits for CI's green verdict; `metrics.sh <n> > .tdd/issue-<n>-metrics.md`, committed as `metrics: what solving #<n> cost (#<n>)` on exit 0, while any other exit commits nothing and is carried as `metrics=<code>`, not a stop, and the file such a render may leave is left out of the tree check that follows; a non-empty `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` just before the push stopping it at `2 tree-check` with exit 1; `push-branch.sh`; `pr-ready.sh <pr-number> <head-sha>` with its stderr to `.tdd/pr-ready.err` (any exit but 0 stops it, with no `pr-ready-failed` event written by it: that bail stays the block's), then `wait-ci.sh <pr-url> 540 "" <head-sha>` into `.tdd/wait-ci.out` and `.tdd/wait-ci.err`, whose exit 1 or 2 stops it before any `gh run view`, flake check, rebase, adjudication or ledger step, and whose exit 0 with a non-empty `.tdd/ci-flake.out` stops it with exit 1, so the block writes the `ci-flake` event. It writes no other event, posts no comment and makes no commit but the two above. For each call it prints one line naming the call and its exit code, then that call's stdout and stderr; what it sends to a file is not printed. The last line is `stopped at <where> <what>` at the first outcome that is not clean (`<where>` `2`; `<what>` the call with `exit <code>`, `tree-check`, or `no PR for <branch>`), after which it runs no later call, writes no `bail` event, calls neither `classify-bail.sh` nor `preflight-refused.sh`, posts no bail or park comment, resets nothing, launches no leaf and grows no plan; or `waiting on wait-ci.sh` when the wait's slice ran out. Its wait record `.tdd/issue-<n>-pr-ready-wait-wait` names the conductor by the pid and start time `.tdd/conductor.pid` held: called again by that conductor it only waits again on the head, with no second `metrics-cut` mark, commit, tick, push or readying, so the PR is readied once across repeated calls; under another it discards the record and starts from its first call. On exit 0 it writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-pr-ready-wait` (never committed), its `pr_url=`, `pr_number=` and `ci=green` lines, `metrics=<code>` when the render failed and `no_checks=1` when `.tdd/wait-ci.err` says the PR `has no checks` (the ticks owed to CI were then proved by nothing), and prints the same lines last. **Exit 0** CI green; **3** a wait's slice ran out, so call it again; **2** bad arguments, a missing `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight` or ledger, a stacked run, no PR for the branch, or a `git` or `gh` call that failed (no answer, whatever the tool's own code); otherwise the stopping call's own code when neither 0 nor 3, and **1** for any other stop.
- `$S/pr-finish.sh <n>` — `DRAFT-PR.md` sub-step 2's second block up to the refactor-owed render, on its clean path, in one invocation, once the first `wait-ci.sh` exited 0; the PR's title and body stay the conductor's. A stacked run (`.tdd/issue-<n>-base` present) is exit 2 before any call. Its calls: the PR url read as sub-step 2 reads it (an empty answer is exit 2), then the `done pr=<pr-url>` mark (`event.sh`, as the conductor of issue `<n>`), written here, once CI's first wait is green, so no reader of a run's end takes the run as finished while that wait is pending or after its conductor died in it, then `refactor-owed.sh render .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md`, its output written to `.tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed-render` (exit 3 an empty file). It makes no commit, no push and no CI wait, and writes no other event: the run's record was closed and pushed by `pr-ready-wait.sh` before readying. For each call it prints one line naming the call and its exit code, then that call's stdout and stderr; what it sends to a file is not printed. The last line is `stopped at <where> <what>` at the first outcome that is not clean (`<where>` `2`; `<what>` the call with `exit <code>`, or `no PR for <branch>`), after which it runs no later call, writes no `bail` event, calls neither `classify-bail.sh` nor `preflight-refused.sh`, posts no bail or park comment, resets nothing, launches no leaf and grows no plan. It makes no wait, so it never exits 3. On exit 0 it writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-pr-finish` (never committed), its `pr_url=` line and `refactor_owed_render=.tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed-render`, and prints the same lines last. **Exit 0** every call clean; **2** bad arguments, a missing `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight`, a stacked run, no PR for the branch, or a `git` or `gh` call that failed; otherwise the stopping call's own code when neither 0 nor 3, and **1** for any other stop.
- `$S/run-done.sh <n>` — `DRAFT-PR.md` sub-step 4 on its clean path, in one invocation. It never merges the pull request and calls nothing that could: no `gh pr merge`, no merge `gh api` call, no merge script. Its calls: `stop-requested.sh` (every exit goes on); a non-empty `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` just before the comment stopping it at `4 tree-check` with exit 1 and no comment posted, except that `.tdd/issue-<n>-metrics.md` is left out of it when `.tdd/issue-<n>-pr-ready-wait` holds a non-zero `metrics=` (the file a failed render left, which `pr-ready-wait.sh` carries rather than commits); `comment.sh <n> done` (any exit but 0 stops it); then, only when the first `stop-requested.sh` exited 0 or 2 (a stop was reported), `stop-requested.sh --clear`. For each call it prints one line naming the call and its exit code, then that call's stdout and stderr. The last line is `stopped at <where> <what>` at the first outcome that is not clean (`<where>` `4`; `<what>` the call with `exit <code>` or `tree-check`), after which it runs no later call, writes no `bail` event, calls neither `classify-bail.sh` nor `preflight-refused.sh`, posts no bail or park comment, resets nothing, launches no leaf and grows no plan. It makes no wait, so it never exits 3. On exit 0 it writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-run-done` (never committed), its `stop_requested=<the first ask's exit>` and `cleared=1` or `cleared=0` lines, and prints the same lines last. **Exit 0** every call clean; **2** bad arguments or a missing `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight`, before any call, or a `git` call that failed; otherwise the stopping call's own code when neither 0 nor 3, and **1** for any other stop.
- `$S/stop-settle.sh <n> <mode> [--drop]` — `STOPPING.md` steps 1–6 in one invocation, `<mode>` the one `stop-requested.sh` printed (`at-ledger`, `at-leaf` or `now`), in the worktree `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight` names.
