Its calls: `wait-leaf.sh <name>` on the leaf its own wait record names, when this conductor wrote it, and on each `.tdd/<name>.pid` whose process still runs `supervise.sh <name>` (0, 1 and 2 all go on); the interrupted rebase or merge check, `git rev-parse --git-path rebase-merge` and `rebase-apply` naming a directory that exists or `git rev-parse --git-path MERGE_HEAD` a file that exists, which records `at=unsettled`, resets and pushes nothing and names in the comment what a human must finish; otherwise `git status --porcelain`, whose non-empty answer stops it at `2 tree-check` with exit 1 before any reset unless `--drop` gives the conductor's judgement that the tree is the run's to settle, then `git reset --hard HEAD`, the changed tracked paths named in the comment as dropped and an untracked one, which the reset leaves, as left; `push-branch.sh`, whose exit 1 or 2 it carries into the comment as `push=rejected sha=<remote-sha>` (the sha its stderr names, `unknown` without one) and goes on; `at` derived from the ledger HEAD holds (`git ls-tree --name-only HEAD -- .tdd/issue-<n>.md`, then `git show HEAD:.tdd/issue-<n>.md`): `unplanned` when `git ls-tree` names none, `planned` with no step ticked, `green` while a ticked step still carries ` ⟂ refactor pending`, `step-done` otherwise, and `unsettled` over all four; the `stop mode=<mode> at=<at>` mark, `VERI_STEP` the step `ledger-steps.sh current-or-last-done` would print; `comment.sh <n> stopped`, with `--note-file .tdd/stop-settle-note.md` when it has something to add, which renders `metrics.sh <n>` into the comment after the mark and is the restart variant, with no operator wording and no resume command, when `.tdd/restart-pending` names a live pid; and `stop-requested.sh --clear`. It writes no `bail` event, calls no `classify-bail.sh`, writes and commits no metrics file, makes no commit, opens or readies no PR, relaunches nothing, and leaves `.tdd/conductor.pid` and the worktree in place. For each call it prints one line naming the call and its exit code, then that call's stdout and stderr, save the ledger's `git show`, of which only that line is printed; it never prints a leaf's `.out` or a suite's output file. The last line is `stopped at <step> <what>` at the first outcome that is not clean, `<step>` the `STOPPING.md` step, after which it runs no later call; or `waiting on <name>` when a leaf's wait ran out. Its wait record `.tdd/issue-<n>-stop-settle-wait` names the leaf and the conductor by the pid and start time `.tdd/conductor.pid` held: called again by the same conductor it only waits again, on that leaf first though its process has ended since, a record another conductor wrote is removed and its leaf not waited on, and a wait that returns removes the record. **Exit 0** the stop settled; **1** a stop on its `stopped at` line (a dirty tree without `--drop`); **3** a leaf wait's slice ran out, so call it again; **2** bad arguments or a missing `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight`, before any call, or any other call that fails (a `git` call, `event.sh`, `comment.sh` or `stop-requested.sh`), on a `stopped at` line naming it: no answer was obtained, never a clean negative.
- `$S/preflight-refused.sh <issue-number> <check> [<cause>]` — record a preflight refusal: write one `preflight-refused` event carrying the check, `cause=preflight-refused` and its classification to the run's event log (`.tdd/wt/<n>/.tdd/events.jsonl` under the main checkout), and post one issue comment headed `### ⛔ Preflight refused — <check>`. The optional third argument `<cause>` is the refusing check's own stderr line: when given, the event carries it as `detail=<cause>` and the comment body carries it under the heading; when absent, the event and the comment are exactly as without it. Exit **0** recorded; **3** the run's standing end mark is already a `preflight-refused` event, so nothing is written or posted; **2** a missing or empty argument, or no answer (a comment GitHub refuses, after the event is written). Preflight calls it on every refusal.
- `$S/pr-body.sh <pr-number> <body-file>` — write the run's body to PR `<pr-number>` from `<body-file>`, keeping every part of the PR's current body the run did not author (a `## Review notes` section an operator appended included) and rewriting only the run's own marked part; a current body with no part marked as the run's own is replaced by the `<body-file>`'s content as the run's marked part. The body is redacted before it is written, line by line: first each local path (the run's worktree and main clone become `<repo>`, a temp directory `<tmp>`, the home directory `~`), the host name `<host>` and the account name `<user>`, by the redaction `refs/veri/runs/<n>` applies to a carried journal line (`run_records.redact_local`; a line still holding one is replaced whole by `<redacted>`); then by the rule comments are redacted with (`comment.sh`): the token patterns and the values of the variables `VERI_REDACT_VARS` names become `[redacted]`. Exit **0** written; **2** bad arguments, or the current body could not be read (nothing written) or the write failed. The conductor calls it for every PR-body write.
- `$S/pr-ready.sh <pr-number> <sha>` — mark draft PR `<pr-number>` ready for review once `<sha>` is its head, by running `gh_pr_ready` (`veri/github.py`) behind a `/bin/sh` shim over `veri/pr_ready.py`, whatever shell calls it. Exit **0** marked ready, **2** bad arguments, or no answer from GitHub. The conductor calls it. It marks its run as stage `ready`: a `stage-start` before it and a `stage-end` after it whose `status` is its exit code, both carrying the run's issue (`VERI_ISSUE`, else `VERI_STATE_LABEL_ISSUE`, which the conductor exports); a call refused for its arguments, or run where there is no `.tdd/`, is not marked, and a line it cannot write never changes its exit code.
- `$S/restore-production.sh <test-path>...` — restore a worktree's production code to `HEAD`, keeping the named tests. A `/bin/sh` shim over `veri/restore_production.py`, run inside a worktree: it restores every tracked path except the named test paths to `HEAD` (`git checkout HEAD`) and removes every untracked, unignored file except the named test paths (`git clean`), so the tree holds no production change beyond `HEAD` while the named test paths are left exactly as they were. Exit **0** restored, **2** no argument (usage line on stderr) or git refused. The revise loop calls it before relaunching a red leaf, and `reroute.sh` on a `red`, `repair` or `missing` answer before launching that answer's red leaf.
- `$S/update-veri.sh [--consent yes|no]` — fast-forward veri's own clone to its upstream default branch, and only when the clone is on that branch with a clean tree. **Exit 0** = moved, **3** = already current, **1** = not allowed (the record or `VERI_SELF_UPDATE=0` says no, or the clone is off the default branch or dirty) or not a fast-forward, **2** = no answer. It honours the consent record at `~/.veri-tools/self-update` (`VERI_SELF_UPDATE_FILE` names another path), which `--consent yes|no` writes, and `VERI_SELF_UPDATE=0` turns it off. `run-conductor.sh` calls it before pinning a fresh run, never on a resume.
- `$S/test-ceiling.sh [--max <n>]` — measure the highest test-worker count this machine runs `make test` at with no case timeout (PRD 036.2). It runs `make test`'s full selection once per step, under the case timeouts `make test` applies (`VERI_PYTEST_TIMEOUT`, timeout marks, `PROCESS_CASE_TIMEOUT`), at `VERI_PYTEST_WORKERS=<w>` for w = 2, 4, 6, … up to the logical cores, or to `<n>`. With a cap on line 1 of `~/.veri-test-cap` (`VERI_TEST_CAP_FILE`, PRD 036.11) the ramp stops at the cap: its top is the least of the cores, `<n>` and the cap, an odd top included, and on exit 0 with no step timed out the printed line names which of the three bounded it; a cap file it cannot use is one stderr line and the ramp runs uncapped. A case failing a step by its per-case timeout is re-run alone with one worker: passing alone, the timeout was load and the ramp stops, the ceiling being the last step with none; timing out alone too, it is a hung case. Each step registers its workers under `~/.veri-pytest-legs` (`VERI_PYTEST_LEGS`) for its duration, as a leg would, and first waits, with one stderr line naming them, while any other live leg is registered. On exit 0 it prints the ceiling and replaces `~/.veri-test-ceiling` (`VERI_TEST_CEILING_FILE`) whole through a temporary file: the ceiling on line 1, then the date, the commit measured at, the load average at start, and one `<workers> <seconds> <timeouts>` line per step, then the two idle durations (PRD 036.10): `idle all <seconds>`, the ceiling step's, and `idle fast <seconds>`, one `make unit` it runs after the ramp at min(4, the ceiling) workers, registered as a step is. **Exit 0** the ceiling, the top step with no case timeout; **1** a load timeout at 2 workers, "no ceiling of at least 2"; **2** a bad argument, a hung case or one failing any other way (named), a suite run exiting non-zero with no failed case recorded, or a red `make unit` after the ramp (named). Exit 1 and 2 leave an earlier record unchanged. Exit 0 also removes `~/.veri-test-load` (`VERI_TEST_LOAD_FILE`), the T a load timeout lowered (PRD 036.7), a file it cannot remove being one stderr line; exit 1 and 2 leave it. `make ceiling` is its one entry point; nothing in the procedure, no other make target and no CI job runs it.
- `$S/push-branch.sh [--force-with-lease=<branch>:<sha>]` — push the branch. When `.tdd/rebase-lease` exists, the push is `git push --force-with-lease=<branch>:<sha> --set-upstream origin HEAD`, `<sha>` being the file's content and `<branch>` the checked-out branch, replacing the arguments the caller passed. A successful push removes the file; a failed push leaves it, so a later push still carries the lease. With no such file the push is as before. It marks its run as stage `push`: a `stage-start` before it and a `stage-end` after it whose `status` is its exit code, both carrying the run's issue (`VERI_ISSUE`, else `VERI_STATE_LABEL_ISSUE`, which the conductor exports); a call refused for its arguments, or run where there is no `.tdd/`, is not marked, and a line it cannot write never changes its exit code.
  After a push that exits 0 on a `tdd/<n>-<slug>` branch it also replaces origin's `refs/veri/runs/<n>`, a commit on no
  branch holding only what a reader restores: the run's `.tdd/events.jsonl` lines for issue `<n>` and those naming no
  issue, `.tdd/issue-<n>-skill-commit` and `.tdd/issue-<n>-timeout` (each pin read from the worktree, else from the main
  tree it sits under); records kept off the branch, since rebase-merge would carry them into `main`'s history. What
  travels is redacted, the local files left byte-identical, in every string of each carried line (decoded JSON, nested
  values and keys included) and in each pin, in two layers. The first replaces the named spellings readably: the
  worktree and main clone roots `<repo>`, then the temp directories (`$TMPDIR`, the system's) `<tmp>`, then the home
  directory `~`, each the longest first and ignoring case, in every named spelling (as given and resolved, with and
  without `/private` and `/System/Volumes/Data`, with `/` written `\/`, URL-encoded with and without `/` kept) and only
  up to a path boundary (`/Users/<name>2` is another path, left alone); then any other temp path (`/var/folders/…`,
  `/tmp`) at a path boundary `<tmp>`; then, as whole words ignoring case, the host's names `<host>` and the account's
  name `<user>`. The second is fail-closed: a string whose copy, URL-decoded (`+` read as a space) until it stops
  changing, then NFKC-normalized with zero-width characters and the soft hyphen dropped, escaped slashes and backslashes
  read as `/` and case-folded, still holds any of those paths or names is replaced whole by `<redacted>`, and so is one
  still changing after 50 decoding passes. A key whose redacted name another key already holds is suffixed (`~-2`,
  `<redacted-2>`…), so no value is lost. The host's names are
  `socket.gethostname()`, `platform.node()` and the `LocalHostName` and `ComputerName` of
  `/Library/Preferences/SystemConfiguration/preferences.plist` (read with `plistlib`; no process is started), each with
  its first DNS label, `.local` and `-` or space either way, never a form that is all digits or under 3 characters and
  never an IP-form name split into labels; the account's name is the home directory's last component and
  `getpass.getuser()`, when at least 3 characters. A journal line that is not JSON is not carried. The records commit is by `veri <veri@localhost>`, its
  author and committer dates in UTC (`+0000`). The push ends after `VERI_GIT_NETWORK_TIMEOUT` seconds. `metrics.sh <n>` reads that ref's events when the
  local journal has none for the issue, and `run-conductor.sh <n>`, on a first launch with no local
  `.tdd/issue-<n>-skill-commit`, pins the run to the commit the ref records, so a run resumed on another machine
  executes the snapshot it started on, but only when that commit is an ancestor of the skill clone's origin default
  branch as just fetched: `refs/veri/*` lies outside that branch's protection, so any other carried pin is named in one
  stderr line and ignored, and the launch behaves as a fresh one does, `update-veri.sh` included. The same first launch,
  with the carried skill pin accepted, restores the carried timeout pin into the run's worktree when the run has none, if its value is one the run's own
  reader accepts (a whole number of seconds >= 1); any other value is named in one stderr line and ignored, and the run
  settles its timeout as a fresh one does. Then it updates the issue's pointer comment (`pointer-comment.sh`), stamped with
  this push's time; after a failed push it does neither. A failure of either, any exception included, is one stderr
  line and never changes the exit code.
- `$S/pinned-scripts.sh <n>` — print the directory holding run `<n>`'s pinned scripts, under the run's worktree `.tdd/`. **Exit 0** = the directory, which exists. **Exit 2** = that directory is absent: the diagnostic names the pinned commit read from `.tdd/issue-<n>-skill-commit` and the recovery line `git archive <pin> skills/tdd/scripts`, or says no pin is recorded when there is no such file.
- `$S/full-tier-time.sh [--record <seconds>] <command>` — read or record a full-tier command's elapsed seconds in `.tdd/` under the main working tree (from a linked worktree too). `<command>` is the full-tier command; `--record <seconds>` records its time, replacing only that command's earlier record, except over a corrupt or unreadable record file, which it replaces whole with a fresh record (the other commands' entries are dropped). Recording also writes the epoch seconds it recorded at for that command into `.tdd/full-tier-time-at.json` beside the record, replacing only that command's entry (the whole file when it is unreadable); `baseline-full-tier.sh` reads it to tell a stale time. Without `--record` it reads: **exit 0** prints the recorded seconds, **exit 3** that command has no record, **exit 2** an unreadable record or bad arguments.
- `$S/baseline-full-tier.sh <issue> <full-tier-command>` — settle the preflight full-tier decision. It prints `reuse <seconds>` when the run's mode is `reuse`, `full-tier-time.sh` holds a recorded time for `<full-tier-command>` and its recorded-at in `.tdd/full-tier-time-at.json` is no more than `VERI_FULL_TIER_MAX_AGE_DAYS` days (default 7) before now, and `run` otherwise (mode `run`; `reuse` with no record for that command; or `reuse` with a recorded-at older than that or missing, when it also writes one stderr line naming the command, the age in whole days or `unknown`, and the knob's value); exit 0 both ways. The mode is the run's pinned value in `.tdd/issue-<issue>-baseline-full-tier` beside the full-tier-time record (the main checkout's `.tdd/`) when that file exists, else `VERI_BASELINE_FULL_TIER` (default `reuse`), so a resumed run keeps the launch's choice; `VERI_FULL_TIER_MAX_AGE_DAYS` is read at each call, never pinned. **Exit 0** printed; **exit 2** fewer than two arguments (usage on stderr), an unreadable record, or under `reuse` a `VERI_FULL_TIER_MAX_AGE_DAYS` that is not a whole number ≥ 1 (naming it and its value). The conductor calls it in preflight step 3 and follows its output.
- `$S/baseline-full-tier.sh --green <issue> <tree> <fast-command> <full-command>` — settle whether preflight may skip its baseline on a local record: it prints `skip` when this machine records a green baseline under the same key, and `run` otherwise. The key is `<tree>` (the tested tree's `git rev-parse HEAD^{tree}`), both commands, the run's skill commit pin `.tdd/issue-<issue>-skill-commit` and a sha256 of `requirements-dev.txt`, both read from the main checkout; the record is `.tdd/baseline-green.json` there, and an absent, corrupt or differently keyed record, an entry that is not green, or an empty `<tree>` answers `run`. Exit 0 both ways; 2 a wrong argument count.
- `$S/baseline-full-tier.sh --record-green <issue> <tree> <fast-command> <full-command>` — record a green baseline under that key, as preflight does once its baseline passed; never after a red one. Exit 0 recorded; 2 a wrong argument count.
- `$S/rebased-tip-suite.sh` — settle where the rebased tip's full suite runs. It prints `ci` or `local`, one line, from three inputs in this order: a set, non-empty `VERI_REBASED_TIP_SUITE` is printed as given; else `VERI_CI_ON_PR` `1` prints `ci` and `0` prints `local`, or, when it is unset, the target repository's `CLAUDE.md` `veri: ci on pr: 0` or `veri: ci on pr: 1` line does the same; else the working tree's `.github/workflows/` decides, `ci` when a `.yml` or `.yaml` workflow there triggers on `pull_request` or `pull_request_target`, or could not be read in full (an unread workflow counts as a trigger), and `local` otherwise, a missing directory included. It takes no arguments and reads the directory it is run in, so it runs from the run's worktree root. **Exit 0** printed; **exit 2** a `veri: ci on pr:` line whose value is neither `0` nor `1` with `VERI_CI_ON_PR` unset, or a `VERI_REBASED_TIP_SUITE` that is neither `local` nor `ci`, or, when the mode is not explicit, a `VERI_CI_ON_PR` that is neither `0` nor `1` (one line on stderr naming the variable). The conductor calls it in the Draft PR step and follows its output.
- `$S/branch-suite.sh` — settle whether CI stands in for the branch's local full-suite run. It prints `ci` or `local`, one line, from three inputs in this order: a set, non-empty `VERI_BRANCH_SUITE` is printed as given; else `VERI_CI_ON_PR` `1` prints `ci` and `0` prints `local`, or, when it is unset, the target repository's `CLAUDE.md` `veri: ci on pr: 0` or `veri: ci on pr: 1` line does the same; else the working tree's `.github/workflows/` decides, `ci` when a `.yml` or `.yaml` workflow there triggers on `pull_request` or `pull_request_target`, or could not be read in full (an unread workflow counts as a trigger), and `local` otherwise, a missing directory included. It takes no arguments and reads the directory it is run in, so it runs from the run's worktree root. **Exit 0** printed; **exit 2** a `veri: ci on pr:` line whose value is neither `0` nor `1` with `VERI_CI_ON_PR` unset, or a `VERI_BRANCH_SUITE` that is neither `local` nor `ci`, or, when the mode is not explicit, a `VERI_CI_ON_PR` that is neither `0` nor `1` (one line on stderr naming the variable and its value).
- `$S/ready-check.sh [--no-suites] [--no-permissions] [repo-root]` — report whether a project has what veri needs to run (`make ready-check` calls it; preflight's step 2 calls it with `--no-suites`, which prints no `<tier> tier run` row and runs no test tier, and with `--no-permissions` too while its record is current, which prints none of the permission rows — `token scopes`, `label <VERI_PRIORITY_LABEL's value>`, `label bug`, `actions read`, `run-state ignore`, `push dry-run`, `bypassPermissions` — and makes none of their probes, while RD7's `gh auth`, `push access` and `label <VERI_LABEL's value>` rows still run). It reads the repo at `repo-root` (default: the main working tree, from a linked worktree too) and prints one `ok    <row>` or `fail  <row>: <fix>` line per check, in order: the fast and full test tiers and the one-test-file runner (`$S/detect-test-command.sh --tier`; `one test file` fails naming the `veri: one test file: <command>` CLAUDE.md line), a workflow triggering on `pull_request` (or `VERI_CI_ON_PR` set, or a `veri: ci on pr: 0` or `1` line in the repo's `CLAUDE.md`, the row's fix naming both) and one triggering on `push`, both read at the default branch's tip (`origin/HEAD`, else `main`), a tracked test file, `.tdd/` git-ignored, `gh auth status`, push access (`gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}`), the `VERI_LABEL` label (`gh label list`), one row each for the run-state labels `pr-ready`, `bailed`, `parked` and `stopped` (`gh label list --limit 1000`; a missing one fails with "create the `<label>` label in the repository", since veri never creates them), `token scopes` (the `X-OAuth-Scopes` header of `gh api -i user`: `repo`, and `workflow` too when `git remote get-url --push origin` is HTTPS; a `gh api -i user` answered with a non-2xx status fails the row naming that status line, and one that prints nothing fails it with "no answer was obtained"), `label <VERI_PRIORITY_LABEL's value>` and `label bug` (`gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/labels/<name>`, the name URL-encoded: an HTTP 404 fails with `gh label create <name>`, any other failure with "no answer was obtained"), `actions read` (`gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/runs?per_page=1`), `run-state ignore` (`.gitignore` and the common git directory's `info/exclude` each carry veri's run-state rule or are writable; a file carrying the rule only in part, or out of order, fails the row naming it, its fix the one `grep -nF` command that shows the rule's lines there), `push dry-run` (`git push --dry-run --no-verify origin HEAD:refs/heads/veri-permission-probe-<random>`, so no `pre-push` hook runs, under `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and, when `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` and `GIT_SSH` are unset and `core.sshCommand` is not configured, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND="ssh -o BatchMode=yes"`, killed at `VERI_GH_TIMEOUT`; a failure carries git's last stderr line), `bypassPermissions` (one paid haiku call, about $0.004: `claude -p --model haiku --permission-mode bypassPermissions --output-format json`, `CLAUDE_BIN` when set, started with `strip_session_env` applied as a leaf's is, in a scratch directory under `.tdd/` removed again on every exit, asked to write one nonce file with a plain Bash command, one with Write, and `shape-probe.txt` with one multi-line Bash command using `$'…'` quoting and a `${<var>/#/…}` pattern substitution; `ok` iff all three files hold the nonce (the shape file as its first line) and `permission_denials` is empty, an exit 0 or `is_error` false proving nothing by itself; a failing row quotes the denied tools and names `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode/managed-settings.json`, `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json` and the user and project `permissions.deny`, and, when `shape-probe.txt` is missing or wrong, `permissions.ask` and `permissions.blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories`; no answer within 300 s, or within what remains of the call's deadline when that is less, fails it saying no answer was obtained), `subagent permissions` (read from the merged `permissions` of `~/.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json`, a file that is missing, not JSON or not an object skipped; `ok` when any sets `defaultMode` `bypassPermissions`, else iff `permissions.allow` holds `Bash(git *)`, `Bash(<full-tier command>)` when the full tier resolved and `Bash(<scripts dir>/*)`; a failing row names the rules to add), bash 5+ and the Python floor; then a `<tier> tier run (<secs>s)` row for each of the fast and full tiers that resolved, none for a tier that resolved to nothing. Each such tier runs once, in a detached worktree of the default branch's tip at `.tdd/ready-<tier>` of the main working tree, through `$S/detach-command.sh start ready-<tier>` and `wait`, the two tiers in turn; one deadline, 560 s from the call's start, bounds both waits and the `bypassPermissions` probe together; the `gh` and `git` reads of the other rows are not counted against it, each bounded only by `VERI_GH_TIMEOUT` or by nothing. When the deadline has passed before a tier would start, it starts none, and the last line is `waiting on ready-<tier> (not started, …)` naming every tier not started, exit 3. A default branch's tip that does not resolve to a commit, or a `git worktree add` that fails, fails the row saying no answer was obtained and starts nothing; the row is `ok` iff the command exits 0, otherwise it fails carrying the exit code and the output's last non-empty line (a run that died without recording an exit fails saying no answer was obtained), and its wall time, in whole seconds from the start to the recorded exit, never fails it. A red tier's whole output is kept at `.tdd/ready-<tier>.out` in the main working tree; a green one leaves no such file, removing one an earlier run kept. When a tier is still running at the wait's bound, the last line is `waiting on ready-<tier>`, with no `ready` or `not ready` line, every tier worktree stays in place, and a re-run waits on that run instead of starting it again — only when `git worktree list` names the worktree and the record it holds (start time, tip commit, command) names the default branch's tip and the tier's command of now. A tier worktree is removed only when its run recorded an exit, or provably died: its pid file's pid answers no more, or names a process whose start time is not the one recorded on the file's second line. One with no exit and no pid file may be running, so it is kept and its row fails naming it; a reused one whose run died fails its row saying no answer was obtained. A stale tier worktree of its own whose run is over is removed and run fresh, one whose run still goes or may fails the row, and anything else at that path fails the row naming it, untouched. A worktree whose suite is or may be running stays, record and all, whatever way the call ends — an exception or interrupt after `git worktree add` included, and a `detach-command.sh wait` exit 2. Two rows may read neither `ok` nor `fail`: `token scopes` reads `not verifiable` when `gh api -i user` answers with no `X-OAuth-Scopes` header (a fine-grained token), and `actions read` reads `skipped` when the default branch's tip has no `.github/workflows/`; each counts as no fail. A `gh` call that fails or prints unusable output fails its row; it never crashes. It only reads, but for the tier worktrees, which it removes again on every exit but 3, save one whose suite is or may be running, the `bypassPermissions` probe's scratch directory under `.tdd/`, which it removes again on every exit, and the kept `.tdd/ready-<tier>.out`: no write to the tree, index, refs or GitHub, and every `gh` call of the token, selection-label and Actions rows is a REST read. The last line is `ready`, `not ready — <n> rows to fix` or `waiting on ready-<tier>`. **Exit 0** = every row holds (`ready`). **Exit 1** = at least one row fails: a clean negative, the report is the answer. **Exit 2** = no answer: more than one argument, an option other than those two (or one given twice), or `repo-root` not a git repository. **Exit 3** = a tier is still running: call again with the same argument. A `$S/detect-test-command.sh` exiting 2 for a tier is not exit 2: that tier's row fails with "no answer was obtained", so the check exits 1.
- `$S/status.sh [--json] [--scope <name>] [--watch [seconds]] | --report | --recover dev [--lane <lane>]` — render every run under the main working tree. `--report` (alone) prints the `/vstatus` report: the sections Landed, Stack, Running, Queue and Waiting, from local files only and with no `gh` call where no stack branch is named, the queue read from the cached snapshot `.tdd/report-cache.json`. When that snapshot is stale (older than `VERI_SNAPSHOT_MAX_AGE` seconds, default 60) and no refresh is running, it starts `report-cache.sh refresh` detached (its own session, no stdin, stdout or stderr), so a caller capturing the report never waits for it. Waiting does not list a PR as ready to merge when a commit subject on local `main` ends in `(#N)`, N its issue or PR number: that PR has landed. Where `stack-branch.sh` names a stack, a subject on `origin/<stack>` counts too, since a joined run is not waiting for a merge; where the stack or its subjects could not be read, such a PR is listed as `merge state unknown: <why>`, never as ready to merge and never left out. The **Stack** section is printed between Landed and Running only where `stack-branch.sh` exits 0: the line `<stack>: joined <j>, tested <t>, landed <l> in 24h`, where *joined* is the issues named by the subjects of `origin/<default>..origin/<stack>`, *tested* those at or below `stack-green.sh`'s commit (0 when it exits 3) and *landed* the issues named by `Closes` in this repository's `<stack>-slice-*` pull requests merged into the default branch in the last 24 hours; then `tracking: <url>` while the stack's tracking pull request is open (read, never opened), and `frozen: <sha> <run-url>` when `stack-red.sh` exits 0. A figure that could not be read prints `?`, never `0`, its reason on stderr; the rest of the section still prints, and the exit code is unchanged. `--json` (without `--watch`) carries the same under a `stack` key: `branch`, `joined`, `tested`, `landed`, `tracking_pr` (`false` when none is open) and `frozen` (`{sha, run_url}`, `false` when `stack-red.sh` exits 3), `null` for what could not be read, and no key where no stack is named. Waiting also lists each `.tdd/decisions.jsonl` record (see `decision-log.sh`) whose `at` is at or after the time in `.tdd/status-last`, or every one when that file is missing, as `decided: <question> → <decision> (rule: <rule>)`, or `owner decided: …` for one recorded by the owner: one line each, a character that does not print (a newline, an escape) written as its escape, each field cut at 300 characters with `…`. The time written to `.tdd/status-last` is taken before the log is read, so a decision at a boundary may be listed twice, never missed. A line of that log that is not a decision record (its `at` more than 300 seconds in the future included) is **exit 2** naming `<path>:<line>`, and a log that cannot be read is **exit 2** naming it and why; either way no report is printed and `.tdd/status-last` is unmoved. **Exit 0** printed, **2** an unknown option (a usage line on stderr), **3** no runs (without `--report`).
  The two `status.sh` modes that read the run journals, `--report` and `--recover`, skip a torn (unparseable) line in a run's journal, still reads the rest of that journal and every other journal, and prints `status.sh: skipped torn line <path>:<n>` to stderr.
  `status.sh --recover dev [--lane <lane>]` prints the dev session's recovery report, in order, the sections Runs, Bailed, PRs, Merge lock, State, Pending decisions, Pending messages, Questions, Unasked questions and Answered questions, `none` under an empty one. Each regular `.tdd/questions/<lane>-*.md` (`main-*` with no lane) is listed, by its `.tdd/questions/<name>` path and sorted by name, in exactly one of the last three: `Answered questions` when it has an `## Answer` line, whatever else it holds; else `Questions` when it has an `## Asked <session-id>` line (a one-word id after one space: the question was asked) or cannot be read; else `Unasked questions` (written, never asked). A directory or dangling link named like a question file is skipped. `State` prints the last recorded `n`, `lane` and `auto` (`auto on` or `auto off`; no `auto` line when none was recorded), then each timed N change and order override, and `no state file` when the role's state file is missing (distinct from `none`, an existing empty file), and a `.tdd/wt/<dir>` with no ledger is skipped from Runs, not a crash. Exit **0** printed, **2** a refused GitHub read or a role other than `dev`.
- `$S/report.sh --report` — print the PRD report from local files only (`.tdd/report-cache.json` in the working tree and `.tdd/report-owed.json` in the main working tree, read and written there whichever directory the script runs in; no `gh` call, no model): first a `snapshot age: <n> hours` line, then one block per active PRD in ascending PRD number, a PRD being the lowest-numbered issue titled `PRD <nnn>`. Each block is a `PRD <nnn> (#<issue>) — <title>` line, then `solved:` (closed children, and how many closed in the last 7 days), `left:` (solvable now, waiting on which open blockers, held by no lane), `owed:` (the stored answer, or `stale` when the PRD issue changed since it was stored) and `ETA:` (a range in days from the 7-day closing rate of the lanes holding the PRD's open children, or `no rate yet` below 3 closed issues, or `no lane`). **Exit 0** printed; **exit 2** with its usage line on stderr for no argument or an unknown one, and naming `.tdd/report-cache.json` when that snapshot is missing. Two helper modes serve `/vreport`: `--stale` prints the numbers of the PRDs whose stored answer is stale, and `--store-owed <prd> <file>` stores the answer for a PRD (exit 2 when the PRD is not in the snapshot), replacing the file whole through a temporary file renamed over it; it exits 2, naming the answer file and leaving the stored file unchanged, when the answer is not a JSON object holding `count`, a whole number of 0 or more, and `sections`, a list of strings. With no stored file, `--stale` lists every active PRD and exits 0. A stored answer in an older shape, one with no `answer` field, counts as stale: `owed:` prints `stale` for its PRD and `--stale` lists it.
- `$S/reject-breakdown.sh < <dated reports>` — break the verifier REJECTs of a set of committed metrics reports down per week (#4568). Stdin holds one `<YYYY-MM-DD> <report path>` line per report; a report carries no date of its own, so the caller passes its newest commit's author date. Per week, dated by its Monday, it prints `week <monday>: REJECT per verify <pct> (<rejects> of <verifies>)`, summing the PASS and REJECT columns of each report's `#### By phase` table; then one `  phase <phase>: <rejects> of <verifies>` line per phase with a verdict; then one `  reason <phase> <class>: <rejects>` line per reason class, green REJECTs as `check <k>` from the report's `Green rejects by check:` line and the rest of each phase's REJECTs as `unclassified`. **Exit 0** = printed, **2** = a line not `<YYYY-MM-DD> <path>`, or a report unreadable or with no `#### By phase` table, named by its line number, **3** = stdin names no report. A maintenance utility the procedure never calls.
- `$S/near-duplicates.sh <commit>` — print where code added between `<commit>^` and `HEAD` repeats code elsewhere in the files `<commit>` touched (#4582). It prints one `<file>:<first>-<last> <file>:<first>-<last>` line per pair, the added block first, for each maximal run of 3 or more consecutive lines added in that span that repeats, ignoring whitespace and identifier names, in the non-test files `<commit>` touched (`refactor-touched.sh`'s list, read as they are at `HEAD`); a line holding no letter and no digit does not count toward the 3. It only reports. Exit 0 with pairs printed, 3 with none (not a failure), 2 with a usage line on stderr for a missing or unresolvable `<commit>`.
- `$S/report-cache.sh refresh` — rebuild the snapshot `report.sh` and `/vstatus` read, `.tdd/report-cache.json` in the main clone's tree (the one a worktree's `.git` file points to): it lists every issue, open and closed, with `gh issue list`, and reads the comments of PRD-titled issues and of issues closed in the last 7 days. A snapshot with `cache_format` 2 and a `fetched_at` is refreshed incrementally, fetching only issues updated since it; a missing, unreadable (it says so on stderr) or older-format one is rebuilt whole. It also records `landed`, every annotated tag cut since the last status time, each a record of the tag `name`, its `date` and the tag annotation `subject`, and `since`, that last status time in epoch seconds (`.tdd/status-last`; with no such file, or one that cannot be read, `since` is 0 and `landed` lists every tag). The file is replaced atomically, so a reader never sees a half-written snapshot. **Exit 0** a snapshot written; **exit 2**, naming `gh issue list`, when `gh` gave no answer; **exit 2** printing `usage: report-cache.sh refresh` for no subcommand or an unknown one. When the one search for the PRD-titled issues' comments gets no answer, it lists those issues' creation times and reads their comments in `created:<first>..<last>` windows of `VERI_COMMENT_CHUNK` issues each (default 25, also when set empty; any value but a whole number >= 1 is **exit 2** naming the variable and its value, before any `gh` call). A window that gets no answer, or a comments search or creation-time listing that returns 1000 rows (the search's cap), is **exit 2** with no snapshot written.
- `$S/dispatch.sh [--target <N>] | --help` (`<N>` a whole number >= 0) — the operator's dispatcher: it launches the next solvable issue into a free slot once the previous launch's ledger exists, appending `launched <n> <time>` to `.tdd/dispatch.log`, and it waits for this repository's queued merges (those whose PR is in the repository the main working tree's `origin` names; with no readable `origin` it waits for every queued merge of this clone). It prints one of `done <n> <pr-url>`, `bail <n>`, `silent-exit <n>`, `merge-failed <pr-url>`, `stalled <n>` (`VERI_DISPATCH_STALL`, default 3600 s), `queue-empty`, or `queue-blocked <pr-url>…`: the closing lines, each of which ends the dispatch. A run's exit is reported even when it happened between two `dispatch.sh` calls (#4605): at its first poll a call also watches the runs `.tdd/dispatch.log` names after the mark kept in the main working tree's `.tdd/dispatch-notified` (`logged <k>`, the count of `launched` lines when the first call that found no mark recorded it, watching none of them), each once, and prints a `done`, `bail` or `silent-exit` line for one that ended before that poll, before launching into the slot it freed; such a run whose worktree is gone is dropped from the watch, never reported vanished, and a blank or malformed log line is skipped. Each printed exit is recorded there as `exited <n> <k>` (`<k>` the log's `launched` line count then), so a later call neither prints it again nor calls it `stalled` unless the log names a launch of `<n>` after line `<k>` or the call saw the run live; a resumed run's next exit is printed. Three more lines are advice, printed while dispatch keeps watching, never closing lines: `stretched <issue> <tier> <seconds>s, idle <idle>s`, `queueing <k> waiting for T=<t>, <w> workers in use: a lower N would shorten the wait` and `t-lowered <old> <new> <cases>`; they no longer close a poll, and the exit line below applies to closing lines only. The `queueing` line (PRD 036.9) is advice printed after a poll's launch decision that kept watching, never a closing line, for a poll whose live waiter entries under `~/.veri-pytest-legs.waiting` (`VERI_PYTEST_WAITERS`, legs waiting for T) were above 0 at each of the last `VERI_QUEUE_POLLS` polls (default 3; a whole number of at least 2, else a usage error, **exit 2**, before any poll) and grew from the oldest to the newest; the counts and the fact that it was printed are kept in the main working tree's `.tdd/dispatch-notified`, so a restarted dispatch neither forgets them nor prints it again until a poll counts no waiter; it changes nothing, and `core-wait` events and wait markers are never counted. The `stretched` line (PRD 036.10) is advice printed while dispatch keeps watching, never a closing line, after the `queueing` rule, for the first `test-stretch` event in a live run's journal whose run and tier it has not reported before: once per run and tier, not per test run, the `stretched <issue> <tier>` key kept in the same `.tdd/dispatch-notified`; advice only, it changes nothing. At each poll that does not end it, dispatch writes its lane's test budget entry under `~/.veri-test-budget` (`VERI_TEST_BUDGET_DIR`), named `<lane>@<16 hex of the main working tree's path hash>` (the lane `main` outside a lane): line 1 the need, max(`<N>`, live conductors) × `VERI_CONDUCTOR_TEST_WORKERS` (default 2; anything but a whole number of at least 1 is a usage error, **exit 2**), line 2 its expiry, now plus `VERI_TEST_BUDGET_TTL` seconds (default 3600); the pytest leg narrows T to the sum of the unexpired entries' needs, never above the ceiling or below 2 (PRD 036.5). The `t-lowered` line (PRD 036.7) is advice printed while dispatch keeps watching, never a closing line, after the `stretched` rule, when a poll finds a lowering on record in `~/.veri-test-load` (`VERI_TEST_LOAD_FILE`: the lowered T, the lowering's time, the T it lowered from and the window's timed-out cases) newer than the last it reported; it records that time in the same `.tdd/dispatch-notified`, so a restarted dispatch never prints it again, and it only reads the file, never acting on it. The `queue-blocked <pr-url>…` line is a closing line, printed at a poll that read a non-empty queue in which every solvable issue was skipped for overlap while no conductor is live: it names each distinct blocking run draft PR, sorted, once per set of blocking PRs, the line kept in the same `.tdd/dispatch-notified`, and it ends the dispatch (**exit 0**), since with no run live no other closing line would come to end the watch and the operator would never read it; a restarted dispatch whose set `.tdd/dispatch-notified` already holds prints nothing and keeps watching, printing it again only once the set differs. `--target 0` holds dispatch: it keeps watching and reporting exits (`done`, `bail`, `silent-exit`, `merge-failed`, `stalled`) and launches nothing, never printing `queue-empty` merely because it launches nothing; re-arming dispatch with a positive `--target` resumes launching. **Exit 0**, except **3** for `queue-empty` (the exit line applies to closing lines only: the advice lines `queueing`, `stretched` and `t-lowered` end nothing and set no exit code); **exit 2** for a usage error, an unreadable queue or a vanished run (`run <n> vanished: no worktree left behind` on stderr): a run whose conductor is not live, whose `.tdd/wt/<n>` is absent and for which no conductor process is alive, on two consecutive polls, then still absent when dispatch re-checks it after one more poll interval (the grace re-check); a run whose own conductor process is alive, still in preflight with no worktree or `conductor.pid` written yet, is never reported vanished (another run's conductor process does not count), and stays under watch (#3693); a run whose `.tdd/wt/<n>` is back at that re-check is never reported vanished and stays under watch, live again or reported by its exit line, and a later absence starts the two polls afresh. For each run it launches it starts `restart-conductor.sh` beside `run-conductor.sh`, and none for a failed launch. A run whose conductor is gone while its worktree holds a `.tdd/restart-pending` marker is a planned restart, not an exit: dispatch defers it while the marker's restarter lives (an empty or unreadable marker counts as living), keeps it under watch every pass, and while the restarter lives the run holds its slot and is counted once, even beside a live run, so dispatch launches only while fewer than `<N>` runs hold a slot; a run whose own conductor process is alive in preflight, before its worktree or `conductor.pid` exists, likewise holds its slot and is counted once (#3693); a run whose `.tdd/wt/<n>/.tdd/conductor.pid` names a pid that answers `kill -0`, with the start time recorded on that file's second line matching (or none recorded), counts as a live conductor and holds a slot whatever its ledger or journal state, hand-launched runs included (the live-conductor reading `watch-runs.sh` shares); it reports `done`, `bail` or `silent-exit` (per the issue's last trusted comment, or the trusted one before it when that starts with neither heading: a comment is trusted when its `author_association` is `OWNER` or `COLLABORATOR`, or its author's login is the authenticated `gh` user's, the association alone deciding when that login cannot be read (so when GitHub reports veri's own comments with an association other than `OWNER` or `COLLABORATOR`, such as `MEMBER`, veri does not see its own outcome comment then: dispatch prints `silent-exit <n>`, and `run-conductor.sh`, missing its own `### ✅ Done`, bails `needs-user`); an untrusted comment is skipped as if absent, so a thread whose only outcome comment is untrusted prints `silent-exit <n>`) once no conductor is live and the restarter is dead, except that `silent-exit <n>` is held until the run has been absent from the live conductors for 30 continuous seconds by dispatch's own clock (a fixed 30 s, no environment variable); a run that comes back live and is absent again gets a fresh 30 s window, and `done` and `bail` print at once; and a marker that never clears ends as `stalled <n>` after `VERI_DISPATCH_STALL`, only while the run's issue is open: the stall check skips a run dir whose issue is CLOSED, printing no `stalled <n>` line for it. Dispatch reads the seen issues' states with one `gh issue list --state all` read per poll, never one read per run dir; a failed state read exits 2 with a stderr line. It skips a solvable issue whose body names a path that an open run draft PR (a draft on a `tdd/` branch whose head is in this repository; a fork's draft on a `tdd/` branch is not one) changes, on any machine, never counting the issue's own run draft PR (a branch `tdd/<n>-…` for that issue `<n>`, so a resumed run is relaunched), unless that PR's issue carries the `parked` label (a parked run rebases when it resumes), and launches the next one with no such overlap; it prints one line per skip naming the issue, the path and the PR, once per issue, path and PR for the life of the process, and never reports `queue-empty` while skipped issues remain. While every solvable issue overlaps, it re-reads the open-PR list at most once per `VERI_DISPATCH_OVERLAP_POLL` seconds (default 60; a non-integer is a usage error, **exit 2**); with a free slot, an empty queue and a live conductor it reads the queue at most once per `VERI_DISPATCH_OVERLAP_POLL` seconds too, so an empty queue is not re-read every poll. A failed overlap read (PR list, PR diff or issue body) that answers an HTTP 5xx or a connection error is retried (up to 12 retries, 13 reads in all, `VERI_DISPATCH_RETRY_SLEEP` seconds apart, default 5; a non-integer is a usage error, **exit 2**, before any read) before it exits 2 naming the failure, any other failed read exits 2 at once. A rate-limited overlap read (PR list, PR diff or issue body), a 403/429 marked by `retry-after` or `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0`, is retried within the bounded budget `VERI_GH_REFUSAL_RETRIES` (default 3) / `VERI_GH_REFUSAL_SLEEP` (default 60 s), waiting for the reset between passes, and a read still rate-limited past that budget exits 2 naming the read; the stall check's issue-states read exits 2 on its first rate limit. A rate-limited queue read waits for the reset, and so do the exit-comment read (the issue's comments, read to print `done`, `bail` or `silent-exit`) and `pr_state`'s pull read (a finished queued merge's state): each waits until the core reset time `gh api rate_limit` reports (sleeping no time when that is already past) and reads again, under the one budget `VERI_GH_REFUSAL_RETRIES` (default 3 waits); a read still rate-limited after the budget exits **2** naming the read, never an open-ended sleep, and so does an exit-comment or pull read whose reset time cannot be obtained; a rate-limited queue read whose reset time cannot be obtained instead prints one stderr line saying so, reads no queue for `VERI_DISPATCH_OVERLAP_POLL` seconds and then reads it again, never exiting on it. With no `--target`, `<N>` and the launch order come from the dev session's role state file, `.tdd/role-dev.state` in the main working tree, or `.tdd/role-dev-<lane>.state` when `VERI_LABEL` is `ready-for-agent:<lane>` (the file `role-state.sh dev --lane <lane>` writes; see `role-state.sh`): `<N>` is its last `n=` line, replaced while a `timed-n <id> <time> <value> until <ISO time>` line has not yet expired (the last such line in the file that is still live wins); the file is re-read at each slot decision, so a change takes effect without restarting dispatch; and each `override <id> <time> <text>` line whose text names an issue number (the first number in it) that is among the launchable ones moves that issue to the front of the launch order (an override naming an issue that is not launchable is ignored). With no `--target` and the file missing or unreadable, it exits **2** naming the file on stderr and launches nothing; a state file with no `n=` line, a non-numeric `n=`, a malformed `timed-n` line (it needs `timed-n <id> <time> <N> until <time>`), or an `override` line of fewer than 4 fields (it needs `override <id> <time> <text>`) likewise exits **2**, the message naming the file or the line. An issue named by several `override` lines moves to the front once, by the first of them. `--help` prints the usage on stdout and exits 0.
- `$S/comment.sh <issue> <kind> [--note-file <path>] [--pr]` — render a routine comment of that kind from the run's journal and ledger, append the note file's text when given, post it through `github.py`, and emit a `comment` event (`kind=`, `url=`). Every comment and body veri's scripts hand `gh` (through `github.py`) is redacted before `gh` is called: each match of `gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{36,}`, `github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{22,}`, `sk-ant-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}` and `AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}`, and each occurrence of the value (8 characters or more) of a variable `VERI_REDACT_VARS` names (default `GH_TOKEN GITHUB_TOKEN ANTHROPIC_API_KEY CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`), becomes `[redacted]`; a body with nothing to redact is posted unchanged. A token split across a line break, a markdown or CI-log line prefix or a zero-width character is still found and replaced whole; a token that ends a line takes nothing of the next line with it when it has its documented length (`gh[pousr]_` 36, `github_pat_` 82, `AKIA` 16 characters after the prefix, a listed value exactly), while an `sk-ant-` key, whose length varies, may take up to 101 minus its own length of the next line's first characters. Not covered: the three `gh api` calls the conductor makes from prose, `SKILL.md`'s draft-PR create (its `WIP:` title and its body) and `DRAFT-PR.md`'s PR title. Every comment veri posts (each kind below, `run-conductor.sh`'s needs-user bail, `park-issue.sh`, `preflight-refused.sh`, `pointer-comment.sh`, `record-answer.sh`, the stack-culprit and stack-fix comments and the 2b start comment, which `plan_publish` posts) also goes through `github.published`: the redaction `run_records.py` applies to carried run records, anchored to the directory it runs in (the run's worktree and main clone as `<repo>`, temp directories as `<tmp>`, the home directory as `~`, host names as `<host>`, the account name as `<user>`, a line still holding one replaced whole by `<redacted>`), then any other absolute path under `/Users`, `/home`, `/tmp`, `/var/folders` or `/private` as `<path>`. Kinds: `step-done` (heading naming the last done step; takes `--note-file`), `red` (takes `--note-file`; reads the red leaf's report `.tdd/develop-red.out`), `verify-red` (reads `.tdd/verdict-red.exit` for its PASS or REJECT heading, and `.tdd/verify-red.reason`), `green` (takes `--note-file`; reads `.tdd/develop-green.out`), `verify-green` (reads `.tdd/verdict-green.exit` and `.tdd/verify-green.reason`: `### 🔎 verify-green — SKIPPED (switched off)`, with no reason appended, when that file reads `skipped`, as for every verify kind: a verdict file reading `skipped` renders `SKIPPED (switched off)`, never `PASS`), `refactor` (takes `--note-file`; reads `.tdd/develop-refactor.out`), `verify-refactor` (reads `.tdd/verdict-refactor.exit` and `.tdd/verify-refactor.reason`: `### 🔎 verify-refactor — SKIPPED (switched off)`, with no reason appended, when that file reads `skipped`; or takes `--skipped`, posting `SKIPPED (nothing landed)`), `verify-revised <phase>` (reads `.tdd/verdict-<phase>.exit` and `.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason`: `### 🔎 verify-<phase> (revised) — PASS` with the reason, or `### 🔎 verify-<phase> (revised) — SKIPPED (switched off)` with no reason appended when the verdict file reads `skipped`), `revise <phase> <k>` (`REVISE.md` sub-step 2's mirror of attempt `<k>`: `### ♻️ Revise <phase> — attempt <k>/<REVISE_BUDGET>`, the budget read through `tunables`, then `.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason` verbatim), `plan` (reads the ledger and the test command `detect-test-command.sh --tier fast` detects from the issue body), `verify-plan` (reads `.tdd/verify-plan.reason`), `pin` (`PIN.md`'s PASS mirror: `### 📌 Already satisfied — <STEP>`, the ledger's last done step, then `.tdd/verify-pin.reason`, or `verify-pin — SKIPPED (switched off)` when `.tdd/verdict-pin.exit` reads `skipped`), `retire` (`PIN.md`'s mirror of a retired step: `### 🪦 Retired — <STEP>`, the ledger's last done step carrying a `⟂ retired` mark, then `.tdd/verify-pin.reason`), `resuming` (takes `--note-file`), `bailed` (takes `--note-file` and `--pr`, the latter posting on the run's PR instead of the issue; reads the last `bail` event's phase and classification), `stopped` (takes `--note-file` or none; reads the last `stop` event, the metrics block, the dropped work, the branch and worktree path, and says the conductor is being restarted for context growth, with no operator wording and no resume command, when `.tdd/restart-pending` names a live pid) and `done` (takes no other argument: `### ✅ Done — <pr-url>`, the pr-url being the last `done` event's, then `.tdd/issue-<issue>-metrics.md` verbatim when it starts with `### 📊 Metrics`, else the report `metrics.sh` renders, else `metrics could not be rendered: metrics.sh exited <code>`; then `⚠️ Unverified phases: <list>` when `.tdd/issue-<issue>-skip-verify` is non-empty, and a stop-arrived line when `.tdd/stop` exists; it writes no second `done` event). **Exit 2** when GitHub gives no answer, with no event, or an unknown kind or bad arguments (usage on stderr), or, for `red`, `green` and `refactor`, when the leaf's report file (`.tdd/develop-<kind>.out`) is missing or lacks a line the comment needs (nothing posted), or, for `plan`, when no fast-tier command is found (one stderr line, `no fast-tier command found`; nothing posted), or, for `done`, when the journal holds no `done` event (nothing posted), or, for `retire`, when the ledger holds no retired step (one stderr line; nothing posted), or, for `bailed`, when the journal holds no `bail` event or, with `--pr`, when no PR has the current branch as its head, or, for `stopped`, when the journal holds no `stop` event (nothing posted).
- `$S/role-state.sh dev [--lane <lane>] set <n|lane|auto> <value> | add <list> <text> | drop <list> <id> | recovered | show` — the dev session's own state file, `.tdd/role-dev.state` (`.tdd/role-dev-<lane>.state` for a named lane), in the main working tree. `set` writes a field: `n` (a whole number), `lane`, or `auto` (`on` or `off`, whether the dev session runs with `--auto`; any other value is exit 2); any other field, `resetter` included, is exit 2 as an unknown field; `add` appends an item to a list (`decision`, `message`, `override` or `timed-n`) and prints its id; `drop` removes an item by list and id; `recovered` records the time and `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`; there is no `request-reset` verb, and `request-reset` exits 2 as an unknown verb with the usage line. A state file still holding a `resetter` line, as older ones do, is read without error, the line ignored. `set n` also writes the lane's test budget entry, the one `dispatch.sh` writes (same name under `~/.veri-test-budget` (`VERI_TEST_BUDGET_DIR`), need max(N, live conductors) × `VERI_CONDUCTOR_TEST_WORKERS`, expiry now plus `VERI_TEST_BUDGET_TTL` seconds), and prints one line, `N=<n>; lane <lane> needs <need> (max(N, <live> live conductors) x <d>); other lanes: <lane>=<need>, … (or none); C=<c> from <source>; T=<t>`, the T a leg would read now; `show` prints the file. Every write is atomic (a temporary file renamed into place). Exit **0** written or shown, **2** bad arguments (an unknown verb or field, a role other than `dev` included, or `recovered` with no `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`), and `add timed-n` of any text not of the form `<n> until <ISO-8601 datetime>`, which it refuses with stderr `timed-n text must be: <n> until <ISO-8601 datetime>`, leaving the state file untouched, **3** no state file for `show`, or no such id for `drop` (the file unchanged). An operator's script; the conductor does not call it.
- `$S/decision-log.sh record <question> <decision> <rule> <by>` — the record of a decision about no issue: it appends one JSON line to `.tdd/decisions.jsonl` in the main working tree (found from files, from any worktree), with the keys `question`, `decision`, `rule`, `by` and `at`, the time it was recorded in ISO-8601 with its offset. `<by>` is `dev` when the dev session decided, or `owner` for the owner's own answer or override, which outranks the dev session's. A token-shaped credential, or the value of a variable `VERI_REDACT_VARS` names, in any field is written as `[redacted]`. A question, decision or rule over 2000 characters is refused, never cut. `status.sh --report` lists the decisions since the last ask. **Exit 0** recorded, **2** an argument missing, empty or extra, a `<by>` other than those two (its usage on stderr), or a field over 2000 characters (naming it); nothing is written on exit 2. An operator's script; the conductor does not call it.
- `$S/reset-cost.sh conductor <n>` — an operator's script; the conductor does not call it. It has no `dev` mode. It prints, per recorded conductor restart (run `<n>`'s `conductor-restart` events), one line: the restart's local time, its fixed cost K in input-token units (the price of the relaunch's first request in `.tdd/conductor-<n>.log` at the model's `veri/prices.py` rates, over the input rate) and the growth per request g over that relaunch. Then it prints the medians of K and g, r (the cache-read rate in the same units) and the optimum growth √(2·K·g / r) next to `VERI_RESET_GROWTH_CONDUCTOR`'s current value, and a line naming those sources; a figure that cannot be read prints `unknown`, never 0, and the optimum is `optimum=unknown` when any restart's K or g is unknown, the sources line saying how many. A transcript line that is not a JSON object is skipped, and not counted. **exit 0** printed; **exit 2** an unreadable transcript or log (stderr names it), or bad arguments — anything but `conductor`, `dev` included, or a bad `<n>` — with the usage line `usage: reset-cost.sh conductor <n>`; **exit 3** no restart recorded, printing nothing.
- `$S/diff-leaf-deviation.sh <leaf> <step-base-sha> <ledger>` — check a diff leaf's commits against the one commit a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step expects. It records one `diff-leaf-deviation` event (keys `leaf`, `base`, `head`, `commits`) to `.tdd/events.jsonl` when the leaf made more than one commit since `<step-base-sha>`, or made a commit touching `<ledger>`. **Exit 0** a deviation recorded; **exit 3** none (no commit past the base, or one commit that leaves the ledger alone), no event written; **exit 2** no answer (bad arguments, a git failure).
- `$S/script-help.sh <name>` — print the one catalogue entry for a script: the column-0 bullet of this file naming exactly `<name>` and its indented continuation lines, so a call is checked without reading the whole catalogue. **Exit 0** the entry printed; **exit 3**, printing nothing, a name with no entry; **exit 2** no argument, with its usage line.
- `$S/build-adjudicate-prompt.sh <n> <cause> <exit-code> <stderr-file> <command…>` — print the adjudicate leaf's prompt on stdout: the cause token, the command, the exit code, the last 50 lines of `<stderr-file>`, the cause's route tokens as the Eligible causes list of `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md` gives them, and the step's ledger line. **Exit 0** the prompt printed; **exit 2** a usage line on no argument, or a cause `ADJUDICATE.md` does not list as eligible.
- `$S/parse-adjudication.sh <leaf-output>` — read the last `ADJUDICATION:` line of the leaf's output and print `retry`, `route <token>` or `bail` on the first line and the reason on the second. **Exit 0** printed; **exit 2** there is no such line, the outcome is unknown, or the route token is not one `ADJUDICATE.md` lists for an eligible cause, and a usage line on no argument. It takes no cause: the check that a route is listed for the cause being judged is `adjudicate.sh record`'s.
- `$S/adjudicate.sh --check-budget | left <n> | record <n> <cause> <leaf-output>` — the adjudication budget and its record. `--check-budget`: **exit 0** when `${VERI_ADJUDICATE_BUDGET:-2}` is a whole number ≥ 0, else **exit 2** naming the variable. `left <n>`: **exit 0** when issue `<n>`'s `adjudicated` events since its last `conductor-start` event number fewer than the budget, **exit 3** when not. `record <n> <cause> <leaf-output>`: parses the leaf output with `parse-adjudication.sh`, a `route <token>` counting only when `ADJUDICATE.md` lists the token for `<cause>` (else it is unparsed), writes `adjudicated cause=<cause> outcome=<retry|bail|route|unparsed> reason=<r>`, prints the outcome; **exit 0** retry, **exit 1** bail or route, **exit 2** unparsed. **Exit 2** with a usage line on no argument.
- `$S/exhausted-outcome.sh [--step-ahead <red-test-id>] < <leaf-output>` — read the `develop-exhausted` leaf's answer from its raw output. The plain read (no argument): **exit 0** prints the split criteria, one per line (the `SPLIT:` lines); **exit 1** prints the needs-user answer (the last `NEEDS_USER:` line's text and every line after it); **exit 3** neither, printing nothing. `--step-ahead <red-test-id>`, run only on the plain read's exit 3, reads the `STEP_AHEAD: <file>::<case>` lines: **exit 0** prints one line `prep <file>::<case> …` naming each case its file defines, each id rebuilt from its checked parts, never the leaf's text, and named once in first-seen order: the path, normalized and relative to the working tree, and the case name with its class chain are confirmed in the file, while a `[param]` suffix is checked against the grammar only and not confirmed; `<red-test-id>`'s case is skipped however its path is spelt (the whole id, paths resolved against the working tree, when it has `::`, else its case name); **exit 3** prints nothing when no line remains; **exit 2** prints nothing on stdout and a stderr line naming the offending line when a value has no `::`, is not an id of the form `<path>::<name>[::<name>…][[param]]` (path `[\w./-]+`, each name `[A-Za-z_]\w*`, param `[\w.-]*`, ASCII), names a path that is absolute, starts with `..` or resolves (symlinks included) outside the working tree, names no existing file, or names a case its file does not define (for a class id `<file>::<Class>…::<case>`, a Python file whose class chain does not define it).
- `$S/seam-outcome.sh < <leaf-output>` — read a legacy step's `develop-seam` leaf's answer from its raw output. **Exit 0** `REACHES: yes` with a `SEAM:` line, printing the seam; **exit 1** `REACHES: no`, printing the leaf's reason (its `PROOF:` line); **exit 2** no readable `REACHES: yes|no` line, `REACHES: yes` with no `SEAM:` line or `SEAM: NONE`, or any argument.
- `$S/surround-mutants.sh <report-file>… -- <command…>` — run a legacy step's mutant table against its characterization tests. It reads every `MUTANT: <file> | <old> | <new>` line of the report files (the `develop-surround` and `verify-surround` reports; a row repeated across them counts once), runs each through `mutation-probe.sh <file> <old> <new> -- <command…>`, and prints one line per row, `<file> | <old> | <new>` ending `red`, `survived` or `no-answer`. **Exit 0** every row red and at least `VERI_SURROUND_MUTANTS` (default 5) rows; **exit 1** a row survived, or every row red but too few, the last line then `short <k> of <N>`; **exit 2** when no answer could be obtained: no report or command, an unreadable report, a `MUTANT:` line without three non-empty fields, a row the probe answered 2, or a `VERI_SURROUND_MUTANTS` that is not a whole number of at least 1 (one stderr line naming it).
- `$S/lint-repair.sh step < .tdd/lint.out` — compose the `⟦diff-checked⟧ ⟦lint-repair⟧` step a red lint gets (`DRAFT-PR.md`): **exit 0** prints `make `<command>` pass: <first finding> ⟦diff-checked⟧ ⟦lint-repair⟧`, the command read from the repo CLAUDE.md's `veri: lint:` line, the output's first non-empty line capped at 200 characters (`the command printed nothing` when it has none), and every local path, the host and the account name redacted as a PR body's are; **exit 3** the repo declares no `veri: lint:` line, printing nothing; **exit 2** bad arguments.
- `$S/tidy-first.sh < <green-report>` — read a green leaf's `TIDY_FIRST: <refactoring> | <file>::<symbol> | <why>` answer (`TIDY-FIRST.md`): **exit 0** prints the `⟦diff-checked⟧` step it asks for, naming the refactoring, the file and symbol, and that the step changes no behaviour and no test file, never the `<why>`; **exit 3** the report has no `TIDY_FIRST:` line, printing nothing; **exit 1**, the leaf's wrong answer, prints nothing on stdout and one stderr line naming the offending line, its local paths, host and account name redacted, on two or more such lines, a line not of that form, a refactoring `SMELLS.md` does not list, an absolute path, or a file that is not a regular file inside the working tree; **exit 2** no answer could be read (an argument, or `SMELLS.md` unreadable).
- `$S/run-pr.sh <n> [--from <stage>] [--hand <stage>]…` — the Draft PR step's five stages in series, in the worktree `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight` names: `branch-gate.sh`, `pr-prepare.sh`, `pr-checks.sh`, `pr-ready-wait.sh` and `pr-finish.sh`, each through this snapshot's own scripts directory. Its calls are exactly those five scripts: it **writes neither the title nor the body** (no `gh api -X PATCH` on a pull, no `pr-body.sh` call) and **launches no leaf**, and it writes no record another stage reads. It prints one line per call with its exit code, then that stage's stdout, never a suite's output file. It stops twice for the conductor, at two seams. The `title` seam: after `pr-checks.sh` exits 0 it ends on `seam=pr-ready-wait code=1 reason=title next=DRAFT-PR.md#4. Draft PR` and exits 1; the conductor sets the title and calls it again. The `body` seam: called again, it runs `pr-ready-wait.sh` then `pr-finish.sh`, and after `pr-finish.sh` exits 0 it prints `pr-finish.sh`'s last lines, then `seam=done code=1 reason=body next=DRAFT-PR.md#4. Draft PR`, and exits 1; the conductor writes the body from those lines and goes on to sub-step 3. Called again after the `body` seam it calls no stage and exits 0. The stacked stop: in a stacked run (`.tdd/issue-<n>-base` present) it never calls `pr-ready-wait.sh` or `pr-finish.sh`; after `pr-checks.sh` it ends on `seam=pr-ready-wait code=1 reason=stacked next=DRAFT-PR.md#4. Draft PR`, again on every later call; a later call with no option while the recorded sha is current calls no stage before that stop. At the first stage that exits non-zero it stops, printing `seam=<stage> code=<c> next=<anchor or run-pr.sh <n>>` after that stage's own `stopped at` line, and exits `c`; a 3 is `next=run-pr.sh <n>`, call it again; a code outside 0–3 is exit 2. Every `next=` it prints is `run-pr.sh <n>` or the anchor `DRAFT-PR.md#4. Draft PR`, the one heading of `DRAFT-PR.md`. The seam is recorded in `.tdd/issue-<n>-run-pr` with the sha of the ledger's last commit; called with no option it resumes there only while that sha is the committed one (an empty sha, git naming no commit, never matches), otherwise it starts at `branch-gate` (a `⟦ci-repair⟧` or repair step changes the ledger and so re-enters the step). `--from <stage>` starts at that stage; `--hand <stage>`, repeatable, stops before it; any stage name other than the five is exit 2. **Exit 0** after the `body` seam, no stage called; **1** a seam (title, body, stacked, hand) or a stage's 1; **2** bad arguments, an unknown stage name, a missing `.tdd/issue-<n>-preflight` or ledger, a stage's 2, or a stage's exit outside 0–3; **3** a stage's 3 (a wait's slice ran out), call it again: the 3 is recorded, so the next call with no option resumes at that stage.
